# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Breaking Changes

- **native-theme**: the `-live` presets are gone: `kde-breeze-live`, `adwaita-live`, `macos-sonoma-live` and `windows-11-live` (`Theme::preset` now returns `UnknownPreset` for them), with `PlatformPreset::is_live` and `PlatformPreset::live_name()`. The platform readers merge over the platform preset (`kde-breeze`, `adwaita`, `macos-monterey`, `windows-11`). Every key of a `-live` file stated its platform preset's value, so the layer changed no resolved value.
- **native-theme**: the `material` preset is now `material-3`: `Theme::preset("material")` returns `UnknownPreset`, with no alias.
- **native-theme**: the `macos-sonoma` preset is now `macos-monterey`, named by the first macOS version whose look it carries: Monterey (12), whose AppKit release notes end the accent-colour highlight of a clicked button; it covers macOS 12 to 15. `Theme::preset("macos-sonoma")` returns `UnknownPreset`, with no alias.
- **native-theme**: the `ios` preset is now `ios-13`, named by the first iOS version whose look it carries: the flat look with its dark variant that iOS 13 introduced and iOS 18 still draws. `Theme::preset("ios")` returns `UnknownPreset`, with no alias.
- **native-theme**: the data-model types have one public path, `native_theme::theme`: the `model` module is private, and the sub-module paths `theme::font::FontSize`, `theme::widgets::ButtonTheme` and the like are gone (`theme::FontSize`, `theme::ButtonTheme`). `theme` lists its types by name, so docs.rs shows them on the `theme` page instead of a `pub use crate::model::*` line. `detect_icon_theme()`, reachable only as `model::icons::detect_icon_theme`, is `detect::detect_icon_theme` (see the `detect` line below).
- **native-theme**: `FontSize` no longer implements `Default`: its `Px(0.0)` was a size validation always rejects, and nothing used it.
- **native-theme**: `PresetInfo` is `#[non_exhaustive]` and has no `light_only` field, which every bundled preset set to `false` (each one has both variants).
- **native-theme**: `ResolvedTheme`, `ResolvedDefaults`, `ResolvedTextScale`, `ResolvedTextScaleEntry`, `ResolvedIconSizes`, `ResolvedDefaultsBorder` and `Resolved` are `#[non_exhaustive]`, as the generated `Resolved*Theme` widget structs already were, so they can no longer be built by struct literal outside the crate; a field added to them later is not a breaking change. `ResolvedWidgetBorder`, `ResolvedPadding` and `ResolvedFontSpec` stay constructible.
- **native-theme**: `AccessibilityPreferences` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a flag added to it later is not a breaking change; outside the crate it is built from `AccessibilityPreferences::DEFAULT` or `default()` with its fields set, no longer by struct literal or `..Default::default()`.
- **native-theme**: `Error::Io` is `Io { path, source }`: `Theme::from_file` names the file it could not read, displayed as "cannot read theme file `{path}`: {source}" (it was "I/O error: {source}", without the path). The blanket `impl From<std::io::Error> for Error` is gone, so `?` no longer turns an `io::Error` into a `native_theme::Error`.
- **native-theme**: `IconRole::ALL` is a `&'static [IconRole]`, as the icon enums native-theme-build generates are (it was `[IconRole; 42]`), so a role added later does not change its type; iterate it with `IconRole::ALL.iter().copied()`.
- **native-theme**: `IconSetChoice` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a choice added later is not a breaking change; a `match` on it outside the crate needs a `_` arm.
- **native-theme**: `IconData::Rgba` holds an `RgbaData` whose fields are private (`IconData::Rgba(rgba)`, read with `rgba.width()`, `rgba.height()`, `rgba.data()` and `rgba.into_data()`), built with `IconData::rgba(width, height, data)`, which returns `None` unless `data` holds `width × height × 4` bytes; deserialising an `Rgba` with a buffer of another length fails. The serialised shape is unchanged in JSON and TOML. It was a struct variant with public fields, so `IconData::Rgba { width: 16, height: 16, data: vec![] }` was a value every consumer had to check again.
- **native-theme**: `AnimatedIcon::new_frames`, deprecated since 0.5.7, is gone; use `AnimatedIcon::frames`.
- **native-theme**: the platform modules are private: `freedesktop`, `macos`, `sficons`, `windows`, `winicons`, `kde::colors` and `kde::fonts` had no public item and showed as empty pages on docs.rs, and `gnome`'s one public item, `GnomePortalData`, was a struct no public function takes or returns. `kde::from_kde_content_pure`, public for the crate's own KDE reader tests, is hidden from the documentation.
- **native-theme**: `IconSetChoice`, `default_icon_choice` and `list_freedesktop_themes` are no longer re-exported at the crate root; their one path is `native_theme::icons`.
- **native-theme**: `system_icon_theme()`, `detect_icon_theme()` and `system_icon_set()` are in `detect`, beside `system_is_dark()` and `detect_is_dark()` and with the `DetectionContext::icon_theme()` cache they share: `native_theme::detect::system_icon_theme` (it was `theme::system_icon_theme`, and `system_icon_set` was `theme::system_icon_set`).
- **native-theme**: the icon data types are in `icons`, beside the loaders that take and return them: `IconRole`, `IconData`, `IconProvider`, `icon_name`, `AnimatedIcon`, `FrameList`, `FramesData`, `EmptyFrameListError`, `TransformAnimation` and `TransformData` (they were in `theme`: `native_theme::theme::IconRole` is `native_theme::icons::IconRole`), with `RgbaData`; one `use native_theme::icons::{IconRole, MaterialLoader};` imports a loader and its role. `IconSet` and `IconSizes`, which a theme states, stay in `theme`.
- **native-theme**: `IconSet::SegoeIcons` is `IconSet::SegoeFluent` and `SegoeIconsLoader` is `SegoeFluentLoader`, matching the set's serialised name `segoe-fluent`, which is unchanged (TOML `icon_set = "segoe-fluent"`, the native-theme-build mapping key and its `segoe-fluent/` directory); code native-theme-build generates names `IconSet::SegoeFluent`.
- **native-theme**: `FreedesktopLoader::size` takes a `u32`, the pixel type of `rasterize_svg` and `IconData::rgba` (it was `u16`), clamped as the other loaders' sizes are (the `MAX_ICON_SIZE` line below).
- **native-theme**: the system loaders' `color` and `color_opt` (`FreedesktopLoader`, `SfSymbolsLoader`, `SegoeFluentLoader`) take an `Rgba` and an `Option<Rgba>`, the crate's colour type that `colorize_monochrome_svg` takes (they took `[u8; 3]`); the alpha is ignored, as the icon keeps its own coverage.
- **native-theme**: `FreedesktopLoader::theme(name)` looks an icon up in `name` only where that is an installed icon theme, one whose `index.theme` lists `Directories`: `.theme("default")`, a theme that only inherits Adwaita, loads `None`, where it loaded Adwaita's icons through `default`'s `Inherits=` (see *Fixed*).
- **native-theme**: `icons::load_icon_indicator(set, theme)` takes the freedesktop theme to take the spinner from (`None` for the system's, as it used before), so a freedesktop spinner from a chosen theme needs no dispatch by hand; the other sets do not use it.
- **native-theme-build**: the generated enum implements `{crate_path}::icons::IconProvider` (it was `{crate_path}::theme::IconProvider`), the trait's path in this native-theme.
- **native-theme-build**: every field of `GenerateOutput` and `BuildErrors` is private, read through accessors: `GenerateOutput::{output_path, warnings, role_count, bundled_theme_count, svg_count, total_svg_bytes, rerun_paths, code}()` and `BuildErrors::{errors, rerun_paths}()` (`output.code` is `output.code()`; `rerun_paths` was a public field on `BuildErrors` and a method on `GenerateOutput`).
- **native-theme-build**: build errors name the file that holds the mistake: `BundledDeAware` has a `mapping_file`, `DuplicateTheme` a `file`, `ThemeOverlap` and `InvalidIdentifier` a `source_file: Option<String>` (`None` for a check on the merged configs of several files), and `MissingSvg` the `role` whose mapping names the SVG. `InvalidIconName` has a `reason`, the rule the name breaks ("names must not contain \"..\""), which its message ends with; it ended with "names must be non-empty and free of control characters" whichever rule failed, and its `offending` character is now the one that breaks that rule.
- **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-egui**: `from_system()` returns a `Loaded`, a `#[non_exhaustive]` struct in each connector with the same field names, so a field added later is not a breaking change: `theme` (egui: `atlas`), `resolved`, `mode: ColorMode`, `accessibility`, `icon_set` and `icon_theme`, the OS colour mode's icon theme (`SystemTheme::icon_theme_for`) that the iced `Spinner::new` and a freedesktop icon take. It returned the tuples `(theme, resolved, is_dark, accessibility)` (iced), `(theme, resolved, is_dark)` (gpui) and `(atlas, resolved, is_dark)` (egui); `loaded.mode.is_dark()` is the `is_dark` they carried. The iced and gpui `SystemThemeExt::to_iced_theme` and `to_gpui_theme` return the same `Loaded` (they returned `(theme, resolved)` and the gpui `Theme` alone); egui's `to_egui_atlas` keeps returning the `ThemeAtlas`, which carries all of it already. `from_preset` is unchanged.
- **native-theme-iced**: the accessors are named after the `ResolvedTheme` leaf they read, without the leading `defaults.` and a unit suffix, as in the gpui and egui connectors: `border_radius` → `border_corner_radius`, `border_radius_lg` → `border_corner_radius_lg`, `scrollbar_width` → `scrollbar_groove_width`, `selection_color` → `selection_background`, `info_foreground_color` → `info_text_color`, `warning_foreground_color` → `warning_text_color`, `line_height_multiplier` → `line_height`. The old names are gone, with no alias.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the accessors are named after the `ResolvedTheme` leaf they read, as in the iced and egui connectors: `frame_width` → `border_line_width`, `shadow_enabled` → `border_shadow_enabled`, `dialog_button_spacing` → `dialog_button_gap`, `scrollbar_width` → `scrollbar_groove_width`, `selection_foreground` → `selection_text_color`, `selection_inactive` → `selection_inactive_background`, `disabled_foreground` → `disabled_text_color`, `line_height_multiplier` → `line_height`. The old names are gone, with no alias.
- **native-theme-egui**: the accessors are named after the `ResolvedTheme` leaf they read, as in the iced and gpui connectors: `border_radius` → `border_corner_radius`, `border_radius_lg` → `border_corner_radius_lg`, `scrollbar_width` → `scrollbar_groove_width`, `line_height_multiplier` → `line_height`. The old names are gone, with no alias.
- **native-theme**: `TooltipTheme`, `DialogTheme` and `ListTheme` gain a field each, `show_delay`, `entrance` and `header_height` (see *Added*). The three are not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a struct literal of one outside the crate needs the new field or `..Default::default()`; their resolved forms are `#[non_exhaustive]` already.
- **native-theme**: `SwitchTheme` gains seven fields, the track's and the thumb's outlines (see *Added*): `unchecked_track_border_color`, `checked_track_border_color`, `disabled_unchecked_track_border_color`, `disabled_checked_track_border_color`, `track_border_line_width`, `thumb_border_color` and `thumb_border_line_width`. `SwitchTheme` is not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a struct literal of it outside the crate needs the new fields or `..Default::default()`; `ResolvedSwitchTheme` is `#[non_exhaustive]` already.
- **native-theme**: `TabTheme` gains `active_outward_corner_radius` (see *Added*); `TabTheme` is not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a struct literal of it outside the crate needs the field or `..Default::default()`; its resolved form is `#[non_exhaustive]` already.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::with_spin_animation` takes the period as a `NonZeroU32`, the type of `TransformAnimation::Spin`'s `duration_ms`, which it is passed as it is (no `.get()`); `AnimatedImageSources::frame_duration_ms` is a `NonZeroU32` as the icon data's frame duration is. A period of 0 made gpui's rotation NaN, a panic in a debug build (gpui-pre's `debug_assert!` that the animated value is finite), and no longer compiles.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `CheckboxLook`, `SwitchLook`, `SliderLook`, `ProgressBarLook`, `SeparatorLook`, `SpinnerLook`, `TabLook`, `TabIndicator`, `ScrollbarGeometry` and `AnimatedImageSources` are `#[non_exhaustive]`, as `Loaded` is, so a field added to them later is not a breaking change; outside the crate they can no longer be built by struct literal or destructured without `..`. A `ScrollbarGeometry` for `apply_overrides` or `scrollbar_styles` is made by `scrollbar_geometry(&resolved)` and edited field by field.
- **native-theme-iced**: `AnimatedSvgHandles` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, as the gpui connector's `AnimatedImageSources` is.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `is_dark(resolved)` is gone: it was a second name for `is_dark_resolved(resolved)`, which stays for a caller holding only a `ResolvedTheme`.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `ActiveNativeTheme::native()`: `cx.native()` gives the `Native` view the `geometry` builders take in one call, as `cx.theme()` gives gpui-component's theme (it was `cx.native_theme().and_then(|t| t.native(cx))`). It is a required method: a type outside the crate that implements `ActiveNativeTheme` must add it (the trait is implemented for `App`).
- **native-theme-gpui**: `SpinnerLook::path_radius()` is gone from the public API (crate-private, for the connector's tests): nothing draws with it, the arc takes its radii from `diameter` and `stroke`. It is also no less than 0 now; it went negative for a stroke wider than the diameter, where the drawn arc fills the disc.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a default build no longer reads the OS theme through a platform reader. The connector turned on native-theme's `linux`, `macos` or `windows` reader for the target it was built for; it now forwards them as the `native` feature, off by default as in native-theme and the iced connector, so without it `from_system()`, and the `SystemTheme::from_system()` whose result `apply_system_theme` takes, give the platform preset on Linux, in the light or dark mode the OS reports and with its reduced-motion preference (the two settings Linux reads without a reader), and an error on macOS and Windows. Add `features = ["native"]` (the README's Quick start does), or name native-theme's `kde`, `portal`, `macos` or `windows` for one platform.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `expander::ExpanderResponse` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, as the connector's `Loaded` is, so the fields egui's `CollapsingResponse` also carries (`body_response`, `openness`) can be added later without a breaking change; its fields are read as before, but outside the crate it can no longer be built by struct literal or destructured without `..`.
- **native-theme-egui**: `convert::to_color32_with_opacity` is gone: nothing in the connector, the widgets crate or the showcase called it; `to_color32(c).gamma_multiply(opacity)` is egui's own spelling.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the module `wrap` is `links` (`links::link`, `links::hyperlink`), named for what it holds: in this crate and in egui "wrap" means line-wrapping (`SegmentedControl::wrap`, `ComboBox::wrap`). The old path is gone, with no alias.
- **native-theme-egui**, **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a default build no longer reads the OS theme through a platform reader. The connector turned on native-theme's `linux`, `macos` or `windows` reader for the target it was built for; it now forwards them as the `native` feature, off by default as in native-theme and the iced and gpui connectors, so without it `from_system()`, the `SystemTheme::from_system()` whose result `to_egui_atlas` takes, and `ThemeWatcher::system_rebuild` give the platform preset on Linux, in the light or dark mode the OS reports and with its reduced-motion preference (the two settings Linux reads without a reader), and an error on macOS and Windows. Add `features = ["native"]` to either crate (the READMEs' Quick start does), or name native-theme's `kde`, `portal`, `macos` or `windows` for one platform.
- **native-theme**: icon sizes are clamped to `1..=icons::MAX_ICON_SIZE` (512): `FreedesktopLoader::size` and `rasterize::rasterize_svg`, as the size of every icon loader this release adds is (`SfSymbolsLoader::size`, `SegoeFluentLoader::size`, `icons::load_custom_icon` and `load_custom_icon_in`). A size above 512 used to be looked up or drawn at the size asked and now comes back at 512: a freedesktop icon from the theme's directory nearest 512, an SVG rasterised at 512 × 512. A size of 0 is taken as 1: `rasterize_svg` returns a 1 × 1 raster for it, where it returned an error. A loader that cannot draw the icon at the clamped size still returns `None`.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_handle` and `icons::custom_icon_to_svg_handle` take `(provider, icon_set, icon_theme: Option<&str>, color, size: Option<u32>)`, as egui's `custom_icon_to_image_source` takes the icon theme and size in its `IconKey` (they took `(provider, icon_set, color)`). They load through `native_theme::icons::load_custom_icon_in`, so a freedesktop icon comes from `icon_theme` (pass `loaded.icon_theme.as_deref()`, the icon theme the `Spinner` draws from), where it came from the system's even under a pinned preset whose variant names another (adwaita's `Adwaita` on a desktop whose icon theme is Breeze), and at `size`, clamped to `1..=MAX_ICON_SIZE`, where every system-set icon came at its loader's default; `None, None` keeps the old behaviour.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::spin_rotation_radians` takes the period as a `NonZeroU32`, the type of `TransformAnimation::Spin`'s `duration_ms`, which it is passed as it is (no `.get()`), as gpui's `with_spin_animation` takes it; `AnimatedSvgHandles::frame_duration_ms` is a `NonZeroU32` as the icon data's frame duration and gpui's `AnimatedImageSources::frame_duration_ms` are. A period of 0, which the function answered with an angle of 0, no longer compiles.
- **native-theme-iced**: `SystemThemeExt` is sealed, as the egui connector's is: a private supertrait implemented for `SystemTheme` alone, so a type outside the crate can no longer implement it, and a method added to it later is not a breaking change.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` takes `(provider, icon_set, icon_theme: Option<&str>, color, size)`, as iced's `custom_icon_to_image_handle` and egui's `custom_icon_to_image_source` take the icon theme (it took `(provider, icon_set, color, size)`). It loads through `native_theme::icons::load_custom_icon_in`, so a freedesktop icon comes from `icon_theme` (pass `loaded.icon_theme.as_deref()`, the icon theme the `Spinner` draws from), where it came from the system's even under a pinned preset whose variant names another (adwaita's `Adwaita` on a desktop whose icon theme is Breeze); `None` keeps the old behaviour.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `SystemThemeExt` is sealed, as the egui and iced connectors' are: a private supertrait implemented for `SystemTheme` alone, so a type outside the crate can no longer implement it, and a method added to it later is not a breaking change.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::PartBounds` is `Fn(Part, Bounds<Pixels>, &mut Window, &mut App)`: the observer gets the window being drawn, as gpui's own prepaint callbacks do (it got `(Part, Bounds<Pixels>, &mut App)`). GPUI Fast draws a view nothing changed for from the last frame without laying the control out again, so the observer is not called on every frame; with the window, it can keep element state that tells whether the frame still draws the part. Nor is it called while GPUI Fast composites a scroll area the control is in as it scrolls: the area's content is drawn moved by the scroll without being laid out again, so the bounds last reported there are off by the scroll since.

### Added

- **native-theme**: `AccessibilityPreferences::DEFAULT`, the preferences `default()` returns (text scaling 1.0, no flag set) as a `const`, usable in a `static`; the gpui connector's `Native::unscaled` uses it.
- **native-theme**: `Resolved::mode`, the `ColorMode` of the variant `Theme::resolve` actually resolved: a theme with only a light variant, resolved for `ColorMode::Dark`, reports `ColorMode::Light`.
- **native-theme**: the crate root re-exports `Theme`, `ColorMode`, `Resolved`, `ResolvedTheme`, `Rgba` and `Error` (`use native_theme::{ColorMode, Theme};`), and the prelude adds `ColorMode` and `Resolved`, the argument and the return type of `Theme::resolve`; the `theme`, `color` and `error` paths stay.
- **native-theme**: `Theme::resolve_with(mode, &ResolutionContext)` resolves with a context the caller built, with the icon-theme tiering `Theme::resolve` gives, and reads nothing from the system; `Theme::resolve` builds `ResolutionContext::from_system()` on every call, which takes the font DPI and the icon theme from the detection cache (detected at the first call and kept until `detect::invalidate_caches()`) and, on Linux, the dialog button order from `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`.
- **native-theme**: `AccessibilityPreferences::text_scale_factor()` (the text-scaling factor when it is finite and positive, else 1.0) and `AccessibilityPreferences::scaled(size)`, the size times that factor, or `size` itself where the product is not a normal positive number (a finite factor that overflows `f32`, or one that makes it subnormal or zero). The iced and gpui connectors' `scaled_text_size` call `scaled`, and the egui connector's multiplies by `text_scale_factor()`, so that a product that is not a positive normal `f32` still takes egui's own size with a `Note::ValueSanitised`; none has a copy of the rule of its own, and each connector's crate docs have a *What each flag does* table: per accessibility flag, what the connector applies and what the application has to pass on.
- **native-theme**: `detect::accessibility_preferences()`, the OS accessibility preferences from the detection cache: `SystemTheme::from_system()` stores its reader's accessibility block there, the first call runs the reader only if nothing is stored yet, and `detect::invalidate_caches()` clears it (`DetectionContext::accessibility_preferences` and `invalidate_accessibility_preferences`). `AccessibilityPreferences::from_system()` stays a fresh read and runs the whole platform reader (5.2 ms measured on one KDE machine, release build); on that machine the cached call took about 50 ns. The connectors' preset docs and the egui and gpui showcases' preset paths use it.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_docsrs.py` documents a crate the way docs.rs will (nightly rustdoc with the crate's `[package.metadata.docs.rs]` features and targets, `DOCS_RS=1`, `--cfg docsrs`, `-D warnings`), and `scripts/check_release.sh` and the crates.io workflow run it for every workspace crate, so a docs.rs failure stops a release before the tag instead of appearing after the crates are published.
- **native-theme**: the `macos-tahoe` preset, macOS 26's Liquid Glass look: AppKit's sizes and colours as an OS probe of macOS 26.6.2 reports and draws them (`docs/platform-facts.md`'s "macOS 26:" values): 24-high controls with their text 12 in on the push button and pop-up and 4 in on the text field, control corners of 6 (the text field's 5), buttons and pop-ups in `secondarySystemFillColor`'s value, a white window, the macOS 26 system colours, a 16 check box whose unchecked box is one fill, `#00000019` / `#ffffff19`, with no outline of its own (`checkbox.unchecked_background`, `unchecked_border_color` transparent), as the probe's render draws it in a window that is not key, a 6-high slider track, an 8-high progress bar whose fill covers its track (its padding 0, as the renders draw it) and a 54 × 24 switch; a card pads its content by 5 on every side, `NSBox`'s default `contentViewMargins`, which for macOS 26 is inferred from Apple's current, unversioned page (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.5, §2.10, §2.26). Where the model needs a number and no source gives macOS 26's, the preset carries `macos-monterey`'s and says so in the file. Its display name is "macOS Tahoe".
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::from_system()` picks the platform preset by the running OS version's generation: on macOS `macos-monterey` on 12 to 15 and `macos-tahoe` on 26 and later, on Windows `windows-11`. `SystemTheme::version_match` (`Option<VersionMatch>`: `Exact`, `PresetOlder`, `PresetNewer`; `None` on Linux) reports how the running version relates to the preset's generation — macOS 27, which has no preset of its own yet, is `PresetOlder` under `macos-tahoe`, and Windows 10 is `PresetNewer` under `windows-11` — and `diagnose_platform_support()` lists it as `DiagnosticEntry::OsGeneration`. `pipeline::platform_preset_name()` names the same preset.
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::from_system_with_preset(name)` and `from_system_with_preset_async(name)` merge the platform reader's live values over a chosen generation of the running platform (on macOS 26, `macos-monterey`'s geometry under macOS 26's colours, fonts and accessibility), reported in `version_match`.
- **native-theme**: crate documentation on docs.rs: a quick start (read the OS theme, resolve a preset, apply an overlay, hand the result to a connector, watch for changes) and a map of the modules; native-theme-derive's says it is internal to native-theme. The README has a `watch` recipe.
- **native-theme**: `SfSymbolsLoader::size(points)` and `SegoeFluentLoader::size(px)`, so a system icon can be asked for at a theme's icon size: an SF Symbol is configured at that point size, a Segoe glyph drawn from the font at that em height. Without it they stay at 24 points and 32 pixels; the size is clamped to `1..=icons::MAX_ICON_SIZE`, as every icon size is (see *Breaking Changes*). A Segoe stock shell icon keeps the system's large icon size.
- **native-theme**: `icons::load_custom_icon(provider, set, color, size)` loads an `IconProvider`'s icon for a set known only at runtime, and every set honours the colour: an SF Symbol or a Segoe glyph loads in it, a Material or Lucide icon, or the provider's own SVG, is coloured through `colorize_monochrome_svg`, and a freedesktop icon takes it where its foreground is left to the caller: a symbolic icon's placeholders, and a `currentColor` the document sets no `color` for (`fill_current_color`); an icon whose own stylesheet sets its colour (Breeze's `current-color-scheme`) and a full-colour icon keep theirs. `SystemTheme::icon` colours the same way. `size` reaches each system set's loader as it takes one: the pixel size a freedesktop icon is looked up at, an SF Symbol's point size, a Segoe glyph's pixel size; `None` keeps their defaults (24 px, 24 pt, 32 px).
- **native-theme**: `icons::load_custom_icon_in(provider, set, icon_theme, color, size)`, `load_custom_icon` with a freedesktop icon looked up in the icon theme given (the system's where it is `None`), so a provider's icon comes from the icon theme the application's theme names, beside its role icons; the other sets have no themes and ignore it. The gpui and iced connectors' `custom_icon_to_*` functions load through it, in place of a private copy each, and so does the egui connector's `custom_icon_to_image_source`, with its key's `icon_theme`.
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::icon(id, mode, color)` and `SystemTheme::icon_indicator(mode)` load a role, a name or a provider's icon, and the animated loading indicator, from the theme's `icon_set`; a freedesktop icon or spinner comes from `icon_theme_for(mode)`, that variant's theme (`breeze` or `breeze-dark` under `kde-breeze`).
- **native-theme**: `icons::fill_current_color(svg, color)` fills a symbolic icon's `currentColor` in with `color`'s `#rrggbb` where the document sets no `color` of its own, the step the gpui and egui connectors take for a freedesktop icon, so a theme's `process-working-symbolic` spinner can be drawn in `defaults.text_color` (a rasteriser draws an unset `currentColor` black); Breeze's, whose stylesheet sets its colour, and any document without `currentColor` come back borrowed and unchanged. The iced `Spinner` uses it.
- **native-theme**: `FreedesktopLoader::any(names)` loads the first of several freedesktop names the theme has, for a concept icon themes name differently (Breeze's `view-visible`, Adwaita's `view-reveal`): each theme of the `Inherits=` chain is searched for every name, in order, before the theme it inherits from, and within a theme each name as `<name>-symbolic`, then as itself. The theme's own icon under a later name so wins over its parent's under an earlier one, where `new(name)` tried name by name takes the parent's: Adwaita's `starred-symbolic` over AdwaitaLegacy's raster `emblem-favorite`. No names load `None`.
- **native-theme**: `fonts::substitute_family_cached(family)` (feature `system-fonts`), `substitute_family`'s answer asked once per family and kept for the process, so a caller may ask every frame: the first call for a family starts the platform's process (`fc-match` on Linux) on the calling thread, and a thread asking for another family, or for one already answered, does not wait for it. `substitute_family` still starts a process on each call. The gpui connector's `font_family` and the egui connector's `FontPlan::from_system` ask through it.
- **native-theme-build**: the generated enum, its variants and its `ALL` constant carry doc comments, so a crate that `include!`s the file into a public module compiles under `#![deny(missing_docs)]`.
- **native-theme**: `DetectionContext::invalidate_font_dpi()` clears the cached font DPI alone, as the other `invalidate_*` methods clear their field; `invalidate_all()` and `detect::invalidate_caches()` clear it too.
- **native-theme**: `DetectionContext::refined_linux_desktop_sync()` (Linux), the desktop the platform readers use: the raw `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` where it names a desktop, else the desktop the portal backend named at the last system read (with `portal`), else KDE where the user's `kdeglobals` exists (with `kde`); the reader, the theme watcher, the icon-theme detection and the font DPI decide by it, and code native-theme-build generates reads it.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a `gpui-fast` feature, off by default, that builds the connector on GPUI Kit's GPUI Fast core instead of gpui-pre (it turns on `gpui-kit/gpui-fast` and `gpui-component/gpui-fast`). The crate names GPUI through gpui-kit (`extern crate gpui_kit as gpui;`, the alias gpui-kit documents), so an application that turns on `gpui-kit/gpui-fast` with gpui-kit's `component` feature gets the connector on GPUI Fast without it; the README's *Backends* section says when an application needs it. docs.rs documents the crate on gpui-pre, with every feature but `gpui-fast`.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Typography page shows `Diff`, over a patch of one Rust file, with a Widget Info panel that claims each colour it paints; the Data page's table has a sortable column, so it draws gpui-component's sort button, and its panel claims the sort button's colours and says why the right-clicked cell draws no outline (the table is not `cell_selectable`).
- **Development**: `scripts/check_connector_parity.py` lists the iced, gpui and egui connectors' crate-root functions, and those of the public modules they share (`icons`), with their parameters and the `ResolvedTheme` leaves each reads, and fails on an accessor named unlike its leaf, or on a function of any class present in one connector and absent from another or present there under another signature, unless `scripts/connector_parity_exceptions.toml` lists it with its reason; `scripts/check_release.sh` runs it and its self-test, and CI, the crates.io workflow and the nightly dependency canary run it.
- **native-theme-iced**: the `native` and `watch` features, off by default as in native-theme. `native` turns on `native-theme/native`, the OS readers, so `from_system()` reads the OS theme without the application naming native-theme; without a reader it is the platform preset on Linux, in the light or dark mode the OS reports and with its reduced-motion preference (the two settings Linux reads without a reader), and an error on macOS and Windows. The README's Quick start enables it.
- **native-theme-iced**: `theme_changes()` (feature `watch`), the OS theme's changes as an iced `Subscription<native_theme::Result<ThemeChangeEvent>>`, through an optional `iced_futures` 0.14: the watcher runs while the application's `subscription()` returns it, and one that cannot start yields its error once and ends. `ThemeChangeEvent` is re-exported at the crate root with it, so an application that matches on the event needs no native-theme dependency. The README and the crate docs have a *Runtime theme change* recipe: clear the detection caches, read again with the entry point the application used (`from_system()`, or `SystemTheme::from_system_with_preset` for a pinned preset), rebuild the `Spinner`. The showcase follows the OS theme through it when built with `--features watch`, in place of its 500 ms poll of a flag the watcher set.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::button_toggle(&resolved, checked)`, a toggle button: the plain `styles::button` while off, and while on `button.checked_background` and `.checked_text_color`, the pressed pair where the theme states none (`docs/inheritance-rules.toml`), as the gpui connector paints a selected button — on windows-11 the accent, `#0067c0` with white. The iced showcase's toggle button uses it.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::expander_frame`, the container round an expander's header and body where the theme states `expander.frame_enabled = true` (libadwaita, WinUI): `expander.border.*`, with no outline where the theme states none. The iced showcase's expanders use it.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::pane_grid`, a `PaneGrid`'s split lines: `splitter.hover_color` at `splitter.divider_width`, for a hovered split and for one being dragged, which the model states no colour of its own for, where iced's `pane_grid::default` draws them in the palette's primary and strong primary, 2 px wide (adwaita light: `#d5d5d5`, 1 px). The highlight under a dragged pane stays iced's. The iced showcase's pane grid uses it, so its dragged split is no longer iced's strong primary.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_readme_versions.py` checks that every `native-theme` and `native-theme-*` dependency a README states in a `toml` block (the root README's and every workspace crate's, each crate's own Quick start among them) names the workspace `version`'s `major.minor`, so a version bump that leaves a README naming the previous minor fails `scripts/check_release.sh` (both modes), CI and the crates.io workflow; the release bump moves every README at once. It also fails on a crate a README's Rust code names, the root of a `use` tree or of a path the block does not bind itself, that no `toml` dependency table or `cargo add` line above that code states: a README's doctests compile against its crate's own dependencies, so a block naming `gpui::` passed through the connector's `gpui` while an application that copied the Quick start had none. The same rule reads the doc examples under each crate's `src/` (core, build, the connectors, egui-widgets): one may name only a crate its README states, in any block, and the crate itself.
- **Development**: native-theme-iced's contract walks the model as well as the output: every colour leaf of `ResolvedTheme` (169) is spliced in all 34 bundled preset/mode combinations, and each must move the native side of a palette, style or accessor row in at least one, or be named, with its reason, by `UNREACHABLE` or a new table of the leaves no row reads (`connectors/native-theme-iced/src/contract/derived.rs`, `UNREAD_COLOURS`: no receiver in iced or `iced_aw`, with its `file:line`; carried by the leaves that inherit it; no such widget; ruled; or a soft option's fallback that no bundled preset leaves unstated, `checkbox.background_color` for `checkbox.unchecked_background`, which names its option and cites the connector's line that falls back, and which the walk proves by clearing the option in a base, where the leaf must then move a row). A new colour leaf nothing names fails it, as does an entry that names a leaf a row now reads. The colour accessors (`border_color`, `focus_ring_color`, `link_color`, `expander_arrow_color`, `selection_background`, `info_color`, `info_text_color`, `warning_text_color`) and `tab_indicator_line`'s colour have rows of their own, and so do the connector's own `Spinner`'s two colours (with `spinner`): `spinner.fill_color`, its arc and a Material or Lucide indicator's tint, and `defaults.text_color`, a freedesktop indicator's `currentColor`, which the walk requires each to reach. The walk splices every leaf in all 34 combinations, where it stopped at a leaf's first moving splice, a base's thread whose walk panics failing the test, and it fails while `contract-leaves.toml` differs from what it finds, naming the command that rewrites it: the test exists only with `--features iced_aw` (check_release's and CI's `iced_aw,watch,native` run), so a default `cargo test -p native-theme-iced` does not compare the file.
- **Development**: native-theme-gpui's contract walks the model the same way (with `widgets`): every colour leaf of `ResolvedTheme` (169) is spliced in all 34 combinations, and each must move one of the connector's outputs in at least one (a `ThemeColor` or `base_palette` field, the scrollbar and resize-handle styles, a `geometry` builder, a `widgets` Look), or be named, with its reason, by `NO_RECEIVER` or a new `UNREAD_COLOURS` table (no receiver in gpui-component or gpui-base, with its `file:line`; carried by the leaves that inherit it; or a soft option's fallback that no bundled preset leaves unstated, `checkbox.background_color` for `checkbox.unchecked_background`, proved as in iced). A new colour leaf nothing names fails it, as does an entry that names a leaf a sink now reads (`switch.checked_background`'s `NO_RECEIVER` entry now holds only without `widgets`, whose switch draws it).
- **Development**: native-theme-egui-widgets' widgets read the theme's colours through one `Look` each (the spinner, the switch, the slider, the links, the progress bar, the radio button, the segmented control and the painted expander; the drop-down reads none). The spinner's carries `defaults.text_color` to a freedesktop indicator as its icon key's tint, which the connector's `load_color` takes before the atlas's text colour, so the indicator is drawn as before. The one colour read made outside a `Look` is the expander's arrow over egui's own header, which the connector's `expander_icon` draws and `mapping.toml` rows (`expander.arrow_color`, `tested_by = "T18(f)"`). A test splices every colour leaf of `ResolvedTheme` in all 34 bundled combinations: a widget whose `Look` moves must list the leaf, and every leaf it lists must move it in at least one; another requires every `Look` the crate defines to be among those it splices. The atlas's cells stay the connector's `mapping.toml` rows.
- **native-theme**: `tooltip.show_delay_ms` (`TooltipTheme::show_delay`, an `Option<u16>`), the delay in milliseconds between the pointer coming to rest on an element and its tooltip showing, each value from its `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.7 row: `kde-breeze` 700 (Qt's `SH_ToolTip_WakeUpDelay`), `adwaita` 500 (GTK's `HOVER_TIMEOUT`), `windows-11` 800 (WinUI's `ToolTipService` waits twice `SPI_GETMOUSEHOVERTIME`, 400 on the OS probe's Windows images) and `material-3` 0 (Compose shows a tooltip on pointer enter). The macOS presets and `ios-13` leave it unset, since neither AppKit nor UIKit states a delay, as do the colour-scheme presets.
- **native-theme**: `dialog.entrance` (`DialogTheme::entrance`), a new `DialogEntranceStyle`: how a dialog moves onto the screen as it opens, `scale_fade` (fading in while scaling up) or `fade_rise` (fading in while rising into place). It names the motion only; the durations and curves stay the toolkit's. From `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.22: `windows-11` (`ContentDialog`), `adwaita` (the floating `AdwDialog`) and `material-3` (material-components-android 1.14.0's M3 dialog animation) `scale_fade`, `kde-breeze` (`Kirigami.Dialog`) `fade_rise`. The macOS presets leave it unset, since AppKit names no dialog motion, as do `ios-13` and the colour-scheme presets.
- **native-theme**: `list.header_height_px` (`ListTheme::header_height`), the height of a table's column-header row in logical pixels, from `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.15: `adwaita` 30 (the column title button's minimum height and padding), `macos-monterey` and `macos-tahoe` 28 (`NSTableHeaderView`, read by the OS probe on macOS 14, 15 and 26) and `material-3` 56 (material-web's data-table header token). `kde-breeze` leaves it unset (Breeze sizes the header to its content), as do `windows-11` (WinUI 3 has no column header), `ios-13` and the colour-scheme presets.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the three values reach gpui. `apply` installs `tooltip.show_delay_ms` as gpui-base's application-wide `TooltipDefaults` show delay, which every tooltip shown through gpui-component's tooltip overlay waits (`Button::tooltip`'s among them), keeps the grace period, and installs it again after an upstream rebuild, so a mode switch through `Theme::change` or `sync_system_appearance` brings that variant's delay; where the theme states none, the application's own delay comes back. `tooltip_show_delay(&resolved)` gives the delay as a `Duration` for GPUI's own `.tooltip()`, which does not read `TooltipDefaults`, through `StatefulInteractiveElement::tooltip_show_delay`. `geometry::dialog_entrance(n)` is for `Dialog::entrance` and `AlertDialog::entrance`, both styles drawn as `DialogEntrance::Fade` because gpui-component has neither a scaling nor a rising entrance, and `geometry::list_header_height(n)` for `DataTable::header_row_height`.
- **native-theme-egui**: the atlas writes `tooltip.show_delay_ms` into `Style::interaction.tooltip_delay`, which egui's own tooltips wait; `tooltip_show_delay(&resolved)` returns it as a `Duration` for a tooltip the application times itself, and `list_header_height(&resolved)` gives `TableBuilder::header` its height. `dialog.entrance` is unmappable: egui's `Window` and `Modal` only fade in.
- **native-theme-iced**: `tooltip_show_delay(&resolved)`, a `Duration` for `Tooltip::delay`, and `list_header_height(&resolved)`, a table header's height. `dialog.entrance` has no receiver: iced 0.14 has no dialog widget.
- **native-theme-iced**: the package carries `contract-leaves.toml`, what the contract's colour-leaf walk finds, as data: for each colour leaf of `ResolvedTheme`, the palette slots, accessors and `styles::*` fields it moves, under which widget statuses, and the bundled preset/mode combinations in which it does not move them, or the `UNREAD_COLOURS` or `UNREACHABLE` entry that names a leaf no row reads, with its reason; and for each row, the leaves that move it. The iced showcase's Widget Info names the rows a colour leaf moves from it. No public item is added.
- **Showcases**: all three apply the new values: their tooltips wait the theme's delay and their tables' headers take `list.header_height` (egui's Theme Map table and iced's Layout table among them), gpui's dialogs open with the theme's entrance, and Widget Info prints a `_ms` value with " ms".
- **native-theme-gpui**: `Native` and `NativeTheme` implement `Debug`.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the `native` and `watch` features, off by default as in native-theme. `native` turns on `native-theme/native`, the OS readers (see *Breaking Changes*); `watch` turns on `native-theme/watch` and an optional `futures` 0.3.34, already in Cargo.lock through gpui-pre.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `on_theme_change(window, cx, f)` (feature `watch`) runs `f(event, &mut Window, &mut App)` on gpui's foreground each time the OS theme changes: native-theme's watcher sends each event from its thread through a channel to a task spawned with `Window::spawn`, and the returned `ThemeSubscription` keeps the watcher running until it is dropped; a watcher that cannot start returns its error at once. Events stop once the window it was registered on closes (an application registers it on its main window), and the task then ends. `ThemeChangeEvent` and `ThemeSubscription` are re-exported at the crate root with it. The README and the crate docs have a *Runtime theme change* recipe: clear the detection caches, read again with the entry point the application used (`SystemTheme::from_system()`, or `SystemTheme::from_system_with_preset` for a pinned preset), then `apply_system_theme`, after which `Theme::sync_system_appearance` keeps switching between the new light and dark palettes. The showcase follows the OS theme through it when built with `--features watch`, re-reading in `f`, in place of its 500 ms poll of a flag the watcher set.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::freedesktop_name_candidates(icon)` (Linux), an `IconName`'s freedesktop names for `FreedesktopLoader::any` in the one icon theme the icon is loaded from: one where the GTK (Adwaita) and Breeze conventions agree, else Breeze's, then GTK's. Which name a theme has is its convention, not the desktop's: `freedesktop_name_for_gpui_icon(icon, de)` gives Breeze's name on every desktop but the five GTK ones, which a GTK-convention theme lacks for most of the 50 icons the conventions name apart (48 in Adwaita with the AdwaitaLegacy it inherits; the adwaita preset's `Adwaita` on KDE, a GTK-convention theme on Sway, Hyprland or COSMIC). Its docs now point at the candidates.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::animated_frames_to_image_sources_for(set, anim, color, size)` converts an icon set's indicator frames coloured as that set is: a Material or Lucide frame through `colorize_monochrome_svg`, a freedesktop frame's `currentColor` filled in the colour only where the document sets no `color` of its own (`native_theme::icons::fill_current_color`), so Breeze's `process-working`, whose stylesheet sets `.ColorScheme-Text { color:#232629 }`, keeps its colour and a symbolic sprite is drawn in the colour, not black; the other sets' frames as they are. `animated_frames_to_image_sources(anim, color, size)` keeps its signature, and its docs say the colour is for a monochrome bundled set's frames, `None` for a system set's. The widgets' `Spinner` draws its frames through it, in place of a copy of the freedesktop rule of its own.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::animated_frames_to_svg_handles_for(set, anim, color)`, gpui's `animated_frames_to_image_sources_for` without its `size` (an SVG handle takes none): an icon set's indicator frames coloured as that set is, a Material or Lucide frame through `colorize_monochrome_svg`, a freedesktop frame's `currentColor` filled in the colour only where the document sets no `color` of its own (`native_theme::icons::fill_current_color`), the other sets' frames as they are. `animated_frames_to_svg_handles(anim, color)` keeps its signature, and its docs say the colour is for a monochrome bundled set's frames and point at the new function for a set's indicator. The `Spinner` draws its frames through it, in place of a frame loop and a copy of the per-set rule of its own.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `layer_over(base, layer)`, the composite the connector lays a theme's state layers over the fill they cover with (source-over, straight alpha in sRGB): the filled buttons' hover and pressed fills and the widgets' hover states are it, so an application drawing a layered state of its own, a stated `hover_background` over a control's fill, matches them. The gpui showcase's drop-down hover uses it.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::Slider::axis(Axis)`: a vertical slider, its minimum at the bottom as gpui-base maps the pointer, its rail `slider.track_height` thick and its height the caller's (gpui-component's 120 px where not given); `widgets::Part::StartThumb`, the bounds a range slider's start thumb reports to `on_part_bounds`.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::ProgressBar::loading(bool)`, an indeterminate bar: the value is ignored and the fill sweeps across the track once a second on gpui-component's own `progress-loading` motion (the model states no motion), standing still in the middle of the track under reduced motion, as gpui-component's `Progress::loading` does; the track, the fill, the height, the radius and the frame are `ProgressBarTheme`'s, where gpui-component's draws its track as its fill at 0.2 at its own sizes. It reports itself as an indeterminate progress indicator, with no value.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::Checkbox`, `widgets::Radio` and `widgets::Switch` take what gpui-component's controls of the same names take: `accessibility_label`, the name a screen reader announces where it is not the drawn label (or no label is drawn); `tab_stop` and `tab_index`, the control's place in the Tab order; and `Styled`, the caller's style refined onto the row after the widget's own, so `w_full()` or a gap of the caller's reaches it. `widgets::Switch::focus_ring(false)` keeps a focused switch's track free of the focus ring. Without a native theme each is passed on to the gpui-component control drawn instead.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::Slider::accessibility_label`, the name a screen reader announces, as gpui-component 0.8.0's `Slider` takes it: a slider draws no text of its own to be named by. Without a native theme it is passed on to the gpui-component slider drawn instead.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: each slider and number field is named for a screen reader by what it sets (`accessibility_label`), since GPUI cannot tie a control to the text beside it.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::TabBar::track_scroll(&ScrollHandle)` tracks the tabs' sideways scroll with the caller's handle, as gpui-component's `TabBar::track_scroll` does, and, where an `on_click` handler is set, a click on a tab the bar clips scrolls it just into view: before the handler runs the bar asks for the tab at that index (`ScrollHandle::scroll_to_item`), and the scroll lands on the next frame, so the handler still sees the old offset, a reveal it asks for itself replaces the bar's, and a handler that reorders or removes tabs reveals whichever tab is then at that index. Without a tracked handle the bar keeps one of its own. Under `wrap()` the tabs do not scroll and a click reveals nothing; a change of the selection scrolls nothing, as upstream's. Without a native theme the handle is passed on to the gpui-component `TabBar` drawn instead.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Inputs page shows a range slider and a vertical one beside the slider, and the Feedback page a loading progress bar under the progress bars, each with its Widget Info (the variant, the range's thumbs and keys, the vertical axis, the loading bar's motion or, under reduced motion, its still fill).
- **native-theme-gpui**: a *Role icons* recipe in the README and the `icons` module documentation (there a compiled `no_run` example): `SystemTheme::icon(role, mode, Some(text_color))` loads the role's icon from the theme's own set, coloured as that set is coloured, and `icons::to_image_source(&data, None, size)` converts it for `gpui::img`. `icons::icon_name` gives gpui-component's bundled Lucide glyph whatever the theme's set.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::list_item_selected`, the text colour of a selected `ListItem`, `list.selection_text_color`, applied after `geometry::list_item` to a selected row, beside `geometry::button_checked` for a toggle button that is on.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::combo_box_fill(n, disabled)`, the fill of a `Select` or `Combobox` trigger in the state it is built in, applied after `geometry::select` or `geometry::combobox` as `geometry::input_fill` is after `geometry::input`.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::accordion`, an `Accordion`'s and its items' edge colour, and `geometry::accordion_hover`, for `AccordionItem::hover`: `expander.hover_background` where the theme states one, and nothing where it does not.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::title_bar_inactive`, a `TitleBar`'s fill and text while gpui's `Window::is_window_active` is false, applied after `geometry::title_bar`.
- **native-theme-egui**: `ThemeAtlas::text_size_cap()`, the largest text size epaint can draw in the pass in progress, which the install plugin derives before every pass from the GPU's `max_texture_side`, the pass's `pixels_per_point` and the widest glyph of the faces the atlas installs (a TrueType face's `head` box where its outlines do not vary; for a `gvar` variable face or a CFF one, its glyphs' own boxes at the weight it is drawn at), and lowers every larger size the atlas hands egui to (see *Fixed*). `None` until a pass has run with the atlas installed; an install into a `Context` that has run one lowers the sizes and sets the cap at once. A size an application hands egui itself, from `text_role_font`, `window_title_bar_font`, `list_header_font` or `scaled_text_size`, is its own to clamp to it.
- **native-theme-egui**: `ThemeWatcher` implements `Debug` (whether an atlas is pending), as native-theme's `ThemeSubscription` does, so an application struct that derives `Debug` can hold an `Option<ThemeWatcher>`, the README's own pattern.
- **native-theme-egui**: the `native` feature, off by default as in native-theme: it turns on `native-theme/native`, the OS readers (see *Breaking Changes*).
- **native-theme-egui**: `RoleVariant::SelectedDisabled`, the disabled look of a checked check box or radio button: the `Role::Checkbox` cell is the checked (`Selected`) box with its label in `checkbox.disabled_text_color`, and where the theme states `checkbox.disabled_background` that fill with the mark in the disabled text colour; where it states none (adwaita) the box keeps its checked fill and `indicator_color` mark, which `disabled_alpha` fades, as the iced and gpui connectors draw it (docs/platform-facts.md §2.1.6). Every other role's `SelectedDisabled` cell is its `Disabled` one. An application need not choose between `Selected` and `Disabled` from the theme's data for a disabled checked box.
- **native-theme-egui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image(ctx, provider, key)`, `custom_icon_to_image_source` wrapped in an `egui::Image` with the key's alt text, as `to_image` wraps `to_image_source`. An `IconRole` is an `IconProvider`, so a role's icon from the installed theme's set and icon theme is one call; the README's icon recipe reads them from the atlas (`ThemeAtlas::icon_set`, `icon_theme`), where it named `IconSet::Lucide` and loaded the icon with no icon theme.
- **native-theme-egui**: `SystemThemeExt::to_egui_builder()`, the `Builder` that `to_egui_atlas` builds, before its `build()`: an application on the OS theme with fonts or a `style_patch` of its own adds them to it (`sys.to_egui_builder().fonts(FontPlan::from_system(&sys.light).with_base(defs)).build()`, the README's *Fonts* section), and a `ThemeWatcher` rebuild closure does the same, where the docs spelled out the seven-call builder chain to copy by hand.
- **Showcases (egui)**: the Range page shows the widgets crate's animated `ProgressBar` (`ProgressBar::animate`) beside egui's.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `slider::Slider::backdrop(colour)` and `segmented_control::SegmentedControl::backdrop(colour)`, the colour under the control, which a disabled one is faded over as one widget (the window's `defaults.background_color` where not given), as `Switch::backdrop` and `RadioButton::backdrop` are.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the `native` and `watch` features, forwarded to the connector's and off by default as there, so an application that depends on this crate alone can read the OS theme and start a `ThemeWatcher`. Its README gains a Quick start `[dependencies]` block and the egui version and `rust-version` it is built for.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `progress_bar::ProgressBar::animate(bool)`, egui's busy animation for an indeterminate bar, as gpui's `ProgressBar::loading`: forwarded to `egui::ProgressBar::animate`, so under the stated corner radius the fill pulses and no arc spins (egui's own rule for a bar given a radius), and under reduced motion the bar holds still, as the crate's `Spinner` does.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `expander::Expander::open(Option<bool>)` and `Expander::enabled(bool)`, as `CollapsingHeader` has them: `open(Some(..))` opens or closes the body whatever the pointer does (an "expand all", a restored state), marking the header's response changed in the pass that moves it, and `enabled(false)` draws the expander in its scope's `Disabled` variant with a header that cannot be clicked, on the painted path, on egui's own and with no atlas.
- **native-theme**: `color::composite_over(layer, base)`, the straight-alpha source-over in sRGB on `[f32; 4]` colours (`Rgba::to_f32_array`'s) that the iced and gpui connectors lay a theme's state layers over the fill they cover with: it covers `layer.a + base.a × (1 − layer.a)`, and where one input decides the result (an opaque layer, a transparent layer, a transparent base) it returns that input exactly.
- **native-theme**: `ResolvedWidgetBorder::padding_outside()`, the widget's padding measured from its outer edge: each side the border's `padding` states plus its `line_width` (an unstated side stays `None`), saturating at `f32::MAX`, for a toolkit that lays content out from the widget's edge and paints the border over the padding; the iced connector's `padding_inside_border` and the egui connector's paddings are it.
- **native-theme**: `icons::MAX_ICON_SIZE` (512), the largest size the system loaders, `load_custom_icon`, `load_custom_icon_in` and `rasterize_svg` load or draw an icon at (see *Breaking Changes*), so the connectors share one bound: a 512-pixel raster is 1 MiB.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::composite_over(layer, base)`, the composite the styles flatten a stated hover or pressed layer onto a widget's fill with (`native_theme::color::composite_over` on iced `Color`s), public as the gpui connector's `layer_over` is, so an application drawing a layered state of its own matches the styles.
- **native-theme-iced**: `is_reduced_motion`, `is_high_contrast`, `is_reduced_transparency` and `text_scaling_factor`, each reading one of a `SystemTheme`'s accessibility preferences, the four readers the gpui and egui connectors export under these names.
- **native-theme-iced**: `pub use native_theme;`, the source crate re-exported as the egui connector does, so an application reaches the rest of native-theme's API (`native_theme_iced::native_theme::detect::invalidate_caches()`) without a second dependency of its own, whose version could drift from the connector's.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `pub use native_theme;`, the source crate re-exported as the egui and iced connectors do, so an application reaches the rest of native-theme's API (`native_theme_gpui::native_theme::detect::invalidate_caches()`) without a second dependency of its own, whose version could drift from the connector's.
- **native-theme-iced**: `to_theme_in(resolved, name, mode)`, `to_theme` in a `ColorMode` the caller knows: the iced theme's light/dark flag (`Extended::is_dark`, which iced uses for the window title bar) is the mode's, where `to_theme` leaves it to iced's test of the background's lightness, as `from_preset`, `from_system` and `to_iced_theme` set it. The iced showcase builds every theme it installs with it.
- **native-theme-iced**: `font(spec)` (feature `system-fonts`), the `iced_core::Font` a theme font is drawn in: the family `system_font_family` gives for it, its weight through `to_iced_weight` and its style, as the gpui connector's `geometry` builders carry each stated font's family, weight and style; iced's styles carry no font, so a widget's stated font (`button.font`, `menu.font`, …) reaches it through the widget's `font`. iced's `Family::Name` takes a `&'static str`, so `font` leaks each distinct family name once, kept for the process, and gives every later call for it the same one, the recipe the crate docs gave the application to write. The crate docs' and the README's *Font Configuration* use it.
- **native-theme-iced**: `text_scale(&resolved)`, the theme's text scale (its `caption`, `section_heading`, `dialog_title` and `display` roles) by reference, as the gpui connector's `text_scale` and iced's own `icon_sizes` hand over their groups.
- **native-theme-egui**: `role_modifier(ui, role, variant)`, `ThemeAtlas::role_modifier` from the atlas installed in the `Ui`'s `Context`, in the scheme it reports, for a call site that holds a `Ui` and not the atlas; `None` with no atlas installed, so the popup or menu is passed no style and keeps egui's own (a menu's `menu_style`, which an empty `StyleModifier` would replace). A free function, as no `NativeThemeUiExt` method returns an `Option`; the showcase's own copy of it is gone.
- **Development**: `scripts/generate_assets_release.sh` compares the three showcases at rest before a release: each Linux screenshot's run also writes its layout dump (`--dump-layout`), its capture and the capture's content offset into one temporary directory, and `scripts/check_showcase_parity.py` fails the asset run on a size, position or colour difference that `docs/showcase-exceptions.toml` lists no reason for, keeping the directory for its `--merge`. The three `scripts/generate_screenshots_*.sh` scripts take that directory as an optional argument.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_release.sh --commit`, the per-commit subset: every lint, check, test, documentation, widget-coverage, showcase-parity and archive-index gate, without the release-only checks (the release-tag check, the gpui connector's tests on GPUI Fast, native-theme's ignored stem-index test, the docs.rs emulation, packaging, the semver checks, the security audit, outdated dependencies and the visual-asset and compatibility stamp checks), each printed as skipped and listed in the summary. Without an argument the script runs every check. `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `scripts/README.md` say when each mode is required: the subset before every commit, the full run on the last commit of a fix phase and before the release commit.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_release.sh` gains three checks. Its full run compares each published library crate's public API with the crate's newest crates.io release through `cargo semver-checks` and reports every change the version in `Cargo.toml` does not allow, its one soft check, since a reported break asks for a version decision. Both modes run the showcase-parity comparator's two static modes, `scripts/check_showcase_parity.py --check-list`, which fails on an element list or a `[parity]` exception that the registry, the list or the presets do not back, and its `--self-test` (CI runs the two in a job of its own), and fail on a `docs/archive/*.md` that `docs/archive/README.md` does not name.
- **Development**: the strict panic lints, clippy's panic-prone lints denied on library code (explicit panics, `unwrap` and `expect`, indexing and slicing, arithmetic side effects, integer division and remainder, process exit), are one list in `scripts/check_strict_panic.sh`, which `scripts/check_release.sh` and CI's clippy job both run; no CI job ran them. Both lint native-theme for the macOS and Windows targets too, and the iced and gpui connectors with their optional features (the gpui connector on GPUI Fast as well), and the test-fixtures crate; `check_release.sh` also lints the OS probe.
- **Development**: the strict panic lints hold the gpui showcase example too, on gpui-pre and on GPUI Fast (`scripts/check_strict_panic.sh -p native-theme-gpui --example showcase-gpui --features native,watch`, and with `gpui-fast`), in `scripts/check_release.sh` and CI's clippy job: its index and count arithmetic saturates or is checked, its menus and animation frames wrap through `checked_rem`, and its `Pixels` arithmetic is done in `f32`, the operations gpui's own operators run, so nothing it draws moved.
- **Development**: the strict panic lints hold the iced showcase example too (`scripts/check_strict_panic.sh -p native-theme-iced --example showcase-iced --features iced_aw,watch,native`), in `scripts/check_release.sh` and CI's clippy job: its counters and indices saturate or are checked, its animation frames are read through `.get()` and stepped through `checked_rem`, its grids are slice chunks, so nothing it draws moved. In CI each connector's library and its showcase example are separate steps, so a library failure no longer hides the showcase's run (the gpui steps split the same way).
- **Development**: `tools/os-probe` (`native-theme-os-probe`, never published), which records what macOS or Windows reports (fonts, colours, control sizes, system metrics) in a `probe.toml` and on macOS renders each control, and `.github/workflows/os-probe.yml`, dispatched by hand, which runs it on each macOS and Windows runner image and uploads what it wrote; CI's clippy job lints it for the macOS and Windows targets. Its runs are the source of the values this release takes from "an OS probe": `docs/platform-facts.md` cites them, and `docs/evidence/os-probe/` keeps what they wrote, as GitHub deletes a run's artifacts after 90 days.
- **Development**: `tools/showcase-support` (`native-theme-showcase-support`, never published), the code the gpui, iced and egui showcases share, with its tests: one command-line parser with one validator and one error text per flag (`--icon-set` taking `default`), the exit rules, the capture's app id and frame check, `--screenshot`'s capture of the window with its frame on macOS and Windows through xcap's safe API (on Windows given up, exit 1, when its window finder finds no window within 20 s, the time the capture scripts wait for a window) and the Retina nudge on objc2-app-kit's safe calls, the layout dump, the one reader of `docs/showcase-elements.toml` (its header names it), the Widget Info values, rows and Copy text (`defaults.icon_theme` reads the icon theme the showcase resolved), the preset and icon-theme pickers' rows and the status bar's environment items. `scripts/check_release.sh` and CI test it, and lint it, the strict panic lints included, for Linux, macOS and Windows.
- **Development**: three checks of the data documents, each with a `--self-test`, which `scripts/check_release.sh` (both modes) and CI's parity job run: `scripts/check_platform_facts_rows.py` fails on a `docs/property-registry.toml` field that has no `docs/platform-facts.md` row, `scripts/check_wrong_safety_nets.py` on a `# see wrong_safety_nets` mark in `docs/inheritance-rules.toml` without its `[wrong_safety_nets]` entry, an entry its `[uniform]` rule does not mark or an entry no rule inherits, and `scripts/check_section_refs.py` on a `§` reference in the registry, the inheritance rules, `docs/showcase-elements.toml` or `docs/new-os-version-guide.md` that names no platform-facts section.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_todo_citations.py`, with a `--self-test`, which `scripts/check_release.sh` (both modes) and CI's parity job run: `docs/todo.md` cites a preset value by its TOML key path (`adwaita.toml` `[light.button] active_background`), and the script fails on a key path that names no key of its preset, or on a preset line citation (`adwaita.toml:122`) that no commit dates, since the presets' growth moved most of the line citations the backlog had to other keys.
- **native-theme**: the switch's outlines (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.21): `switch.unchecked_track_border_color` and `checked_track_border_color`, their `disabled_*` forms, `track_border_line_width_px`, `thumb_border_color` and `thumb_border_line_width_px`, each drawn inside its part. They are soft options, unset where the platform draws no outline: libadwaita and macOS outline neither part, WinUI and Material only the off track. kde-breeze states qqc2-desktop-style's (the off track and the handle in the Button set's `linearInterpolation` at the frame contrast, #cccfd2 / #4e5359, the on track in the selection colour, 1 wide), windows-11 WinUI's `ToggleSwitchStrokeOff` (#00000072 / #ffffff8b, 1 wide) and material-3 `unselected-track-outline-color` (`outline`, 2 wide), with their disabled forms. Hover outlines, which only KDE distinguishes, are not carried (docs/todo.md, *KDE: the switch's hover and disabled thumb outlines*).
- **native-theme-gpui**: `SwitchLook::track_border`, `track_border_width`, `thumb_border` and `thumb_border_width`, the outlines the theme states for the switch's state.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `SwitchLook::end_inset`, the thumb's inset from either end of the track: `inset`, or 0 where the thumb overhangs the track (`inset` stays the inset from the track's top).
- **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the switch draws the outlines the theme states for its track and thumb (above), where it drew none: kde-breeze's tracks and thumb, windows-11's off track in its `ToggleSwitchStrokeOff` stroke and material-3's in its 2px `outline`. The thumb lies over the track's outline, as qqc2-desktop-style's handle does (gpui draws the outline as a box of its own under the thumb, since it paints a box's border after its children). iced's `styles::toggler` emits the theme's border widths and colours for the track and the thumb (they were iced's own, none), the track's composited over the track's fill in each state, as WinUI strokes `ToggleSwitchStrokeOff` and Material its `track-outline` over the fill, since iced's quad paints a border in place of the fill under it (a translucent outline, windows-11's `#00000072` / `#ffffff8b` off track or material-3's disabled `#1d1b201f` / `#e6e0e91f`, would otherwise land on the window), and `native_theme_iced::switch` draws them on its track and thumb.
- **native-theme**: `tab.active_outward_corner_radius_px`, the selected tab's outward bottom corners (WinUI 3's folder-shaped `TabView`: 4 against its 8 top corners).
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::TabBar` draws the selected tab's outward corners where the theme states them (`TabLook::outward_radius`).
- **native-theme-iced**: `tab_flares`, the selected tab's outward corners over a row of tabs, an iced_aw `TabBar` or `Tabs`.

### Changed

- **native-theme**: validation errors name the TOML key a theme author writes: `button.min_height_px`, `button.border.padding_left_px`, `defaults.icon_sizes.toolbar_px` (they named the model fields, `button.min_height`, `button.border.padding_left`, `defaults.icon_sizes.toolbar`), and an out-of-range font size the key in the unit its value carries, `size_pt` or `size_px`, under the table the value lands in (a `button.font` that inherits a `defaults.font` in points is `button.font.size_pt`). Errors from `Theme::resolve`, `Theme::resolve_with`, `SystemTheme::from_system()` and `SystemTheme::with_overlay` start each path with the variant that failed, `light.` or `dark.`, except `layout.*` and `context.font_dpi`, which no variant holds.
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::from_system()` no longer detects the font DPI a second time after the platform reader supplied it.
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::from_system()` and `AccessibilityPreferences::from_system()` block on `pollster` on macOS and Windows as they do on Linux, so `pollster` is a dependency on every platform; the single poll that answered `PlatformUnsupported` ("unexpected async suspension") if a reader ever awaited is gone.
- **native-theme-egui**: `from_preset` resolves both variants on one `ResolutionContext`.
- **native-theme**: the Windows reader no longer states WinUI 3's design sizes and radii; they come from the windows-11 preset alone, so on Windows the splitter divider is 1 px, the preset's and docs/platform-facts.md §2.17's, instead of 4.
- **native-theme**: outside high contrast the Windows reader writes only what the user sets and the windows-11 preset cannot know: light or dark, the accent colours, the text scale, the non-client fonts and the accessibility flags; the rest comes from the preset, which states what WinUI 3 draws. Live Windows themes no longer take classic Win32 colours from `GetSysColor` (the button's, menu's, tooltip's, text field's and list selection's, the placeholder's `COLOR_GRAYTEXT`, the title bar's text and inactive fill), which follow neither dark mode nor what WinUI 3 draws, nor the DWM colorization as the title bar's fill, `UISettings`' black or white foreground and background as the text and window colours, the accent as the focus ring and the selection, the foreground as the accent button's text (black on the dark accent fill in light mode), the window background as the surface, the midpoint of foreground and background as the disabled text, or `SM_CXFOCUSBORDER` (1) as the focus ring's width (WinUI 3 draws 2); a classic colour set in the registry no longer shows outside high contrast. From the accent shades it reads, it writes the fills of the accent and checked buttons as WinUI 3's `AccentFillColorDefault` (AccentDark1, dark AccentLight2; the dark fill was AccentLight1), the link colour as `AccentTextFillColorPrimary` (AccentDark2, dark AccentLight3), the link's hover and pressed text as `HyperlinkForegroundPointerOver` and `HyperlinkForegroundPressed` (AccentDark3 and AccentDark1, dark AccentLight3 and AccentLight2; outside high contrast) and the informational colour as `SystemFillColorAttention` (the accent, dark AccentLight2). Under high contrast the reader maps WinUI 3's HighContrast theme dictionaries (microsoft-ui-xaml 8463f45) to the system colours: the text, focus ring, informational colour, tooltip text and placeholder `COLOR_WINDOWTEXT`; the window, surface, menu and tooltip `COLOR_WINDOW`; buttons and text fields `COLOR_BTNFACE` with `COLOR_BTNTEXT` (the menu's text too); the accent `COLOR_HIGHLIGHT` with its text `COLOR_HIGHLIGHTTEXT`, so the fills and text that inherit them follow; the selection, the list's selected row and the accent and checked buttons `COLOR_HIGHLIGHT` with `COLOR_HIGHLIGHTTEXT`; links `COLOR_HOTLIGHT`; disabled text `COLOR_GRAYTEXT`; and the progress bar gets its 1 px border.
- **native-theme**: the `kde-breeze` and `adwaita` presets name the style version their values were checked against: Breeze v6.7.5 (Plasma 6.7, KDE Frameworks 6.31.0) and libadwaita 1.10.0 (GNOME 51).
- **native-theme**: the macOS reader no longer states AppKit's design sizes; they come from the macOS preset alone, as the Windows reader's do; the scroll bar's groove width, which it stated as the legacy style's 15 under either style, is the width AppKit gives the user's scroller style (under Fixed). It no longer reads `NSColor.shadowColor`, an opaque black, as the drop-shadow colour, so the preset's `NSShadow` default applies; it reads a selection's text colour from `alternateSelectedControlTextColor` (white), as `docs/platform-facts.md` names it, where it read `selectedTextColor` (black in light mode), which now gives the selected text's colour beside `selectedTextBackgroundColor`; and the text field's selection takes those text-selection colours instead of `unemphasizedSelectedContentBackgroundColor`.
- **native-theme**: the first `Theme::preset(name)` in a process parses that preset's TOML alone, not all 17 bundled ones, and each preset is parsed on its own first use: a cold `Theme::preset("kde-breeze")` took a median 0.84 ms instead of 6.3 ms (21 fresh processes each, release build, on one Linux machine). `SystemTheme::from_system()` makes that call for its platform preset.
- **native-theme**: a Segoe glyph is drawn from the font created at its size as the em height (CreateFontW with a negative height); the 32 pixels it was drawn at were the font's cell height, which includes the internal leading, so a glyph at the default size may draw larger than before, by that leading.
- **native-theme**: the font DPI that `ResolutionContext::from_system()` detects, which `Theme::resolve` and `ThemeMode::resolve_system` use, and the KDE reader's font DPI are one chain (with the `kde` feature, `kcmfonts`' `forceFontDPI` on an X11 session, then `Xft.dpi`, then xrandr), detected once and cached until `detect::invalidate_caches()`, which a consumer of `watch::on_theme_change` calls on each event before it reads `SystemTheme::from_system()` again: `Theme::resolve` no longer runs `xrdb -query` on every call, so resolving `kde-breeze` took a median 36 µs instead of 3.5 ms, and a warm `SystemTheme::from_system()` 1.25 ms instead of 5.3 ms (three release runs each, on one KDE Wayland machine).
- **native-theme**: a freedesktop icon name a theme has only as an `.xpm` file, which the lookup never returns, is `None` at once: it passed the index of the theme's file names and ran freedesktop-icons' whole fallback search on every load (2.3–2.7 ms per load, a release build; about 310 ns now).
- **native-theme-gpui**: requires gpui-component, gpui-base and gpui-kit 0.7.3 and gpui-pre 0.3.9 (MSRV unchanged, 1.95.0). gpui-kit is a normal dependency, without its default features, since the crate names GPUI through it, so the library's build includes gpui-kit's platform layer (`gpui-pre-platform` and its platform crates), which an application links anyway.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::to_svg_handle`, `into_svg_handle` and `animated_frames_to_svg_handles` colour an SVG through `native_theme::icons::colorize_monochrome_svg`, core's colouriser, which the gpui and egui connectors use, so an icon that mixes `currentColor` with an explicit black fill or stroke has the black coloured too; iced's own copy of the colouriser, which stopped after `currentColor`, is gone.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::to_image_source`, `into_image_source`, `bundled_icon_to_image_source`, `bundled_svg_to_image_source` and `animated_frames_to_image_sources` colour an SVG through `native_theme::icons::colorize_monochrome_svg`, which was written with gpui's algorithm, so the colours they draw are unchanged; gpui's own copy of the colouriser is gone.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::into_image_source` and `custom_icon_to_image_source` hand an RGBA icon's own pixel buffer to gpui, and an SVG rasterised with `svg-rasterize` is handed over in the buffer the rasteriser returned; each was copied once more first. A 512 × 512 RGBA icon converts in a median 221 µs instead of 277–325 µs (`tests/timings.rs`, three runs each, release build, on one Linux machine at a load average of about 5.0–5.3 before and 3.65 after).
- **native-theme-gpui**: the crate docs' Quick Start installs the theme, as the README's does: `gpui_kit::init`, then `apply_system_theme` for the OS theme, or `from_preset` and `apply` for a preset; it stopped at a theme built and installed nowhere. `from_preset` no longer calls itself the primary entry point. The Quick Start and Manual Path examples, and those of `from_preset`, `from_system` and `SystemThemeExt`, compile as doctests (`no_run`), where they were `ignore`d.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the `icons::icon_name` example runs as a doctest, comparing through `matches!` since gpui-component's `IconName` has no `PartialEq`, and the `to_image_source` and `bundled_icon_to_image_source` examples compile (`no_run`); the first two could not compile as written (an `assert_eq!` on `IconName`, an unbound colour) and all three were `ignore`d.
