- **native-theme-gpui**: the public documentation cites no review tickets (`from_system`'s "Issue 19/31", `apply`'s and `apply_accessibility`'s `D34`, `D35`, `D37`, `D38`, `D43`), and each `§` citation on docs.rs names its document (`docs/archive/todo_v0.5.8_gpui-component-0.6-spec.md`, its rationale, `docs/archive/todo_gpui-widgets-spec.md`, `docs/archive/todo_v0.5.9_gpui-kit-0.6.4-spec.md`, `docs/platform-facts.md`), where it said "spec" or "rationale" alone.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the README says what paints a window's background: gpui-component's root plugin, which `gpui_kit::init` registers, fills every `gpui_kit::open_window` root with the theme's `background` (`window.background_color`), while gpui-base's `Root` paints none, so a view opened with gpui's own `cx.open_window` paints `cx.theme().background` itself, and a surface the theme states translucent (windows-11's sidebar) shows that background through it.
- **native-theme-egui**: the preset Quick start, in the README and the crate docs, sets egui's colour scheme to the variant `is_dark` named (`ctx.set_theme(..)`): a preset pins the scheme, and without the call egui showed the OS scheme on macOS and Windows and its `fallback_theme` on Linux, whatever variant was asked for. The README's *Runtime theme change* says how an application that pinned a preset with `SystemTheme::from_system_with_preset` rebuilds (a closure that reads with it again, where `ThemeWatcher::system_rebuild` reads with `from_system()`), and `from_preset`'s documentation that a one-variant preset's returned `ResolvedTheme` is the variant it has.
- **native-theme-egui**: `ThemeAtlas::install` of the atlas already installed (the one `ThemeAtlas::from_ctx` returns, or a clone of it) restores its two base styles and does nothing else: it set the fonts again, which egui compares face by face, byte by byte, forgot every icon this crate cached and requested a repaint on every call, so an application that installs in every pass never let egui idle and uploaded each raster icon again every pass. `ThemeAtlas::clear` then `install` runs the whole sequence, for an application that changed the fonts itself and wants the atlas's back.
- **native-theme-egui**: `ThemeAtlas::install`'s and `icons::to_image_source`'s docs, and the README's icon section, say that a raster icon's `ImageSource::Texture` names a texture freed at the next install of another atlas and by `ThemeAtlas::clear`, so an icon is converted in each pass, which reuses the cached texture, rather than kept.
- **native-theme-egui**: the README, the crate docs and `ThemeAtlas::install`'s doc say that eframe paints `App::clear_color` behind every panel, by default a translucent near-black (`rgba(12, 12, 12, 180)`), which a panel fill the theme states transparent lets through (`windows-11`'s sidebar), and that an application returns `visuals.panel_fill.to_normalized_gamma_f32()`, the theme's `defaults.background_color`, from `clear_color`, as the showcase does.
- **native-theme-egui**, **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the crate docs name the design specification their section numbers cite, `docs/archive/todo_v0.6.0_egui-connector-spec.md` and `docs/archive/todo_egui-widgets-spec.md` in the repository (the mapping document's preamble names the connector's too), and every `§` citation in the documentation reads `spec §N` or names its document, where about 170 cited a bare `§N` of a document a docs.rs reader could not find; a test in each crate keeps it so.
- **native-theme-egui**: `FontPlan::from_system`, and so `from_preset`, `SystemThemeExt::to_egui_atlas` and a `ThemeWatcher` rebuild, asks the platform for a missing font family's substitute once per family per process, through `native_theme::fonts::substitute_family_cached`, as the gpui connector does; it started one `fc-match` process per missing family on every call. A warm `from_preset` of a preset whose two families the system lacks (windows-11, the macOS and iOS presets, the ten Inter and JetBrains Mono presets) took 31.4–32.1 ms on one Linux machine and takes 0.42–0.55 ms (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 3–6; 31.4–32.9 ms and 0.41–0.55 ms in three runs each at 86–132); the first call that brings a new family still pays its ask. The showcase's semibold faces take the same substitute.
- **native-theme-egui**: `NativeThemeUiExt::native_scope` records its child `Ui` for the focus ring without first searching the pass's records for it, which a `Ui` created in that pass is never in; only `native_set_style`, which can be called twice on one `Ui`, still searches. A pass of 1000 sibling role scopes with a focused widget took 755–769 µs on one Linux machine and takes 567–581 µs, one of 100 scopes 64.2–64.3 µs and 59.4–60.9 µs (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 2.2 before and 4.6–4.9 after).
- **native-theme-egui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` keeps the icon a provider's name loads, per name, set, icon theme, size and colour, in the `Context` until `icons::forget_icons` (the next install of another atlas, or `ThemeAtlas::clear`), and its doc names what a call costs; it looked the icon up and read its file from disk, for a freedesktop icon, on every call, which an application makes in every pass. A provider's own SVG and a name the set lacks are still loaded on every call. 42 freedesktop icons from breeze-dark took 1.86–1.87 ms per pass on one Linux machine and take 22–28 µs (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 2.2 before and 6.7–6.9 after).
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `links::hyperlink` tests its URL against the visited set without copying it: the set is kept shared, and a click copies it only while a read still holds it, where every hyperlink in every pass cloned every visited URL. A pass of 100 hyperlinks with 100 visited URLs took 591–611 µs on one Linux machine and takes 188 µs (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 2.4).
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: `Expander::show` reads the expander's theme from the installed atlas in place, where it copied the whole `ResolvedTheme` (every widget's, with their font family names) for every expander in every pass. A pass of 100 closed expanders took 306–307 µs on one Linux machine and takes 248–254 µs (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 2.5).
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `Spinner` drawing its icon set's indicator as an SVG keeps each frame's image source, per key and colour scheme, beside the indicator it loaded, where every pass copied, coloured and hashed the frame's SVG again and formatted its URI; a kept frame whose bytes `icons::forget_icons` forgot (the install of another atlas, `ThemeAtlas::clear`) is made again, so the next one forgets it too. A pass with one Material spinner took 5.80–5.83 µs on one Linux machine and takes 4.19–4.39 µs, an empty pass about 2.7 µs (release `timings` medians, three runs each at load average 6.4).
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::slider` lays a stated `slider.thumb_hover_color` over the thumb (`styles::composite_over`), as the gpui connector's slider does, where it used the colour as given: the platform states the hover as a layer over the thumb. No bundled preset changes, as every stated thumb hover is opaque and an opaque layer composites to itself.
- **native-theme-egui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` loads a freedesktop icon at the key's size clamped to `1..=512` by native-theme, as the gpui connector does: a size above 512 loads it at 512, where it was looked up at the size given.
- **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-egui**: each connector's `scaled_text_size` doc states both contracts: iced's and gpui's return a size whose product with the factor is not a normal positive number unscaled (`AccessibilityPreferences::scaled`), egui's returns that product as it is, by its spec §8.6, because its atlas writes egui's own size in its place and reports it with a `Note::ValueSanitised`. The three keep one name and signature.
- **native-theme**: adwaita states no shadow on the text input, the checkbox, the drop-down and the segmented control (`border.shadow_enabled` false, both variants), as libadwaita draws none (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.4, §2.5, §2.24, §2.25 `border.shadow_enabled`); they inherited the default border's `true`. No connector reads these four today, so nothing on screen changes.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's button, text input and drop-down have no border at rest (`border.line_width_px` 0, both variants): libadwaita draws each as a `$button_color` fill and adds its inset 1px line only under `prefers-contrast: more` (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.3, §2.4, §2.24 `border.line_width`). They inherited the default border's 1px `$border_color` outline, which the connectors drew; the text area follows the text input (§2.29).
- **native-theme**: adwaita's segmented control has the toggle group's fill, libadwaita's `$button_color` (`currentColor` at 10 %) over the window, `#e6e6e7` / `#38383c` (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.25 `background_color`); it inherited the window background, `#fafafb` / `#222226`.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's slider thumb is libadwaita's `$slider_color`, white at 80 % over the view, `#ffffff` / `#d2d2d2` (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.9 `thumb_color`); the dark thumb inherited the surface colour, `#1d1d20`.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's expander is libadwaita's `AdwExpanderRow` in a boxed list, as its other expander values are: its edge is the list card's 1px outline, `RGB(0 0 6 / 3%)` (`#00000608`) in both modes, and its corners are the card's 12 (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.27 `border.color`, `border.line_width`, `border.corner_radius`). It had GtkExpander's title radius, 6, and the default border's `$border_color`. The model's one radius carries a lone collapsed row, which is the list's first and last; between rows libadwaita rounds none. The shadow stays on, the card's (§2.27 `border.shadow_enabled`).
- **native-theme**: adwaita's toolbar has no edge (`toolbar.border.line_width_px` 0) and no shadow (`shadow_enabled` false), both variants: `.toolbar` sets no border and casts no shadow (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.13 `border.line_width`, `border.shadow_enabled`). It inherited the default border, so the connectors ended the bar in a 1px `$border_color` rule.
- **native-theme**: adwaita states its progress bar's padding, 0 on every side: the progress fills the trough (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.10 `border.padding_*`); it was unset.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's toolbar ends in Breeze's 1px tools-area line, the Header set's separator colour `#b9bbbd` / `#535659` (`toolbar.border.line_width_px` 1, `color`), with no shadow (`shadow_enabled` false): Breeze draws no frame round a toolbar but strokes that line along the bottom of a main window's menu bar and toolbars, the edge the connectors draw (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.13 `border.color`, `border.line_width`, `border.shadow_enabled`). It stated a width of 0, so no edge was drawn, and inherited the default border's shadow.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's toolbar fills with the Header colour set's background, `#dee0e2` / `#292c30` (`toolbar.background_color`), as Breeze fills a main window's menu bar and toolbars in the default configuration: both Breeze schemes have Header colours and the window decoration's border size is automatic unless the user sets one (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.13 `background_color`). It inherited the window background, `#eff0f1` / `#202326`.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's popover is qqc2-desktop-style's `Popup`: its edge is `#c2c5c9` / `#474c51`, the Window colours interpolated at the scheme's frame contrast, and it pads its content by 12 on every side (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.16 `border.color`, `border.padding_*`). It inherited the default border's `#bcc0bf` / `#4d545b` and stated no padding.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's slider thumb is the Button colour set's background, `#fcfcfc` / `#292c30`, which Breeze fills the handle with (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.9 `thumb_color`); it inherited the surface colour, `#ffffff` / `#141618`.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's switch tracks are qqc2-desktop-style's `SwitchIndicator`'s: off `#e6e7e7` / `#3e4144`, the Button text tinted by its background at 90 %, and on `#92cae8` / `#3e7797`, the off track with the highlight at 50 % laid over it (`switch.unchecked_background`, `checked_background`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.21). They were the default border colour, `#bcc0bf` / `#4d545b`, and the accent, `#3daee9`.
- **native-theme**: a hovered kde-breeze button keeps its fill, `#fcfcfc` / `#292c30` (`button.hover_background`): Breeze's hover only draws the outline in the highlight colour (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.3 `hover_background`). It was a pale blue, `#93cee9`, in both variants.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's card is Kirigami's `Card`: a 1px edge in `#ced1d4` / `#3c4146` (the View colours interpolated at the scheme's frame contrast), corners of 5, a shadow and 7 of padding inside the edge on every side, Kirigami's 8 from the card's outer edge less the 1px border (`card.border`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.26). Its fill stays the inherited surface colour, `#ffffff` / `#141618`, which is the View set's background Kirigami fills a card with. The card stated nothing, so it had no edge, no radius and no padding.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze states its progress bar's padding, 0 on every side: Breeze's contents rect is the groove's (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.10 `border.padding_*`); it was unset.
- **native-theme**: windows-11's toolbar has no edge, no radius and no shadow at rest (`toolbar.border.line_width_px` 0, `corner_radius_px` 0, `shadow_enabled` false, both variants): a closed `CommandBar` draws no border, rounds its content only while open and casts no shadow (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.13 `border.line_width`, `border.corner_radius`, `border.shadow_enabled`). It inherited the default border, so the connectors ended the bar in a 1px rule.
- **native-theme**: windows-11's unselected tab labels are WinUI 3's `TabViewItemHeaderForeground`, `TextFillColorSecondary` `#0000009e` / `#ffffffc5` (`tab.font.color`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.11 `font.color`); they inherited the default text colour, `TextFillColorPrimary`, which only the selected tab takes.
- **native-theme**: windows-11's popover edge is WinUI 3's `SurfaceStrokeColorFlyout`, `#0000000f` / `#00000033` (`popover.border.color`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.16 `border.color`). It inherited the default border colour, `#e5e5e5` / `#454545`.
- **native-theme**: windows-11 states its progress bar's padding, 0 on every side: the style leaves the control's `Padding` at its default (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.10 `border.padding_*`); it was unset.
- **native-theme**: macos-monterey states its progress bar's padding, 0 on every side: the OS probe's renders of macOS 14 and 15 start the fill in the track's first column and cover the track's rows (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.10 `border.padding_*`, measured); it was unset.
- **native-theme**: macos-monterey pads a card's content by 5 on every side, `NSBox`'s default `contentViewMargins` (`card.border.padding_*`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.26); it was unset. The box's fill and border are not published, so the card keeps its inherited fill.
- **native-theme**: adwaita, windows-11 and material-3 state their sidebar's padding (`sidebar.border.padding_*`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.12): adwaita 6 at the sides, 6 above and 4 below, libadwaita's `.navigation-sidebar` row margin and list padding; windows-11 3 at the sides, NavigationView's pane and item margins (−1 + 4), its vertical unstated, as no one value is given; material-3 12 at the sides, Compose's `NavigationDrawerItemDefaults.ItemPadding`, its vertical unstated, as the application chooses it; it was unset. The gpui and egui showcases pad their side panel by it, so under these presets the panel is padded and its theme settings and inspector are not: adwaita's panel is padded 6 at the sides, 6 above and 4 below, where its content was padded by `layout.container_margin`, 12; under windows-11 and material-3 the panel's top and bottom take `layout.container_margin`, 6, in the gpui showcase, and egui's own panel margin, 2, in the egui one.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's text input and drop-down are filled with libadwaita's `$button_color` over the window, `#e6e6e7` / `#38383c` (`input.background_color`, `combo_box.background_color`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.4, §2.24 `background_color`), the fill that shows them now that they have no border at rest. The text input was white / `#303034`, uncited, and the drop-down inherited the window background, `#fafafb` / `#222226`.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's focus ring and focused text input are libadwaita's `$focus_border_color`, the standalone accent at 50 %, `#0461be80` / `#81d0ff80` (`defaults.focus_ring_color`, `input.focus_border_color`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.1.5 `focus_ring_color`, §2.4 `focus_border_color`): `--accent-color` is the accent background `#3584e4` with its OKLab lightness capped at 0.5 in light and raised to 0.85 in dark. Both were the accent background, `#3584e4`. A hovered text input no longer recolours its edge (`input.hover_border_color` unset, §2.4: libadwaita's only hover rule on an entry is its icons' opacity); it was `#3584e4`.
- **native-theme**: adwaita's button fill is libadwaita's `$button_color` over the window, `#e6e6e7` / `#38383c` (`button.background_color`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.3 `background_color`), as its text input, drop-down and toggle group are; it was `#e8e8e8` / `#3a3a3e`, uncited.
- **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-build**: every Rust block of the README is compiled by a doctest (`no_run`), as the core and egui READMEs' are, where each was `ignore`d; one line stays `ignore`, the build README's `include!` of `OUT_DIR`, which a doctest has no build script to set. The gpui and iced crate docs, their item examples among them, name native-theme through the connector's re-export, and the iced Quick Start and Manual Path use `?` where they unwrapped. The gpui README and examples name GPUI and gpui-component through gpui-kit (`gpui_kit::…`, `gpui_kit::component::…`), the names an application that depends on gpui-kit, as the Quick start does, has; they named `gpui::` and `gpui_component::`, which only the connector's own dependencies resolved, and the README says how an application on gpui-component directly names them. The iced Quick start and `iced_aw` section state the `iced` and `iced_aw` dependencies their code names, the egui README's examples and the egui and egui-widgets crate docs' name egui through the connector's re-export (`native_theme_egui::egui`, `native_theme_egui_widgets::connector::egui`) and no eframe, and the native-theme-build Quick start states the `native-theme` dependency the generated code names.
- **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-egui**: the three READMEs share one outline (What it does, How it fits, Compatibility, Quick start, Core concepts, Features, Accessibility, Common recipes, Showcase, Gallery, Links, License), with the same five recipes in the same order (Apply user overrides, Runtime theme change, Custom icons, Role icons, Animated spinners). The gpui and iced recipes reach native-theme through the connector's re-export (`native_theme_gpui::native_theme`, `native_theme_iced::native_theme`) and drop the second `native-theme` dependency they asked for, and *Custom icons* generates the enum with `IconGenerator::crate_path` set to it; the native-theme-build README says so. iced's *Features* and *Accessibility* are sections of their own, the widget metrics and fonts out of the recipes. The egui README gains the overlay, custom-icon and spinner recipes, the showcase's `--features watch` command and a link to its source. The connectors' CHANGELOG links are relative, and the egui and egui-widgets READMEs state the licence in full.
- **Development**: the three `scripts/generate_screenshots_*.sh` scripts and `scripts/generate_gifs_theme_switching.sh` share one launch-and-capture loop, `capture_showcases` in `scripts/capture_window.sh`, which takes the showcase, its cargo features and the entries to capture, where each script wrote its own; the screenshots' six presets are one list there, `SCREENSHOT_ENTRIES`. The features stay as they were: iced with `--features iced_aw`, egui with `--all-features`, gpui with its defaults.
- **native-theme**: the root and crate READMEs' `ResolvedTheme` paragraph counts 34 optional colours, the switch's five outline colours among them (it said 29), which a theme, a bundled preset included, may leave `None` (it said every bundled preset states them), and says that the switch's outlines are `None` where the theme states none, so no outline is drawn, and that a disabled track outline's colour left `None` takes the enabled one; the root README's also names the expander's arrow side and frame and the tab's active-indicator side among the values that are `None` where the theme states none, as the crate README's did. The crate README's icon section names the feature each loader needs, without which it gives `None`, and loads a freedesktop icon from the active icon theme and from a named one; the root README's egui showcase line gives its `--features watch` command, as the gpui and iced lines do.
- **Development**: `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s commit rules say that a `refactor` changes no behaviour (it said no API), that a breaking change marks its prefix with `!` whatever its kind, and that a commit resolving a code-review item ends its summary with the item's id; its release step 2 says that `scripts/generate_assets_release.sh` stops on a showcase-parity difference `docs/showcase-exceptions.toml` gives no reason for and rewrites `docs/COMPATIBILITY.toml` and the connector READMEs' Verified lines, which the asset commit includes; and its crate table says that native-theme uses `native-theme-derive` without re-exporting it. The pull request template's checklist starts with `./scripts/check_release.sh --commit`, the canonical gate.
- **Development**: the presets README's gated sizes, which a native preset states only where `docs/platform-facts.md` gives them, take in the tab's item gap and indicator width, the expander's arrow gap and indent, the checkbox's radio and check-mark sizes and the switch's unchecked thumb, and it says that the colour-scheme presets and `ios-13` state none, both of which `native-theme/src/presets/documented_sizes.rs` checks. It also says that every platform preset states `button_order` in both variants, which no reader reads and `resolve_platform_defaults` fills only for a theme that states none (the colour-scheme presets), that every preset states `icon_theme`, `kde-breeze` per variant, which the KDE and GNOME readers replace in the variant they read, and that on a GNOME or Budgie X11 session the reader reports a text-scaling factor of 1.0 with the DPI still 96. `docs/new-os-version-guide.md` says the Windows reader reads no focus-border metric, lists the sizes `documented_sizes.rs` checks and the switch's outline widths, stated only where the platform draws that outline, and walks a new Linux desktop through `LinuxReader`, `linux_reader_kind()`, `linux_reader()`, `linux_preset_for_de()`, the icon-theme detection's `icon_theme_source()` and the watcher's `linux_backend()`, where it named `select_reader()` alone.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the command line is the one the three showcases share (`tools/showcase-support`), with its validators and error texts. `--icon-set default` makes the icons follow the theme's own icon theme, as the icon-theme Select's `default` row does (`system` where the theme names none that is installed); `--icon-theme` is gone, as `--icon-set <installed theme>` already loads from that theme; `--tab text-inputs` is gone, `inputs` naming the page. On macOS and Windows `--screenshot` captures the window with its frame through xcap's safe API, with the same frame check, in place of the showcase's own AppKit and Win32 calls. On Windows the copy is a `BitBlt` of the one monitor at the frame's centre without `CAPTUREBLT`, so a layered window over the showcase's is not copied with it, and a window across two monitors is clipped at that monitor's edge and fails the frame check.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: a colour-mode switch under `default` installs the other variant of the desktop's theme read at its last install, without reading the OS again, which Reload System Theme and the theme watcher do. The watcher (`--features watch`) reads an OS theme change in every colour mode, so under a chosen Light or Dark the OS's accent, fonts and icon theme still follow it in that variant, where it followed the OS only under System; and a theme install loads the icons again only where their set, their icon theme or, for a system set (freedesktop, SF Symbols, Segoe Fluent), `defaults.font.color`, which its loaders colour a symbolic icon in, changed, and otherwise recolours the loaded ones. Each switch read the whole OS theme and loaded every icon again (timed on gpui's test platform on one KDE host, release build: about 20 ms a switch with the theme's own icons and 8 ms with Material chosen, now 18.5 and 6.2).
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the inspector builds the look its Widget tab is drawn in, and the resolved theme's JSON its rows read their values from, once a theme install, and keeps them (a preset or the desktop's theme installed, a colour-mode switch, a Preferences change, the theme watcher's re-read): it built them, the whole theme serialised, on every frame the window drew, the hint shown before any hover included, and the Basic page's Spinner asks for a frame every frame (building them took about 70 µs of a frame's 6 ms, timed on gpui's test platform on one KDE host, release build). A frame no longer clones the gpui-component theme, in the window's content or in the inspector, nor the inspector the info it shows.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Icons page's animation timer runs only while the Icons page is shown, the one page that draws its frames: on every page each tick stepped the frames and redrew the whole window, page, chrome and inspector, once a frame of the chosen set's indicator, every 42 ms for a bundled set's and every 80 ms for a freedesktop theme's (native-theme `spinners.rs`, `freedesktop.rs`).
- **Showcases (gpui)**: every page but Basic is padded by `layout.window_margin`, its sections are `layout.section_gap` apart and the controls of a row `layout.widget_gap` apart, as on the Basic page, where gpui-component's rem multiples stood whatever the theme stated: `p_4` round the page, `gap_5` between its sections (`gap_4` on the Theme Map) and `gap_1` to `gap_8` in its rows. Under kde-breeze, at its 13.33 px font, a page's margin goes from about 13 px to 10, its sections from about 17 px to 18 apart and its rows' controls to 6. Under macos-tahoe, which states no `section_gap`, the sections of all ten pages touch: nine lose their `gap_5` and the Theme Map its `gap_4`. The Inputs page's InputGroups are `layout.widget_gap` apart too, and a Badge's count stands `layout.widget_gap` from the next Badge: upstream draws it 3 px a digit past its Button (the Badges were 2 rem apart whatever the theme). Two distances no theme value stands for keep their size as named showcase constants, each named in its Widget Info: the Icons page's icon grids and the room beside the Layout page's Carousel for its outside controls; the Theme Map's swatch gaps and the inspector's swatches, already the showcase's own, now say so in theirs too.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Widget Info of the samples the showcase lays out itself names the padding and gaps it gives them, each a named choice of the showcase's with its size, as the theme states none: the Icons page's cells and animated-icon cards, the Layout page's Separator samples, scroll area, Collapsible and Carousel slides, the Overlays page's ContextMenu area and the Theme Map's swatch square. The icon cells said only that their padding was the showcase's own layout, and the others said nothing. What they draw is unchanged.
- **Showcases (iced)**: the command line is the one the three showcases share (`tools/showcase-support`), with its validators and error texts, and so are the element list's reader, Widget Info's values, rows and Copy text, the layout dump's writer, the theme and icon-theme pickers' rows and the status bar's environment text. `--icon-set default` makes the icons follow the theme's own icon theme, as the icon-theme picker's `default` row does (`system` where the theme names none that is installed), where it was refused; `--tab inputs` opens the Inputs page, the key the gpui and egui showcases give their Inputs page, where it was `text-inputs`, and `text-inputs`, `textinputs` and `thememap` are reported and ignored as any unknown page is. `--open-menu` takes any title of the menu bar (File, View, Theme, Help) and holds that menu open, and an unknown one is reported and ignored, where the showcase held only the Theme menu open and exited with 1 on any other name. `--dump-layout F --screenshot P` writes both files and quits once the later of the two is written, where the showcase quit once F was written, before P's capture; under `--capture` the window stays open after either file, for the tool that captures it, so `--screenshot P --capture` now keeps it open after P is written, where the showcase quit. A `--dump-layout` run whose dump is not written within 20 s, the time the capture scripts wait for it, exits with 1 naming its flag and path, where it could stay open, and a failed `--screenshot` names its flag and path too. A colour the showcase prints in Widget Info and on the Theme Map prints through native-theme's `Rgba`, so one whose alpha rounds to 255 (from about 0.998 up, which only a computed colour reaches) prints as `#rrggbb`, where it printed `#rrggbbff`.
- **Showcases (iced)**: on macOS and Windows `--screenshot` captures the window with its frame through the shared crate's capture (`tools/showcase-support`, xcap's safe API), as the gpui and egui showcases do, with the same frame check, in place of the showcase's own AppKit and Win32 calls, which needed `unsafe`; the example's `objc2` and `windows` dev-dependencies are gone. On Windows the copy is a `BitBlt` of the one monitor at the frame's centre without `CAPTUREBLT`, so a layered window over the showcase's is not copied with it, and a window across two monitors is clipped at that monitor's edge and fails the frame check.
- **Showcases (iced)**: Widget Info's line for a colour of a listed element that the connector draws is read from the connector's `contract-leaves.toml`: it names the `styles::*` fields, palette slots or accessors the colour moves, under the statuses the page draws the element in, and on a bundled preset only those it moves there; under the OS theme it names those it moves in a bundled theme and says so; a colour no row reads gives the contract's reason. A colour the showcase draws itself names the function that draws it (`ghost_button`, `menu_row`, `tab_style`, `list_row`, `surface`, `line_style`, `basic_table` and the others), each held to the leaf by a test that splices it. The On toggle's `button.active_background` and `.active_text_color` show as drawn only where the theme states no checked pair. Lines that did not hold go: a disabled check box's and switch's checked colours where the theme states their disabled ones, `defaults.focus_ring_color` on the focused field, where the line said iced draws no focus ring and adwaita's inset ring is drawn as the field's border in `input.focus_border_color`, and `defaults.font.color` as the text's colour, which iced takes from `defaults.text_color`.
- **Showcases (iced)**: the chrome is spaced as the gpui showcase's, and a layout distance the theme leaves unstated draws none, where the showcase's own 8, 16 and 24 px stood in for it. On the 15 presets that state no `status_bar.border.padding` (the four catppuccins, dracula, gruvbox, ios-13, macos-monterey, macos-tahoe, material-3, nord, one-dark, solarized, tokyo-night and windows-11) the status bar is padded as gpui's, by gpui-component's StatusBar padding: a quarter of the scaled `defaults.font.size` above and below and a half at the sides, where `layout.container_margin` stood (6 px on twelve of them, 8 on ios-13 and macos-monterey, 16 on macos-tahoe). Under macos-tahoe, which states no `section_gap` and no `container_margin`, the sections of every page and of Preferences and the Basic page's columns and groups touch, where they were 24 px apart, so each of the Basic page's five columns is at least the 170 px its column count assumed (about 186), where they were 167 and squeezed its 170 px controls to their width; the side panel's settings, the inspector's tab row and content, the page tabs and the Display, Graphics and Extra pages' containers are unpadded, where they were padded 16 px; the toolbar is padded 8 px all round, gpui's `TOOLBAR_PADDING`, where it was 16; and the inspector's Theme tab prints `not stated` for `section_gap` and `container_margin`, where it printed "24px (showcase)" and "16px (showcase)". Where the theme states a side of `sidebar.border.padding` (adwaita, windows-11 and material-3), the whole side panel is padded by it, a side it leaves unstated by `container_margin`, and its settings, tab row and content add none, where the panel ignored the stated padding and padded its content by `container_margin` (adwaita: 6 across, 6 above and 4 below the panel, where its content was padded 12). The Command Palette, Preferences and About dialogs are padded 16 px inside their border on a side `dialog.border.padding` leaves unstated, gpui-component's `Dialog` padding: on the eleven presets that state a `container_margin` and no dialog padding (the four catppuccins, dracula, gruvbox, ios-13, nord, one-dark, solarized and tokyo-night) where `container_margin` stood, measured from the bounds (6 px, 8 on ios-13). Where the theme states none, the toolbar's items are 4 px apart (gpui's `TOOLBAR_GAP`), the theme settings' rows 8 (`THEME_SETTINGS_GAP`) and the menu bar's sides 8 (`MENU_BAR_PADDING`), each named in Widget Info as gpui's showcase constant; no bundled preset leaves `widget_gap` unstated.
- **Showcases (iced)**: every tooltip stands from its control by one rule, gpui-component's tooltip margin (`m_3`, three quarters of the scaled `defaults.font.size`: 10 px under kde-breeze's 10 pt at 96 dpi), and the Tooltip's Widget Info names the rule and its value. The gpui showcase places its tooltips, the chrome's and the Basic page's alike, that margin and a pixel off the pointer; iced's tooltip widget places a tooltip by its control, so iced's keep the same distance from the control. The Basic page's Tooltip button and the Display page's four stood 4 px from their control, and the chrome's tooltips (the toolbar's buttons, the status bar's side-panel toggle, the dialogs' Close button and the About dialog's link) and the Basic page's Copy, Paste and Delete buttons `layout.widget_gap` away. The side-panel toggle's tooltip opens above it, where below it, at the window's bottom edge, iced pulled the bubble back over the toggle; gpui's turns above the pointer there too.
- **Showcases (iced)**: on macOS the menus are in the system menu bar, as the gpui and egui showcases' are, and the window draws no menu row, where it drew its File, View, Theme and Help row on every platform. The menu bar follows Apple's HIG (*The menu bar*): the app menu holds About showcase-iced (the About dialog), Settings… ⌘, (the Preferences dialog), Services, Hide showcase-iced ⌘H, Hide Others ⌥⌘H, Show All and Quit showcase-iced ⌘Q; File holds Close ⌘W; View and Theme hold the window row's rows, Theme without its Preferences…; a Window menu holds Minimize ⌘M, Zoom and Bring All to Front; there is no Help menu. `--open-menu` is reported and ignored there, as no menu can be held open, and a `--screenshot` run exits with 1 before its capture where the menu bar macOS holds does not have those five menus, in that order. The menus go in once the window is open, so winit's default app menu can show for the first frame (docs/todo.md, *iced showcase: the macOS system menu goes in after the window opens*).
- **Showcases**: the three list their pages in one order, gpui's, and `docs/showcase-elements.toml`'s header records it with how their pages correspond (iced's Display and egui's Text are gpui's Typography, egui's Containers its Layout, iced's Graphics its Charts): iced's Text Inputs page is Inputs, as the gpui and egui showcases' is, and the egui showcase's page tabs and View menu list Inputs before Selection, Data before Text and Colour after Overlays, where egui and iced ordered Selection and Inputs oppositely and nothing recorded which pages correspond. Each showcase's test holds its page list to the record.
- **Showcases (iced)**: the side panel's Theme picker is searchable, as the gpui showcase's is: it is iced's `combo_box`, dressed as the Mode and Icon theme drop-downs and as wide and tall as they are, and typing filters its rows (`nord` leaves Nord), Up and Down move the highlight and Enter installs the highlighted preset, where it was a drop-down without search. iced's combo box matches the name a row shows, not the preset's key, which gpui's matches too, so a key's word the name lacks finds no row of that preset: `3` of material-3, `13` of ios-13, `monterey` of macos-monterey (macOS Sonoma); the field's placeholder says "Filter by name…". Mode and Icon theme stay drop-downs, as gpui's are.

### Deprecated

- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::freedesktop_name_for_gpui_icon(icon, de)` (Linux). It picks a freedesktop name by desktop, where the icon theme in use decides which name exists (Adwaita, with the AdwaitaLegacy it inherits, lacks the Breeze name for 48 of the 50 icons the two conventions name apart); use `icons::freedesktop_name_candidates(icon)` with `native_theme::icons::FreedesktopLoader::any` in the one icon theme the icon is loaded from.

### Fixed

- **native-theme**: a `[layout]` value that is negative, NaN or infinite (`widget_gap_px`, `container_margin_px`, `window_margin_px`, `section_gap_px`) fails `Theme::resolve`, `SystemTheme::from_system()` and `SystemTheme::with_overlay` with `Error::ResolutionInvalid` naming its key, `layout.widget_gap_px`; it reached `SystemTheme::layout` unchecked, although the four fields declare a non-negative range.
- **native-theme**: validation range-checks the card, so a negative, NaN or infinite `card.border` padding side fails with `ResolutionInvalid` (it reached `ResolvedTheme::card`), and a widget border's `corner_radius_px` and `line_width_px` must be finite and non-negative, as `defaults.border`'s already were (`[light.button.border] corner_radius_px = -4.0` resolved).
- **native-theme**: a NaN or infinite `defaults.focus_ring_offset_px` fails validation; a negative offset, an inset ring, stays valid.
- **native-theme**: `Theme::from_toml` parses a theme file with no `name`, such as an overlay that states only `[light.defaults] accent_color`; it failed with `missing field "name"`, although `Theme::merge` keeps the base's name. The name is then empty.
- **native-theme**: a `link.font.color` a preset, a TOML overlay or a platform reader states reaches the resolved theme; resolving replaced it with `defaults.link_color`, which now fills it only when it is unset. The KDE reader no longer states `link.font.color` beside `defaults.link_color` (both were `[Colors:View] ForegroundLink`), so on KDE an overlay's `defaults.link_color` still recolours link text.
- **native-theme**: on KDE a hovered and a pressed link follow the colour scheme's `ForegroundLink` and an overlay's `defaults.link_color`, as link text does: the `kde-breeze` preset no longer states their colours (Breeze's default blue, `#2980b9`, dark `#1d99f3`), which a Qt link does not change from the palette's link colour on hover or press.
- **native-theme**: the KDE reader colours a combo box in the colour scheme's `[Colors:Button]` background and text, and a menu's text in `[Colors:Window] ForegroundNormal`, as `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.24 and §2.6 give them (they followed the window colours); it no longer states the popover's colours or the separator's, which inherit the window's and the border colour (§2.16, §2.18): it gave the popover the `[Colors:View]` set and the separator `[Colors:Window] ForegroundInactive`, a text colour. A `menuFont` keeps the menu's text colour.
- **native-theme**: the KDE reader derives the colours kde-breeze states from the colour scheme as Plasma computes them, so another scheme, a custom accent or `[KDE] frameContrast` reaches them; it served Breeze's values under every scheme: a hovered button keeps the `[Colors:Button]` background and a slider thumb is filled with it; the switch's off track is the Button text tinted by its background at 90 % (`Qt.tint`) and its on track the `[Colors:Selection]` background at 50 % over it; the toolbar fills with `[Colors:Header]` (each missing key, or the whole set without the group, from `[Colors:Window]`) and its edge is that set's separator colour; the popover's edge is the Window set's and the card's the View set's background moved towards their text by the frame contrast (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.3, §2.9, §2.13, §2.16, §2.21, §2.26). The arithmetic is Qt's (`QColor`'s 16-bit channels, Qt Quick's `tint`, `KColorUtils::mix`, Kirigami's HSV interpolation), checked against Qt 6.11.2 on test vectors (`scripts/generate_qt_color_vectors.py`); the checked button and the checkbox border use the same `mix`. The accent blends come from kdeglobals, so an overlay's or the portal's accent does not move the switch's on track, as it does not move the checked checkbox or the progress fill, and the toolbar takes the Header fill as Breeze paints it with the default window border size (the reader does not read kwinrc).
- **native-theme**: `kde-breeze` states Breeze's colours and sizes where the inherited ones differ, each from its `docs/platform-facts.md` row: the title bar in `[WM] activeBackground` (`#e3e5e7`, dark `#272c31`; it was the surface colour), a list in `[Colors:View] BackgroundNormal` (`#ffffff`, dark `#141618`; it was the window colour) and its header in `[Colors:Header]` (`#dee0e2`, dark `#292c30`), a combo box in `[Colors:Button]` (`#fcfcfc`, dark `#292c30`; it was the window colour), a visited link in `ForegroundVisited` `#9b59b6` (it was the link colour), menu icons at KIconLoader's Small 16 (they were the toolbar's 22), and the dark switch thumb in `[Colors:Button] BackgroundNormal` `#292c30` (it was `#fcfcfc`), the handle colour qqc2-desktop-style draws, and a disabled switch's thumb in that colour's Disabled shade (`#f0f0f0`, dark `#272a2e`; it was `#fcfcfc` at half alpha); the placeholder, menu, list and combo box text state their colour sets. Text fields (and so text areas), check boxes, progress bars, combo boxes, segmented controls and expanders state no shadow, where `defaults.border.shadow_enabled` gave them one.
- **native-theme**: a `text_scale` entry may state its size and its line height in different units (`size_pt` with `line_height_px`), each converted to pixels on its own; `Theme::from_toml` rejected the pair, so a theme merged from an overlay's `[light.text_scale.caption] size_px = 12.0` onto `ios-13`'s `size_pt` and `line_height_pt` could be saved with `to_toml` but not read back.
- **native-theme**: on a Linux desktop without a reader (KDE without the `kde` feature, GNOME without `portal`, XFCE, Sway, an unrecognised desktop and the like), `SystemTheme::from_system()` returns the desktop's platform preset whole: on KDE without `kde` it was a mix, the active variant Adwaita's colours, the inactive one Breeze's, named "Adwaita" with the `breeze` icon theme; it is now `kde-breeze` in both variants, named "KDE Breeze". Its `mode` is read fresh from the OS (`detect::detect_is_dark`), as the readers read it; it came from the cached `detect::system_is_dark`, so a second `from_system` after the user switched to dark mode returned the old mode unless `detect::invalidate_caches()` ran first.
- **native-theme**: `pipeline::platform_preset_name()` names `kde-breeze` on an unrecognised Linux desktop with a `kdeglobals` file (with the `kde` feature) where no portal backend answers, the preset `SystemTheme::from_system()` uses there; it named `adwaita`. `from_system` asks the portal backend first and follows its answer, which `platform_preset_name()` cannot ask, so where a backend answers the two can still differ.
- **native-theme**: `Error::PlatformUnsupported` from `SystemTheme::from_system()` and `watch::on_theme_change` names the platform (`std::env::consts::OS`, e.g. "freebsd"), as the field documents; it was "unsupported".
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::with_overlay` overlays stack: `sys.with_overlay(&a)?.with_overlay(&b)?` keeps what `a` states unless `b` states it too; the second call replayed the platform reader and the preset alone, and dropped `a`.
- **native-theme**: `SystemTheme::with_overlay` applies the overlay's `icon_theme`, `icon_set` and `[layout]`, through the same icon-theme tiers as `SystemTheme::from_system()` (a variant's `defaults.icon_theme`, then the theme's, then the detected one); it kept the base theme's `icon_theme`, `icon_set` and `layout` whatever the overlay stated.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset states what WinUI 3 draws (`docs/platform-facts.md`, checked against microsoft-ui-xaml at 8463f45 and an OS probe of Windows 11 25H2): text on a status fill `#000000e4` in dark mode and white on the light warning fill; the focus ring in `FocusStrokeColorOuter` (`#000000e4`, dark `#ffffff`) instead of the accent, 1 px outside the control; toolbar and menu icons 16 instead of 20; the button's hover fill `ControlFillColorSecondary` as it draws over the window (`#f6f6f6`, dark `#323232`) and its pressed fill `ControlFillColorTertiary` the same way (`#f5f5f5`, dark `#272727`); the tooltip text 12 px (9 pt); the slider's track and thumb, the progress bar's track and its 1.5 radius; tabs at least 100 wide; the selected sidebar and list row in `SubtleFillColorSecondary` with primary text instead of the accent; the popover's acrylic fallback (`#f9f9f9`, dark `#2c2c2c`); the dark switch's knob black; the combo box's fill `ControlFillColorDefault`.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset states WinUI 3's translucent brushes with their alpha, as the resources give them: text `TextFillColorPrimary` (`#000000e4`), muted and placeholder text `TextFillColorSecondary` (`#0000009e`, dark `#ffffffc5`), disabled text `TextFillColorDisabled` (`#0000005c`, dark `#ffffff5d`), the surface and button fills (`#ffffffb3`; dark `#ffffff0d`, `#ffffff0f`), the unchecked check box's and switch's fill (`#00000006`, dark `#00000019`) and the check box's border (`#00000072`, dark `#ffffff8b`). Links take the accent shades WinUI draws them in (`#003e92`, dark `#99ebff`, and the next shades on hover and press), dark info `#4cc2ff`; the tooltip is light, the popover's acrylic fallback `#f9f9f9` with primary text (it was `#2c2c2c` with white text), and the dark one that fallback, `#2c2c2c` (it was `#3a3a3a`); the docked sidebar and the scroll bar's track are transparent, the dialog `SolidBackgroundFillColorBase`; menu, list and sidebar hovers are `SubtleFillColorSecondary` (`#00000009`), and the link's hover is transparent (an inline `Hyperlink` has no background); a pressed button's text is `TextFillColorSecondary`; the separator takes the border colour, and the expander's chevron the text colour.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset's border, and the separator with it, is `CardStrokeColorDefault` as the resource gives it (`#0000000f`, dark `#00000019`; it was the composite `#e5e5e5` and an unsourced dark `#454545`), and `defaults.border.opacity` that brush's alpha (0.059, dark 0.098; was 0.14); the input's fill is `TextControlBackground` = `ControlFillColorDefault` (`#ffffffb3`, dark `#ffffff0f`; was `#ffffff` and `#2d2d2d`) and its hover border its rest border, as in WinUI; the combo box's hover and the off switch's hover are WinUI's replacing fills as they draw over the window (`#f6f6f6` and `#e5e5e5`, dark `#323232` and `#2a2a2a`), the off switch's disabled track `ControlAltFillColorDisabled` (transparent) and the slider's disabled track `ControlStrongFillColorDisabled` (`#00000051`, dark `#ffffff3f`).
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset states its title bar: Mica, whose solid fallback `SolidBackgroundFillColorBase` is the window background (`#f3f3f3`, dark `#202020`), active and inactive, with the TitleBar control's `TextFillColorPrimary` text and, inactive, `TextFillColorTertiary` (`#00000072`, dark `#ffffff87`). Since the Windows reader stopped writing the DWM colorization the title bar resolved to the translucent surface (`#ffffffb3`), and the inactive fill and text were unsourced (`#f1f1f1` and `#1a1a1a99`, dark `#2b2b2b` and `#ffffff99`). Under high contrast the reader writes the title bar `COLOR_WINDOW` with `COLOR_BTNTEXT` text, inactive `COLOR_GRAYTEXT`, as WinUI 3's HighContrast dictionaries draw the TitleBar control.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset's text selection inherits the accent, as WinUI 3's `TextControlSelectionHighlightColor` (`SystemAccentColor`), so on a live system it follows the user's accent instead of a fixed `#0078d4`, and so do the segmented control's active fill and text; the text box's focused line is `SystemAccentColorDark1` (`#0067c0`, dark `SystemAccentColorLight2` `#4cc2ff`), the on switch's hover `AccentFillColorSecondary` as it draws over the window (`#1875c5`, dark `#48b2e9`), and the slider's disabled fill and the on switch's disabled track `AccentFillColorDisabled` (`#00000037`, dark `#ffffff28`), where they were unsourced accent tints. It states the fields whose fallbacks `docs/inheritance-rules.toml` lists as wrong: the placeholder `TextFillColorSecondary` and the menu's and combo box's text `TextFillColorPrimary` (the colours they inherited), the menu's background the acrylic fallback (`#f9f9f9`, dark `#2c2c2c`; it was the window background), the list header the window background (it was the translucent surface), the light scroll bar thumb the measured `#c2c2c2` (it was the muted text colour), and no shadow on the text box, check box, progress bar, combo box, segmented control and expander. Under high contrast the Windows reader writes the border `COLOR_WINDOWTEXT` (`CardStrokeColorDefault`'s HighContrast colour) and the progress bar's border `COLOR_WINDOWTEXT` and track `COLOR_WINDOW`, which kept the preset's light-mode strokes.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset fills the checked button with `AccentFillColorDefault` (`#0067c0`, dark `#4cc2ff`) instead of the accent itself; the card and the selected tab are framed in `CardStrokeColorDefault`, 1 px, radius 8, with no shadow; the text-scale entries take the Fluent type ramp's line heights (Caption 16, Subtitle 28, Title 36, Display 92 px); and it no longer states unsourced slider thumb hover and disabled colours, the switch's disabled thumb, the expander's hover, the splitter's hover and the scroll bar's pressed thumb, which now inherit or stay unset.
- **native-theme**: the `windows-11` preset fills the primary button with `AccentFillColorDefault` (`#0067c0`, dark `#4cc2ff`), WinUI 3's `AccentButtonBackground` (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.3), as its checked button and the Windows reader's primary button already were; it inherited the accent itself, `#0078d4`, because the iced connector derived the primary button's hover from the accent. Its label is unchanged.
- **native-theme**: on macOS `SystemTheme::from_system()` reports the active appearance in `SystemTheme::mode` (it was always `Light`), so an application on a Mac in dark mode starts dark in every connector.
- **native-theme**: the documentation of the `windows` module, which docs.rs builds for its Windows target, no longer links to two private items, and the `fonts` and `watch` modules are documented in one place (their own file), so their links resolve in the same scope on stable and on the nightly rustdoc docs.rs uses, which reported their explicit targets as redundant; the `watch` summary is no longer shown twice.
- **Development**: `scripts/generate_gifs_spinners.py` assembles the theme-switching GIFs the READMEs show at the captured window's size, as the screenshots beside them are, instead of scaling them to 600 px wide, and refuses frames of differing sizes; the root and crate READMEs show the spinner GIFs at their own 200×120 instead of 80 px high.
- **native-theme**: the `ios-13` preset takes what iOS 13-18 draw where a source gives it: the section heading (17 pt Semibold), dialog title (28 pt) and every text-scale line height from Apple's Dynamic Type table; muted text in `secondaryLabel` and disabled text in `tertiaryLabel` (a disabled select's too); a button's fill, its blue text (pressed and hovered too) and the switch's green on track and grey off track as WebKit draws them on iOS; and the dark text field's fill in `systemBackground`. Each value with a source cites it in the file, whose header states the iOS versions it covers (13-18) and was verified on (18).
- **native-theme**: the `ios-13` preset states the rest of what its sources give for iOS 13-18: a disabled switch's tracks keep 0.4 of their alpha, as WebKit draws them (they were the tracks at half alpha, unsourced); the slider's and progress bar's tracks are `opaqueFill` (`#e4e4e5`, dark `#2b2b2e`) and a text field's placeholder `placeholderText` (they took the muted text colour); a drop-down's text is systemBlue on `opaqueSecondaryFill`, as WebKit's iOS select (it was black or white on the window colour); the line height is SF Pro's 1.19 (it was 1.2); and text fields, check boxes, progress bars, drop-downs, segmented controls and expanders cast no shadow (they inherited one).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's split-view divider is AppKit's default, the thick one: 9 wide (it was 6, a GNUstep value) and clear, as `NSSplitView.dividerColor` is for that style (it took the border colour).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's push button is 20 high, AppKit's `intrinsicContentSize` on macOS 14 (it was 22), with its title 1 below the bezel's top and 3 above its bottom, measured from the bezel's outer edge, where AppKit's title rect puts it (it was 3 and 3).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's slider knob is 20 across, as macOS 14 draws it (it was 21).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's drop-down places its title 1 below the bezel's top and 3 above its bottom, where AppKit's pop-up button puts it (it was 3 and 3).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's colours are the ones macOS 14 and 15 report (an OS probe of 14.8.9 and 15.7.9) for the AppKit colours `docs/platform-facts.md` names, with their alpha where AppKit gives one: window `#ececec` (dark `#323232`), text `labelColor` `#000000d8` (dark `#ffffffd8`), muted and disabled text, borders and separators (`separatorColor`), the focus ring, links, the selection (`#0064e1`, dark `#0059d1`) and text selection, and the button, text-field and list colours, among them the placeholder, column header and grid. The success green is macOS 14's `#28cd41` (dark `#32d74b`; it was the macOS 26 green), the dark accent is `#007aff`, as `controlAccentColor` is in both appearances, and text beside a status colour is `labelColor` (it was white). The drop shadow is `NSShadow`'s default colour, black at an alpha of 1/3 (`#00000055`; it was `#00000018`, dark `#00000040`).
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset's `disabled_opacity` is 1.0 (it was 0.3, which faded the already dimmed disabled colours a second time) and `border.opacity` 0.098, `separatorColor`'s alpha; tooltip text is 11 pt, as `toolTipsFontOfSize:` returns, menu icons are 16 (they inherited the 24 toolbar size), the overlay scroll bar's track is transparent, and the text field, check box, progress bar, drop-down, segmented control and expander cast no shadow, and the spinner is drawn in black (dark white) at the leading fin's alpha, 82/255, instead of the accent colour.
- **native-theme**: `kde-breeze` light draws text on a selection and on the accent in Breeze's `#ffffff` (`[Colors:Selection] ForegroundNormal`; `#fcfcfc` was Breeze's before Plasma 5.21), and danger, success and info text in the window text `#232629`, as the KDE reader reads them on a Breeze Light desktop. `adwaita` draws its lines in libadwaita 1.10's `$border_color` over the window, `#dcdcde` light and `#434347` dark (were `#d5d5d5` and `#4a4a4e`), and a popover's outline in libadwaita 1.10's `RGB(0 0 0 / 5%)` instead of the line colour.
- **native-theme**: `kde-breeze` states the inactive title bar's text in Breeze's `[WM] inactiveForeground` (`#707d8a`, dark `#a1a9b1`; it was the window text and an unsourced `#a1a4a6`), alternate list rows in `[Colors:View] BackgroundAlternate` (`#f7f7f7`, dark `#1d1f22`; were `#f5f6f7` and `#2a2e32`) and the light pressed button's text in `#ffffff`, `[Colors:Selection] ForegroundNormal`; its popover takes the window colour and its separator the border colour, as `docs/platform-facts.md` gives them (they were `#ffffff` / `#31363b` and `#c4c8c6` / `#3e4550`). `adwaita` uses overlay scroll bars, GNOME's `overlay-scrolling` default (it stated persistent ones), and its dark surface is libadwaita 1.10's `view_bg_color` `#1d1d20` (it was `#303034`), which the dark list headers and slider thumb inherit.
- **native-theme**: `adwaita`'s text caret is the entry's text colour, libadwaita 1.10's `caret-color: currentColor` (`#2e3436`, dark `#ffffff`; were `#3584e4` and `#78aeed`). Its dark title bar is `headerbar_bg_color` `#2e2e32` and its dark cards `card_bg_color`, white at 8 % (`#ffffff14`); an inactive title bar is `headerbar_backdrop_color`, the window colour (`#fafafb`, dark `#222226`; light was the title bar's `#ffffff`, dark `#303034`).
- **native-theme**: on GNOME the text-scaling factor is applied once on Wayland. The GNOME reader, and `ResolutionContext::from_system()` on a GNOME or Budgie desktop, took the font DPI from `Xft.dpi`, which gnome-settings-daemon sets to 96 × `text-scaling-factor` × the X11 UI scale, while the connectors also multiply text sizes by the factor, so at 1.5 text was drawn 2.25 times its size. GNOME's font DPI is 96 now; the factor stays in `AccessibilityPreferences`.
- **native-theme**: on a GNOME or Budgie X11 session the text-scaling factor is applied once: the GNOME reader reports an `AccessibilityPreferences::text_scaling_factor` of 1.0 there. winit and gpui take their X11 scale factor from the `Xft.dpi` gnome-settings-daemon writes as 96 × `text-scaling-factor` × the window scale, so the connectors' text scaling drew text at the factor squared (2.25 times its size at 1.5). An X11 client through Xwayland on a Wayland session still gets the factor twice: the reader cannot see a client's backend.
- **native-theme**: the `macos-monterey` preset takes what an OS probe of macOS 14.8.9 and 15.7.9 reports and draws (`docs/platform-facts.md`): the text field is 21 high with its text 2 in from each edge (it was 22, with 4 and 3) and its caret `textInsertionPointColor`, `#007aff` (it took the text colour); a check box's label sits 6 from its box (it was 4); the slider's track is 4 high (it was 5) and its dark knob white at alpha 128/255 (it took the dark surface colour); the switch's knob is 20 across (it was 18), its off track black (dark: white) at alpha 26/255 (it was `#d5d5d5`, dark `#3d3d3d`) and its dark knob white at alpha 191/255; the drop-down is 20 high (it was 21) with its title 8 in from the left edge, in `controlColor` with `controlTextColor` text (it took the window's background and text colours); the segmented control is 22 high (it was 24); and a list's rows are `controlBackgroundColor`, `#ffffff` (dark `#1e1e1e`), under the alternate rows' `#f4f5f5` (they took the window background, so the alternate rows were the lighter ones). The dark slider knob keeps its colour on hover and when disabled (it turned an unsourced `#3a3a3c` and `#2d2d2d80`, darker than the window).
- **native-theme**: serialising a widget's theme struct (`ButtonTheme`, `LayoutTheme` and the others), and a `Theme` with it, to a format other than TOML, such as JSON, leaves out the unset fields, as TOML output did; it wrote `null` for each, because their `#[serde_with::skip_serializing_none]` came after `#[derive(Serialize)]`, where it has no effect. TOML output is unchanged.
- **native-theme**: a colour string with a non-ASCII character, such as `"#é0"` in a theme file, fails with `invalid hex color "#é0": not ASCII hex digits`, quoting the string; it was reported as a wrong digit (`invalid red hex digit 'Ã'`, the first byte of `é`) or, for `"#é"`, as a length counted in bytes (`invalid hex color length 2`).
- **native-theme-derive**: a malformed `#[theme(...)]` or `#[theme_layer(...)]` attribute is a compile error at the attribute, never a proc-macro panic: a `min_max_pair`, `resolved_name` or `inherit_from` segment that is not an identifier, an `inherit_from` that does not start with `defaults.`, `category` beside `nested` or `resolved_type`, a second `check` or range, a range whose minimum exceeds its maximum and an f32 range bound that is not finite; the later attribute silently won, or the range rejected every value. `#[theme_layer(skip_inventory)]` also skips the border and font inheritance registrations.
- **native-theme**: `Error::NoVariant` displays "theme defines neither a light nor a dark variant (requested Dark)", the only case it is returned for; it said "theme has no variant for Dark".
- **native-theme**: a `ResolutionContext` whose `font_dpi` is not finite and positive (0, negative, NaN, infinite) fails `ThemeMode::into_resolved`, `Theme::resolve_with` and the OS-theme pipeline once, with `ResolutionInvalid` on the path `context.font_dpi`, before any point size is converted; it was reported as every point-sized font of the theme (`defaults.font.size` and the widgets' fonts), and a theme in pixels passed with it.
- **native-theme**: on Windows, dropping the `ThemeSubscription` of `watch::on_theme_change` right after it returned no longer hangs: the watcher thread creates its message queue before it reports that it started, so the `WM_QUIT` the drop posts arrives; it was lost when it came first, and the drop waited for the thread forever. `on_theme_change` returns `Error::ReaderFailed` (reader `windows-watcher`) when COM, `UISettings` or one of its subscriptions (`ColorValuesChanged`, `TextScaleFactorChanged`) fails to set up; it returned `Ok` with a watcher that never fired. The watcher releases its WinRT objects before `CoUninitialize`.
- **native-theme**: on macOS, `watch::on_theme_change` delivers appearance changes: its watcher thread ran a run loop of its own, which a distributed notification never reaches (Apple delivers them through the process's main run loop), so the callback never fired, and dropping the `ThemeSubscription` right after it returned could hang forever. The observer now has no thread: the change arrives through the application's main run loop, which must be running in a common mode (every GUI toolkit's event loop is), the callback runs on a private serial queue, never on the main thread and never twice at once, and dropping the subscription removes the observer.
- **native-theme**: a malformed freedesktop icon no longer panics or aborts the process: a `process-working` sprite sheet whose viewBox is not finite or narrower than 1 (`viewBox="0 0 0 0.005"` panicked with "capacity overflow") is a single-frame spinner, and one whose frames would take more than 64 MiB (`viewBox="0 0 1 1000000000"`, a billion copies of the file) too; a PNG whose header declares a decoded or RGBA image over 64 MiB (a few bytes claiming 1 000 000 × 1 000 000 pixels aborted on a 4 TB allocation) is `None`, and a decode buffer that cannot be allocated is `None` instead of an abort. 64 MiB is the png crate's default memory limit, a 4096 × 4096 RGBA image.
- **native-theme-build**: an SVG that several roles map to (`play` and `resume` both `"play_arrow"`) counts once in `GenerateOutput::svg_count()` and `total_svg_bytes()`, and is listed once in `rerun_paths()`; it counted once per role.
- **native-theme-build**: `GenerateOutput::emit_cargo_directives()` prints the `cargo::warning` lines before it writes the generated file, as its documentation says, so a failed write no longer loses them.
- **native-theme-build**: a DE-aware mapping takes the keys `hyprland`, `sway`, `river`, `niri`, `wayfire` and `cosmic` (`LinuxDesktop::CosmicDe`), the desktops native-theme detects beside the seven it took; such a key was warned about as "will never be used at runtime" and its arm dropped.
- **native-theme-build**: the generated file has no `extern crate native_theme;`, so two generated files `include!`d into one module compile (the second was E0259, "the name `native_theme` is defined multiple times"); a renamed dependency is named with `IconGenerator::crate_path`.
- **native-theme-build**: `icon_name` and `icon_svg` return `::std::option::Option`, so an enum named `option` (`pub enum Option`) compiles, on every edition, and a role or enum name whose PascalCase has a character other than an ASCII letter, digit or `_` (`"x²"`, which heck keeps) is an `InvalidIdentifier` rather than generated code that does not compile.
- **native-theme-build**: `.derive("core::hash::Hash")` or `.derive("std::fmt::Debug")` is deduplicated against the base derives by its last path segment, as the documentation says; the enum derived the trait twice (E0119).
- **native-theme-build**: the enum name is checked once, the one the generated enum takes (the `.enum_name()` override, else the first file's `name`), so `.enum_name("AppIcon")` builds a file whose unused `name = "3d-icons"` failed the build, as did an invalid `name` in a second file that only drew a warning.
- **native-theme-build**: one mistake gives one error: an unknown theme (`bundled-themes = ["materal"]`) gave the error twice, the second time without its file, and a "theme directory not found" error; an invalid role and a theme in both lists of one file were reported twice, and the overlapping theme's mapping errors twice too. The checks of the merged configs report only what crosses files: a theme one file bundles and another loads from the system, a PascalCase collision between roles of two files. `DuplicateTheme` reads "appears more than once in bundled-themes of icons.toml" (it had a double "in").
- **native-theme-build**: a TOML error in a master or mapping file is one `cargo::error=` line that carries its cause, "failed to parse icons.toml: line 3, column 9: invalid type: string \"a\", expected a sequence"; the directive held only toml's header line ("TOML parse error at line 3, column 9"), and the cause landed on plain stdout lines cargo does not show as the error.
- **native-theme-build**: a mapping value of the wrong type (`play = 5`, `play = ["a"]`, `play = { kde = 1, default = "x" }`) is reported as "expected an icon name string or an inline table of desktop = icon name pairs with a `default` key"; it was "data did not match any variant of untagged enum MappingValue".
- **native-theme-build**: the base directory in a generated `include_bytes!` path is escaped, so a directory name with a `"` no longer breaks the literal; its `\` become `/` on Windows only, where they are separators (on Unix a `\` is part of a name and was rewritten into a path that does not exist), and a base directory that is not valid UTF-8 is an `IoOther` error rather than a lossy path to a file that does not exist.
- **native-theme-build**: an SVG symlink with a relative target outside its theme directory (`material/play.svg -> ../shared/play.svg`) is warned about; the check compared the joined path, whose `..` `Path::starts_with` keeps, so only an absolute target was caught. The link and the theme directory are now compared resolved (`fs::canonicalize`), a dangling link as written.
- **native-theme-build**: a relative base directory whose name looks absolute to the string test (`\x`, `a:` on Unix) is resolved against `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` in a generated `include_bytes!`; the test read the first characters, and `Path::is_absolute`, the host's rules that rustc follows, now decides.
- **native-theme-build**: `generate_icons(path)` is `IconGenerator::new().source(path).generate()`, so an absolute path no longer needs `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, and a directory is reported as "is a directory; expected a TOML file", as the builder reports it.
- **native-theme-build**: the generated `icon_name` of a DE-aware mapping picks its name by the desktop native-theme's platform readers use, `detect::system().refined_linux_desktop_sync()`, whose cache `detect::invalidate_caches()` clears; it kept its own `OnceLock` of the raw `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` that nothing could clear, and on an unrecognised desktop that native-theme takes as KDE (by the portal or `kdeglobals`) it gave the default name where native-theme read KDE's icon theme.
- **native-theme-build**: the published crate's tests compile and pass on their own, as a distribution packager runs them: native-theme is a dev-dependency with a version, which `cargo package` kept (a path-only one it dropped, so the unit tests did not compile), the `LinuxDesktop` drift tests read native-theme's `detect.rs` at run time (it was `include_str!`ed from the sibling crate, which the package does not hold) and skip without it, and so do the generated-code compile checks without the sibling native-theme.
- **native-theme**: the KDE reader reads `kdeglobals` as KConfig cascades it: `kdeglobals` in each `$XDG_CONFIG_DIRS` entry (`/etc/xdg` by default), then `kdedefaults/kdeglobals`, then the user's file, later files overriding earlier keys, so a colour scheme, icon theme or other key Plasma 6 writes only to `kdedefaults` reaches the theme (`SystemTheme::name` was "KDE" on such a desktop). A missing `kdeglobals`, a byte that is not UTF-8 and a line KConfig skips (a `[Group` header without `]`, an entry with an empty key) no longer fail `SystemTheme::from_system()`; a `#` or `;` inside a value (`ColorScheme=My#Scheme`) is kept, as KConfig has only whole-line `#` comments. `detect::system_is_dark()` reads the same cascade, on KDE and in its fallback on other desktops, and goes on to its next source (gsettings, then `gtk-3.0/settings.ini`) when no kdeglobals file states a `[Colors:Window] BackgroundNormal` it can parse.
- **native-theme**: a KDE font whose Qt 6 weight lies outside CSS 100–900 (`font=Noto Sans,10,-1,5,950,…`) gets its weight clamped to that range, after Qt's own clamp to 1–1000 (so 0 is 100, and 70000, which the cast wrapped to 4464, is 900), and a weight field that is not an integer leaves the weight to the preset while the family and size stay; such a font failed `SystemTheme::from_system()` with `ResolutionInvalid`. A Qt 5 weight converts as Qt's own `qt_legacyToOpenTypeWeight` does, to the nearest Qt 5 constant: Black (87) is 900 (it was 800), ExtraBold (81) 800 (it was 700) and ExtraLight (12) 200 (it was 100), and the values between constants move with them. A font saved with a pixel size (`Noto Sans,-1,13,…`) is read as 13 px, where the preset's font replaced it, a pixel-sized body font's headings in pixels too, and Qt's style field gives the font's style, italic or oblique, the title-bar font's too.
- **native-theme**: KDE's `forceFontDPI` is read as Plasma declares it, a whole number with 0 for unset, and on an X11 session only, where Plasma 6.7 reads it: `forceFontDPI=inf` failed `SystemTheme::from_system()`, and a key left from an X11 session scaled the fonts of a Wayland session, which Plasma does not.
- **native-theme**: KDE's `forceFontDPI` is read from `kcmfonts` alone, the file Plasma's fonts KCM writes it to and krdb reads it from, a name without `rc`: the KDE reader and the font DPI detection took a `forceFontDPI` in `kdeglobals` first, where Plasma never writes one, then read `kcmfontsrc`, a file Plasma never writes, which the theme watcher watched in place of `kcmfonts`.
- **native-theme**: the KDE reader's icon sizes are the icon theme's `[Icon Theme]` `SmallDefault`, `MainToolbarDefault`, `DesktopDefault`, `DialogDefault` and `PanelDefault`, the sizes KIconLoader uses, with KIconLoader's own fallback for a key the theme omits (Desktop 32, MainToolbar 22, Small 16, Panel 48, Dialog 32): on Breeze it reported small 12 and large 32, the smallest directories the theme lists, where Breeze states 16 and 48. The theme's `index.theme` is looked up where KIconLoader looks, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/icons` (any absolute `$XDG_DATA_HOME`) before each `$XDG_DATA_DIRS` entry's `icons`, so a user's copy of a theme wins over the system's, then `~/.icons`; the data dirs are searched without a home directory too, and a theme name that is not one directory (`../x`) is not looked up.
- **native-theme**: the GNOME reader parses `font-name`, `monospace-font-name` and `titlebar-font` as Pango parses a font description: style, weight, stretch and variant words after the family (`Noto Sans Bold Italic 11`, any case, with or without the hyphen) leave the family and give the weight and style (italic or oblique), Pango's Semi-Light (350), Book (380) and Ultra-Black (900) included; a `px` size (`Cantarell 15px`) is read in pixels; trailing `@variations` and `#features` are skipped; of a family list (`Cantarell, Sans 11`) the first family is taken. Such strings kept the style words in the family or dropped the font. A size of `NaN` or `inf` drops only that font, which keeps the preset's; it failed `SystemTheme::from_system()` with `ResolutionInvalid`.
- **native-theme**: the xdg-desktop-portal reads have a deadline: the GNOME reader's portal calls, the KDE-with-portal reader's accent read and the portal-backend probe on an unrecognised desktop each finish within 2 s, the bound each `gsettings` read already has, and proceed as if no portal answered when the time is up. A hung or slow-starting xdg-desktop-portal blocked `SystemTheme::from_system()` without limit, as zbus sets no method timeout. On a desktop `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` does not name, a running KDE or GNOME portal backend picks the reader before an installed one, and when both a KDE and a GNOME backend are running (or, none running, both installed) the `kdeglobals` check, or Adwaita, decides; it took whichever installed backend the bus listed first.
- **native-theme**: on GNOME, when xdg-desktop-portal gives no colour scheme (no portal, or one that does not answer or lacks the setting), the GNOME reader takes dark or light from `gsettings` `color-scheme`, through the same function `detect::system_is_dark` uses, so the two agree; it returned the light theme while `system_is_dark` said dark. Its `gsettings` reads finish within 2 s together, after the portal's own 2 s (each had 2 s, up to 18 s for nine keys), and `icon-theme` is read once per theme read, not twice.
- **native-theme**: on a Linux desktop `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` does not name (unset, as for an app started from a systemd unit or over ssh, or a value such as `i3`), the theme watcher, the icon-theme detection, the font DPI and `detect::system_is_dark()` follow the desktop `SystemTheme::from_system()` reads by: its portal backend's, else KDE where `kdeglobals` exists. `watch::on_theme_change` returned `WatchUnavailable` there although `from_system` read KDE or GNOME; `detect::system_icon_theme()` took KDE's icon theme before GNOME's even where the GNOME reader ran, so it, an unthemed `FreedesktopLoader` and the `IconSetChoice::System` label could name another theme than the GNOME reader's; and `ResolutionContext::from_system()` (the preset path) took the font DPI from `Xft.dpi` where the GNOME reader gives 96, so text was scaled twice. On KDE reached that way `system_is_dark()` reads `kdeglobals` first, as the KDE reader does, so it agrees with `SystemTheme::mode`. These synchronous functions (`on_theme_change`, the icon theme, the font DPI, `system_is_dark`, `pipeline::platform_preset_name()`) cannot ask the portal and use the desktop the last system read refined; before one, only the `kdeglobals` check, and a system read whose portal answer names another desktop clears the font DPI, icon theme and dark mode cached from it. On KDE (with the `kde` feature) the icon theme `detect` reports is the KDE reader's, read from the same KConfig cascade (`/etc/xdg/kdeglobals` included).
- **native-theme**: a detection cache (`system_is_dark()`, `prefers_reduced_motion()`, `accessibility_preferences()`, `system_icon_theme()`, the Linux desktop, the font DPI) no longer keeps a value read before an invalidation that lands while it is read: a `SystemTheme::from_system()` or a cached read that began before the user changed a setting and ended after a watcher's `detect::invalidate_caches()` cached the old value, which every later read returned until the next invalidation. Each field counts its invalidations, and a read stores its value only if none landed since it began.
- **native-theme**: every command the detection runs is bounded by the same 2 s: the icon-theme detection's `gsettings` and `xfconf-query`, `fc-match` for `fonts::substitute_family`, macOS `defaults` without the `macos` feature and `gsettings --version` in `diagnose_platform_support()` waited as long as the command ran (`xrdb` and `xrandr` were bounded already); an icon-theme error names a command that timed out ("timed out after 2s"), and `diagnose_platform_support()` reports a `gsettings` that timed out or could not be run for a reason other than not being found as `ToolError`, not `ToolMissing`. A command's output is read while it runs, so an `xrdb -query` that prints more than a pipe holds (64 KiB on Linux) is no longer killed at 2 s and read as no `Xft.dpi`, and a process a command leaves running with its output no longer holds the call past 2 s.
- **native-theme**: the user's config directory is `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` only when it is an absolute path, else `$HOME/.config`, as the XDG Base Directory Specification says, for every file read from it (`gtk-3.0/settings.ini` for dark mode, `kdeglobals`, `kcmfonts`, `lxqt/lxqt.conf`) and for the KDE watcher: an empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` made the dark-mode fallback read `/gtk-3.0/settings.ini`, and a relative one was read from the working directory. Without either, no user file is read: the KDE reader no longer took `/etc/xdg/kdeglobals` as the user's file and `/etc/xdg/kdedefaults` as its defaults, the KDE watcher fails with `ReaderFailed`, and `diagnose_platform_support()` reports `HOME` as not set.
- **native-theme**: reduced motion has one read per platform, the one its theme reader makes: `detect::prefers_reduced_motion()` and `detect_reduced_motion()` read `kdeglobals`' `[KDE] AnimationDurationFactor` on KDE (with the `kde` feature), the portal's `reduced-motion` then `gsettings` `enable-animations` on GNOME and Budgie, `NSWorkspace` on macOS and `SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION` on Windows.
