They asked GNOME's `enable-animations` on every Linux desktop, which on KDE can be absent or out of step with Plasma's animation speed, and `UISettings.AnimationsEnabled` on Windows. `AccessibilityPreferences::from_system()` and `detect::accessibility_preferences()` no longer add that second answer to the reader's, so they agree with `SystemTheme::from_system().accessibility`; where no reader runs or it fails, `AccessibilityPreferences::from_system()` takes `reduce_motion` from that read, as `SystemTheme::from_system()` now does on a Linux desktop without a reader (it was `false` there), and a system read whose portal answer names another desktop clears the cached reduced motion as it clears dark mode.
- **native-theme**: the theme watchers: on KDE and GNOME, `watch::on_theme_change` returns `Error::ReaderFailed` (reader `kde-watcher`, `gnome-watcher`) when the inotify watch of the config directory, or the session bus connection, the portal proxy or the `SettingChanged` subscription, fails to set up; it returned `Ok` with a watcher that never fired. A callback that panics loses that one event and the watcher keeps running, on every platform: on Windows the panic aborted the process, on Linux it ended the watcher thread silently, and on macOS it unwound through Foundation. The KDE watcher fires once per burst of writes, 300 ms after the last, so the consumer re-reads after KDE's last write; it fired on the first and dropped the rest. Dropping a KDE subscription while files in the config directory keep changing returns within 200 ms; it waited for a 200 ms gap with no event. On macOS, dropping the subscription waits for a callback already queued, so none runs after the drop returns (except for a drop from inside the callback). A callback that drops its own subscription no longer panics in `JoinHandle::join` (a thread joining itself, EDEADLK): the drop detaches the watcher thread, which exits once the callback returns.
- **native-theme**: `watch::on_theme_change` fires on the changes of what the platform's reader reads; it missed several. On GNOME and Budgie it fires on the portal's `SettingChanged` for the `org.gnome.desktop.interface` keys the reader reads (fonts, text scale, animations, overlay scrolling, icon theme, colour scheme), the title bar font in `org.gnome.desktop.wm.preferences` and high contrast in `org.gnome.desktop.a11y.interface`, which the portal's GNOME and GTK backends forward under their schema names; it listened to `org.freedesktop.appearance` alone (of which it fires on the four settings the reader reads). On KDE it watches `kdedefaults/kdeglobals`, which the reader reads beneath the user's `kdeglobals`, from the moment Plasma creates the directory. On macOS it observes `NSSystemColorsDidChangeNotification` (the accent and highlight colours), `NSPreferredScrollerStyleDidChangeNotification` (overlay scroll bars) and, on `NSWorkspace`'s center, `NSWorkspaceAccessibilityDisplayOptionsDidChangeNotification` (reduce motion, increase contrast, reduce transparency), and the appearance notification is delivered immediately while the application is inactive, which `NSApplication` suspended until it was active again. On Windows it subscribes to `UISettings::TextScaleFactorChanged` and, from Windows 10 2004, `UISettings::AnimationsEnabledChanged` (client-area animation, which `UISettings.AnimationsEnabled` read the same as on the three Windows probe images) too, and the callback runs on the watcher thread; the non-client fonts, high contrast and the high-contrast `GetSysColor` colours are still not watched (only a top-level window receives `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` and `WM_SYSCOLORCHANGE`, and `AccessibilitySettings::HighContrastChanged`, which the watcher subscribes to where Windows accepts it, does not fire outside UWP). One change that raises several notifications fires the callback once, 300 ms after the last, on every platform (it was KDE only). Each reader module lists its sources, and a test checks that each has a watched notification.
- **native-theme**: the Windows reader leaves a non-client font's weight to the preset when `lfWeight` is 0 (`FW_DONTCARE`, the default weight), which it reported as 100 (Thin); it reads the tooltip font from `lfStatusFont`, "the font used in status bars and tooltips" (NONCLIENTMETRICSW), as it does the status bar's; and it reports `reduce_transparency` when Windows' *Transparency effects* setting is off (`UISettings.AdvancedEffectsEnabled` false), where it always reported `false`. `watch::on_theme_change` fires when that setting changes (`UISettings::AdvancedEffectsEnabledChanged`).
- **native-theme**: on macOS the scroll bar's groove width follows the user's scroller style: the reader writes the width AppKit gives the style `NSScroller.preferredScrollerStyle` reports (`scrollerWidth(for: .regular, scrollerStyle:)`: 15 for the legacy style and 16 for the overlay style on macOS 14 and 15, 17 for both on macOS 26), where it stated the legacy style's 15 under either style.
- **native-theme**: a freedesktop icon size from 32768 up no longer panics in a debug build: freedesktop-icons computes with the size as an `i16`, so 32768 wrapped to -32768 and overflowed its directory distance ("attempt to subtract with overflow"; in a release build it picked a directory by the wrapped size). The loader's size is at most 512 now (`MAX_ICON_SIZE`), and the lookup itself takes a size above 32767 as 32767.
- **native-theme**: a GTK-convention theme's `process-working-symbolic` spinner has its foreground placeholders (`#2e3436` and the like) turned into `currentColor`, as every other symbolic icon of the theme has; it kept them, so the connectors, which colour `currentColor`, drew it dark grey on a dark theme.
- **native-theme**: `icons::is_freedesktop_theme_available`, `icons::list_freedesktop_themes` and the freedesktop icon lookup agree on the installed themes: a theme whose first `index.theme`, in the Icon Theme Specification's order (`~/.icons`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/icons`, then each `$XDG_DATA_DIRS` entry's `icons`), lists `Directories` in its `[Icon Theme]` group, read once per process at the first call of any of them. `is_freedesktop_theme_available` accepted any `index.theme`, so a cursor theme (`breeze_cursors`) or `default`, which only inherits Adwaita, was available and `default_icon_choice` took it as the default (an icon is no longer looked up in either, as in any theme that is not installed); a theme installed while the process ran was listed and available but loaded no icon until a restart; and `list_freedesktop_themes` missed a theme that writes `Directories = …`. A theme installed both for the user and system-wide is read from the user's `index.theme` (the theme chain merged both, the system's `Inherits=` first); freedesktop-icons still takes each icon from the system copy where both copies have it.
- **native-theme**: `icons::default_icon_choice` takes an SF Symbols theme as the default only on macOS and a Segoe Fluent one only on Windows, each with the `system-icons` feature, where its loader is compiled; elsewhere it returns `IconSetChoice::System`, as for a freedesktop theme that is not installed (every icon loaded from such a default was `None`).
- **native-theme**: a Segoe Fluent glyph icon is its font's `size × size` em box with the glyph where the font places it (its black box at `gmptGlyphOrigin`, measured from the em box's top, the font's ascent less its internal leading), so every glyph of one size has the same raster and they line up; each came back at the size of its own ink ("Remove", a bar, was a few pixels high). A Segoe stock icon whose device context or `GetDIBits` fails is `None`, not a transparent icon, and the Segoe and SF Symbols loaders return `None` for an image whose pixels exceed the 64 MiB icon decode bound (an SF Symbol has twice its points in pixels on a 2× display). Segoe stock icons load one at a time: of the `SIID_WARNING` loads that ran at once on a Windows CI image, one returned `None` and another the icon.
- **native-theme**: `rasterize_svg` centres an SVG whose aspect ratio is not 1:1 at the offset it computes; the offset was scaled with the icon, so a 10 × 5 SVG rasterised at 100 landed below the pixmap and came out fully transparent. Its buffer is allocated fallibly, at a size clamped to 512 (see *Breaking Changes*); `rasterize_svg(svg, 100_000)` aborted the process on a 40 GB allocation. An `<image>` is drawn only from an inline `data:` URL: an `href` naming a file was read from disk, relative to the process's working directory. Its errors are `ReaderFailed` with reader `svg-rasterizer`, of kind `Platform`, as its documentation now states.
- **native-theme**: every `Error::ReaderFailed` reader name is lowercase kebab-case (`"preset-cache"` was `"preset_cache"`, `"svg-rasterizer"` was `"svg_rasterizer"`).
- **native-theme**: a range violation of a font's `size_pt` reports the stated points (`size_pt = -3` prints `got -3`, not the px conversion).
- **native-theme**: the README's icon and watch examples are compiled by the README doctest.
- **native-theme-iced**: the iced theme's light/dark flag (`Extended::is_dark`, which iced takes for the window's title bar) follows the theme's mode, where it was iced's guess from the background's lightness (OKLCH L < 0.6): `from_preset` takes the mode of the variant it resolved (`Resolved::mode`, so a light-only preset asked for dark is light), and `from_system()` and `SystemThemeExt::to_iced_theme` take the OS mode (`SystemTheme::mode`). `to_theme` keeps iced's lightness guess, since a `ResolvedTheme` carries no mode.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `from_preset` builds the gpui theme in the mode of the variant it resolved (`Resolved::mode`), so a light-only theme asked for dark gives a light theme; it built it in the mode asked for. Every bundled preset has both variants, so none of them changes. `to_theme`'s docs name the theme's mode (`Resolved::mode`, `SystemTheme::mode`) as its `is_dark`, where they pointed at the background-lightness guess `is_dark_resolved`.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::expander` reads `expander.frame_enabled`: where the theme states it, framed or not, the header draws no outline of its own (its width is 0), so a kde-breeze header no longer wears a 1 px `#bcc0bf` frame KDE does not draw, and an adwaita or windows-11 one no longer a frame of its own inside `styles::expander_frame`'s. A disabled header's label and outline are faded by `defaults.disabled_opacity`, as the link's are (0.5 on adwaita); it looked enabled.
- **native-theme-iced**: a hover the theme leaves unset is the widget at rest in `styles::pick_list`, `styles::toggler` (both tracks), `styles::checkbox`, `styles::radio` and `styles::segment`, where they composited the fill under it over itself, so a translucent drop-down field or switch track came out at about twice its alpha under the pointer, and a hovered unchecked box or unselected radio with `checkbox.unchecked_background` stated would have taken `checkbox.background_color` as its hover.
- **native-theme-iced**: a `Spinner` on a freedesktop icon theme whose spinner is only `process-working-symbolic`, as GNOME's is, is drawn in `defaults.text_color`; its `currentColor` was left unset, which iced's SVG renderer draws black, so it was black on a dark theme.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::spin_rotation_radians` and the `Spinner`'s arc stay exact however long the animation has run: they took the remainder of the turn after converting the elapsed time to `f32`, and an `f32` of the milliseconds steps by 128 ms twelve days in, one of the seconds by 2 s from 2^24 s, so the rotation moved in steps after about a day, and the arc stood still from 2^24 s (194 days). At 14 days and 50 ms a 1000 ms turn is now at 0.1π, where it was 0.
- **native-theme-iced**: `icons::custom_icon_to_svg_handle` given a colour leaves a full-colour freedesktop icon in its own colours: the loaded icon went through the monochrome colouriser a second time, which painted every `currentColor` in it in the colour (a Breeze `folder`'s body, `fill:currentColor` in the accent its stylesheet states). Both `custom_icon_to_*_handle` functions load through `native_theme::icons::load_custom_icon_in`, which colours a freedesktop icon only where its foreground is left to the caller (a symbolic icon's placeholders, a `currentColor` the icon sets no `color` for).
- **native-theme-iced**: a freedesktop indicator's frames keep the colour their stylesheet gives `currentColor` through `icons::animated_frames_to_svg_handles_for(IconSet::Freedesktop, &anim, Some(color))`: `animated_frames_to_svg_handles(&anim, Some(color))`, iced's only frame conversion, put every frame through the monochrome colouriser, which painted Breeze's `process-working`, under `.ColorScheme-Text { color:#232629 }`, in the colour, and with `None` left a symbolic sprite's `currentColor` unset, which iced's SVG renderer draws black.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` given a colour leaves a freedesktop icon the loader does not colour in its own colours, as iced's `custom_icon_to_*_handle` do: the loaded icon went through the monochrome colouriser a second time, which painted every `currentColor` in it in the colour asked for, so a full-colour icon lost its palette (a Breeze `folder`'s body, `fill:currentColor` in the accent its stylesheet states) and an icon whose own stylesheet sets the `color` its `currentColor` takes (a Breeze `current-color-scheme`, symbolic ones too) lost that colour. It loads through `native_theme::icons::load_custom_icon_in`, which colours a freedesktop icon only where its foreground is left to the caller (a symbolic icon's placeholders, a `currentColor` the icon sets no `color` for), and converts with no colour afterwards; recolouring Breeze's stylesheet from the theme, as KIconLoader does, is an open item.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source`'s `size` reaches the system icon loaders: a freedesktop icon is looked up at it, so a PNG-only icon comes from the theme's directory nearest the size, an SF Symbol is configured at it in points and a Segoe glyph drawn at it in pixels. They were loaded at their defaults (24 px, 24 pt, 32 px), and gpui scaled the small raster up to the element. The size is clamped to `1..=512` first, as for an SVG, so a 0 or a huge size cannot make a loadable icon `None`.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `apply`, `apply_system_theme` and `apply_accessibility` keep the application's own gpui-component settings `Theme::notification`, `list`, `sheet` and `motion`, as upstream's `Theme::change` does: they replaced the whole theme, so a `sheet.margin_top`, notification placement or motion duration the application had set went back to upstream's default at every theme switch.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a one-variant `apply` drops the other mode's variant that another theme stored (each stored variant keeps the display name of the theme that stored it), so after `apply_system_theme`, or an `apply` of another theme in the other mode, an upstream `Theme::change` or `sync_system_appearance` to that mode no longer shows the earlier theme's palette, scrollbars, tooltip delay and `geometry` values under the new theme's name; it shows upstream's built-in light palette, as `apply`'s docs say. Two `apply`s of one theme, one per mode, still store both; the docs also say what a one-variant light `apply` leaves after a change to dark (upstream's empty light config replaces the theme's until the next `apply` or `apply_accessibility`), which applying both modes, or `apply_system_theme`, avoids. `apply_accessibility` rebuilds from the stored variant under that variant's theme name.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the scrollbar thumb is drawn at least `scrollbar.min_thumb_length` long: gpui-base takes the inset off both ends of the clamped length, so the drawn minimum was `min_thumb_length − 2 × inset` — 7 px where kde-breeze states 20, 6 px where windows-11 states 17. `base_layer::scrollbar_styles` passes the minimum plus twice the inset.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::tooltip`, `status_bar`, `dialog_title`, `dialog_description`, `table`, `title_bar`, `toolbar` and `link` set the platform's `defaults.line_height` with their text size: they left the text at gpui's default line height (the golden ratio, where upstream sets none) or, on a dialog title, upstream's 1.25, so a tooltip's wrapped lines on windows-11 were 1.618 em apart instead of 1.43. The control-height builders, `button_label` and `text_area` already set it.
- **native-theme-gpui**: above a text-scaling factor of 1, `geometry::data_table_size` and `geometry::list_header_height` grow a `DataTable` row and header with their text, by the control-height rule: at least the scaled `list.item_font` line box at `defaults.line_height` (the header's text is drawn in it too; `list.header_font` gives the header only its colour), upstream's own cell padding and the row's 1 px bottom border, which upstream draws inside the row. They kept the stated height, which upstream sets verbatim and clips each cell to, so at factor 2 the cell text ran out of its row under every native preset (macos-tahoe: a 31 px line in a 24 px row). At 1 or less the stated heights stand.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Icons page's freedesktop icons, and the chrome's icons taken from them, load from the icon theme in use under whichever of the icon's names that theme has (`freedesktop_name_candidates`), looked up in that one theme with `FreedesktopLoader::any`, so the theme's own icon wins over its parent's: it took the running desktop's name, so on a non-GTK desktop with a GTK-convention theme, or on KDE with the adwaita preset's `Adwaita`, most of the 50 icons the two conventions name apart showed as not found.
- **Showcases (egui, iced)**: the side-panel toggle's freedesktop icon loads from the icon theme the chrome chose under whichever of its names that theme has (`sidebar-expand-left`, `sidebar-show`), looked up in that one theme with `FreedesktopLoader::any`, as the gpui showcase loads it: the name was picked by the desktop, so on KDE with the adwaita preset's `Adwaita`, on Sway, Hyprland or an unknown desktop it asked Adwaita for Breeze's `sidebar-expand-left`, which Adwaita lacks, and the toggle showed its label.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a widgets `Spinner` on the system's icon set draws the system icon theme's indicator after that theme changes (Breeze to Papirus, say), once `native_theme::detect::invalidate_caches()` has cleared the detection and the theme is installed again (`apply_system_theme`), as the runtime theme change recipe does: its cache keyed a freedesktop indicator with no theme named by no theme, so it found the first theme's indicator for the life of the application unless the colour or size changed too. The key names the theme `system_icon_theme()` detects, asked once per install of the native theme rather than every frame the spinner turns. Sizes above 512 px, the largest an indicator frame is rasterised at, share one cache entry.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a hover or pressed layer is composited over the fill it covers source-over, so a translucent layer over a translucent fill covers both: `Hsla::blend`, which the button states and the widgets' hover states used, kept the fill's alpha. Over an opaque fill or under an opaque layer nothing changes. A pressed or hovered button (`button_active`, `button_secondary_active` and their hovers) over a translucent fill now covers more than the idle fill, where it showed the idle fill's alpha; material-3's unchecked checkbox shows its hover state layer, which a transparent box hid; windows-11's checkbox hover is laid over its translucent unchecked fill as a layer, where it showed the fill.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a disabled `widgets::Slider` fades as one control over the colour under it, as the switch, the check box and the radio button do: on a platform that dims by opacity (adwaita at `slider.disabled_opacity` 0.5, ios-13 at 0.3) gpui faded the rail, the fill, the thumb's outline and the thumb each on its own, so the rail showed through the fill and both through the thumb. `SliderLook::faded_over(ground)` composites the rail over the ground, the fill over the rail and the outline and the thumb over the fill, each faded by the opacity over the ground, and `Slider::backdrop` names the ground (the window's `defaults.background_color` where not given).
- **native-theme-gpui**: `widgets::Slider` over a range `SliderState` (`default_value(20.0..80.0)`) draws a thumb at the range's start and the fill from it to the end thumb, as gpui-component's slider does; it drew one thumb at the end and the fill from the rail's start, while a press nearer the start moved the start, which no thumb showed. Left, Right, Home and End move the range's end and keep its start (the end stops at the start, as a pointer keeps it); they turned the range into a single value.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a disabled `widgets::Slider` drawn from the theme reports itself disabled to assistive technology, as gpui-component's slider does; it reported a slider that takes input. It also registers for gpui-kit's test API under the id gpui-component's slider registers, `("slider", state.entity_id())`, so an application's test finds it by id, label or role (`find`, `find_by_label`, `find_all_by_role(Role::Slider)`) under a native theme as without one, where it found nothing.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the labels of `widgets::Checkbox` and `widgets::Radio` are drawn in `checkbox.font`'s family and style (normal, italic or oblique), as they were in its size and weight: they took the window's family, `defaults.font.family`, upright, so a theme that states a family or a slanted style for the checkbox did not reach gpui. Every bundled preset's checkbox font is its default font, so their labels look as before.
- **native-theme-gpui**: each tab label of `widgets::TabBar` is drawn in `tab.font`'s family and style (normal, italic or oblique), as it was in its size and weight: it took the window's family, `defaults.font.family`, upright, so a theme that states a family or a slanted style for its tabs did not reach gpui. No bundled preset states a tab family or style apart from its default font, so their tabs look as before.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the `geometry` builders that carry a font (`menu_item`, `toolbar`, `title_bar`, `input`, `text_area`, `select`, `combobox`, `checkbox`, `radio`, `list_item`, `table`, `tooltip`, `status_bar`, `dialog_title`, `dialog_description`, `button_label`, `toggle`, `link`) carry its family and style (normal, italic or oblique) as well as its size and weight, the family through `font_family`, as the installed theme's own: they left the window's family, `defaults.font.family`, upright, so the family and the italic flag the KDE reader states for `menu.font` (`[General] menuFont`), `toolbar.font` (`toolBarFont`) and `window.title_bar_font` (`[WM] activeFont`) did not reach gpui. No bundled preset states a widget family or style apart from its default font, so they look as before.
- **native-theme-gpui**: an unchecked `widgets::Checkbox` or `widgets::Radio` under a theme that states `checkbox.unchecked_background` and no `hover_background` stays at rest under the pointer, as iced's does (`styles::checkbox`) and as the switch and the slider do: the hover laid `checkbox.background_color` over the box, a different fill. Every bundled preset states `hover_background` but macos-tahoe, which states no unchecked fill either, so the bundled presets' controls look as before.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the style given to a `widgets::RadioGroup` is the group's own, as gpui-component's `RadioGroup` refines it onto its root: it went to a box inside the group, so a layout style such as `flex_1()`, `w_full()` or an absolute position did not reach the element the group's parent lays out, and the group neither grew nor moved.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Icons page draws a freedesktop theme's animated indicator through `animated_frames_to_image_sources_for`, as the widgets' `Spinner` draws it: it painted every `currentColor` of Breeze's `process-working` in the foreground, over the `#232629` its stylesheet sets, so the page and the widgets' `Spinner` drew one indicator in two colours. Its Widget Info says which parts take the foreground, and the Feedback page's spinner panel claims `defaults.text_color` for a freedesktop indicator that spins, which gpui draws as a mask in that one colour, where it said the icon kept its own colours.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::button_primary` hovers and presses from its own fill, `button.primary_background`: iced's derivation of its primary pair (`Primary::generate`, the `strong` fill hovered, the `base` fill pressed) is applied to that fill, with `button.primary_text_color` as the label, where `button::primary` derived both from the palette's primary slot, `defaults.accent_color`. Where a theme states a primary fill that is not its accent, as the Windows reader does (`AccentFillColorDefault`), a hovered primary button was the strengthened accent, a different colour from the button at rest. Apart from windows-11, which now states that fill too (see its line above), the bundled presets' primary fill is the accent and their primary buttons look as before; windows-11's hover and pressed fills follow its new `#0067c0` / `#4cc2ff`.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::text_editor` frames the multi-line field in `text_area.border`, its colour, width and corner radius, as `text_area_padding` already reads that border's padding; it took `input.border`, so a theme that frames its text area apart from its input did not reach iced. The hovered and focused outline colours stay `input.*`'s. Every bundled preset's text area inherits the input's border, so their editors look as before.
- **native-theme-iced**: `system_font_family` (feature `system-fonts`) gives a theme font whose family the system lacks the family the platform draws in its place, as the gpui and egui connectors draw it: on a system without the stated family it asks `native_theme::fonts::substitute_family_cached` (fontconfig's match on Linux, the font every native application of the system gets; asked once per family, one `fc-match` process with a 2 s timeout) and returns the family of the face `system_face` gives for that, where it returned the stated name, which iced drew in cosmic-text's own fallback. windows-11's "Segoe UI" on a Linux system without it is fontconfig's match for it (Adwaita Sans on one Linux system); a family the system has stays as it was, and so does every family on macOS and Windows, where native-theme asks the platform for no substitute.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `to_theme` scales `font_size` and `mono_font_size`, and the theme switch's config (`Theme::change`) its copies, through `AccessibilityPreferences::scaled`, and `geometry::tooltip_content` takes the rem of a side the platform does not state through `scaled_text_size`, so a finite text-scaling factor whose product with the font size overflows `f32` (above about 2.6e37 for a 13 px font) leaves them as the theme states them; it made the window rem, and with it every rem-relative size in gpui-component, infinite, and a tooltip's text width 0.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `font_family`, and with it `to_theme`, `from_preset` and `from_system`, no longer holds its process-wide cache of family substitutes locked while it asks the platform for one (on Linux an `fc-match` process, with a 2 s timeout): a second thread building a theme waited for the first one's ask, even for a family already answered. The first ask for a family still runs on the calling thread, once per process (about 12.5 ms measured on one Linux machine), as `from_preset`, `to_theme` and `font_family` now document.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::input` and `geometry::text_area` letter a field in `input.font.color`: the `Input` root sets no text colour of its own, so a field's text took the window's `foreground`, which macos-monterey's and macos-tahoe's `input.font.color` differs from in both modes. `geometry::input_fill(n, true)` gives a disabled field `input.disabled_text_color`, which gpui-base still draws at half its alpha as it draws every disabled field's text.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a selected `ListItem` can be lettered in `list.selection_text_color`, with `geometry::list_item_selected` (see *Added*): upstream marks a selected row by its fill alone, so its text stayed the row's colour, dark on kde-breeze's accent where Breeze letters it white. The showcase's List and Tree rows take it in place of the colour they set themselves.
- **native-theme-gpui**: a `Select` or `Combobox` trigger can be filled with `combo_box.background_color`, and a disabled one with `combo_box.disabled_background`, `combo_box.disabled_text_color` and `combo_box.disabled_opacity`, through `geometry::combo_box_fill` (see *Added*): upstream fills a trigger as it fills an `Input`, with `Theme::input_background` or the `input` token faded, which no `ThemeColor` field makes the platform's. The disabled text colour reaches a `Combobox`'s text and not a `Select`'s title, which mutes itself.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::dialog` fills a `Dialog` or `AlertDialog` with `dialog.background_color` and edges it in `dialog.border.color`, which upstream paints with the window's `background` and `border` tokens, and `geometry::dialog_title` letters the title in `dialog.title_font.color`, where upstream sets no colour.
- **native-theme-gpui**: an `Accordion` follows the theme's expander: `geometry::accordion_title` letters an item's title in `expander.font.color`, over the `foreground` upstream sets on an open one; `geometry::accordion` edges the Accordion's card and the line under each item in `expander.border.color`, which upstream draws in the window's `border` token; and `geometry::accordion_hover` fills a title row under the pointer with `expander.hover_background`, where upstream draws no hover (see *Added*).
- **native-theme-gpui**: `ThemeColor::sidebar_border`, the edge of a `Sidebar`, is `sidebar.border.color`, which inherits `defaults.border.color`; it was `window.border.color`. No bundled preset changes: in each the two are the same colour. `geometry::toolbar` letters the row in `toolbar.font.color`, where gpui-component's `Toolbar` sets no colour; and `geometry::link` fills a `Link` with `link.background_color`, transparent in every bundled preset.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the title bar of a window that is not the active one can take `window.inactive_title_bar_background` and `window.inactive_title_bar_text_color`, through `geometry::title_bar_inactive` (see *Added*): gpui-component's `TitleBar` reads no window activation and draws every bar alike. The showcase's title bar takes it while its window is inactive.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::group_box_content` fills a `GroupBox`'s content with `card.background_color`, on every variant and over the Fill variant's `group_box` token: the connector withheld it because most presets inherit it from `defaults.surface_color`, which kept the fills material-3 and adwaita (dark) state from a source off the screen. A preset that states no card fill fills its cards with the inherited `defaults.surface_color`, which is the platform's own card fill under kde-breeze (Kirigami's `Card`, the View set's background), adwaita light (`card_bg_color`, `#ffffff` as the view's) and windows-11 (`CardBackgroundFillColorDefault`, its surface colour); the macOS presets, whose box fill Apple does not publish, and the colour-scheme presets take it too. A Fill box took the connector's `group_box` blend and a Normal or Outline box had no fill.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::popover` edges a `Popover` or `HoverCard` in `popover.border.color` at `popover.border.line_width`, inside the shadow ring gpui-component draws as a popover's edge, which stays: the connector withheld the edge because most presets inherit it from `defaults.border`, which kept the edge adwaita states from libadwaita's outline (`#0000000d`) off the screen. kde-breeze and windows-11 state their platform's edge too (qqc2-desktop-style's `Popup`, WinUI 3's `SurfaceStrokeColorFlyout`); under the other presets the popover has the inherited `defaults.border` edge.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::toolbar` draws the toolbar's bottom edge, `toolbar.border.color` at `toolbar.border.line_width`, inside the bar, where gpui-component's `Toolbar` draws none: the connector withheld it because most presets inherit it from `defaults.border`. A theme that states a width of 0 draws none: adwaita, windows-11 and material-3, whose platforms draw no toolbar edge (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.13). kde-breeze's bar ends in Breeze's 1px tools-area line, in the Header set's separator colour; under the macOS presets, whose title-bar separator the system chooses, and the colour-scheme presets it ends in the inherited `defaults.border` rule, which the showcase's chrome toolbar drew itself.
- **native-theme**: `kde-breeze` states no `expander.hover_background`: KDE's expander, `KCollapsibleGroupBox`, paints its arrow and title and nothing else (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.27), so the `#dee0e2` / `#31363b` hover fill it stated had no source; the field is unset and resolves to none, so a hovered header keeps its rest fill.
- **native-theme-egui**: a huge but finite text size, such as a theme file's `size_px = 30000` (core accepts any finite positive size) or a large text-scaling factor, no longer panics in epaint, which asserts that a glyph fits its font atlas (`texture_atlas.rs:226-231`), at most 16384 px wide and as wide as the GPU's `max_texture_side`. The install plugin lowers every text size of the installed base styles above `ThemeAtlas::text_size_cap()` when `install` writes them, before each pass, and again when a zoom takes effect; the role styles `native_scope`, `native_set_style` and `role_modifier` hand over are lowered the same way.
- **native-theme-egui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` hands the key's size (`IconKey::size`), clamped to `1..=512` as every icon size is, to the SF Symbols and Segoe loaders: an SF Symbol is configured at it in points and a Segoe glyph drawn at it in pixels, where they were loaded at their defaults (24 pt, 32 px) and egui scaled the small raster up to the image. A freedesktop icon was already looked up at it.
- **native-theme-egui**: `icons::custom_icon_to_image_source` loads through `native_theme::icons::load_custom_icon_in`, with the key's icon theme, size and colour, and keys the loaded bytes with no second colouring pass. A provider's own SVG for SF Symbols or Segoe is the icon on every platform, as the function's doc said, where it was `None` wherever the set's loader does not run (SF Symbols on Linux and Windows, Segoe on Linux and macOS), and it is drawn in the tint, else the atlas's text colour, where only a tint coloured it, so it drew black on a dark scheme without one. A freedesktop icon from the icon theme is coloured as before: where its foreground is left to the caller, never where its own stylesheet sets its colour (Breeze's `current-color-scheme`) or it is full-colour. A provider's own SVG under a freedesktop key is now coloured as one for SF Symbols or Segoe is, as a monochrome icon through `colorize_monochrome_svg` (an implicit or black fill too), where only a `currentColor` it set no colour for was filled; give a system set a monochrome SVG.
- **native-theme-egui**: where the integration reports no colour scheme (Linux, under winit 0.30.13), the window title bar follows the UI again after an atlas with an OS mode (`from_system`, `to_egui_atlas`, `Builder::os_mode`) gives way to one without (`from_preset`, `to_theme`) or to `ThemeAtlas::clear`: the install plugin had told the window the OS mode's scheme (`SetTheme(Light)` or `Dark`), which then stayed while egui drew in `Options::fallback_theme`, until the theme preference changed. The plugin now sends egui's own `SetTheme(SystemTheme::SystemDefault)` back once, the command egui believes the window holds.
- **native-theme-egui**: a failed `ThemeWatcher` rebuild requests a repaint, as a successful one does, so an idle application reads `ThemeWatcher::last_error` on the next pass; it was seen only when some other event repainted.
- **native-theme-egui**: `ThemeAtlas::clear` forgets the icons this crate cached with the atlas (`icons::forget_icons`), as installing another atlas does: their `bytes://` URIs stayed in egui's loaders and the stored `TextureHandle`s kept the raster icons' textures alive until a later `install`, which might never come.
- **native-theme-egui**: where the scroll bar is an overlay one with a fully transparent track (macos-monterey, macos-tahoe), a `ProgressBar` drawn outside `Role::ProgressBar` and an application's `Frame::canvas`, a plot's frame among them, have a fill again: egui fills both from the base style's `visuals.extreme_bg_color`, which held that transparent track. It holds `input.background_color` there, egui's own meaning of the slot (a text field's background), and the base style writes the three `spacing.scroll.*_background_opacity` 0, so the overlay groove egui paints from the same slot stays clear. A solid scroll bar keeps its track in the slot, as egui paints its groove from it at full opacity: on windows-11, which states that track transparent, a canvas frame and a role-less progress bar stay unfilled (the README's *Limits*).
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the `Parts` of widgets no longer drawn are let go: every widget kept its parts in the `Context`'s data under its own id, which egui never clears, so each id ever drawn (auto ids in a list whose rows come and go, rows salted by a changing key) left an entry behind for the life of the `Context`. The parts of a viewport's widgets are now kept in one store holding that viewport's last two passes that drew a widget of the crate, and dropped once egui closes the viewport; `Parts::of` answers as before for a widget drawn in either, in whichever viewport drew it, and `None` once both have gone by without it.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a checked `RadioButton` built with `.enabled(false)` is its checked self faded as one over its backdrop where the theme states no `checkbox.disabled_background` (adwaita), the checked fill with its `indicator_color` dot, as libadwaita fades it; it drew the unchecked circle, white on adwaita, with a dot in the disabled text colour. It opens the connector's new `RoleVariant::SelectedDisabled` scope.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `Slider` built with `.enabled(false)` under a theme that dims by opacity (adwaita and the community presets, `slider.disabled_opacity` 0.5) is faded as one widget over its backdrop, as libadwaita fades the whole scale: the rail over the backdrop, the trailing fill over the rail, the knob and its outline over the backdrop (where a translucent knob overlaps the rail it shows the backdrop's composite), each faded over the backdrop and painted at full opacity. egui faded each shape on its own, so the rail showed through the trailing fill and both through the knob.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `SegmentedControl` added to a disabled `Ui` whose `disabled_alpha` is below 1 (adwaita and the community presets, 0.5) is faded as one widget over its backdrop: the outline's fill over the backdrop, each segment's fill over the outline's, each label over its segment, each faded over the backdrop and painted at the opacity the `Ui` had before egui faded it. egui faded the outline's fill and each segment on its own, so the border colour showed through every segment.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `Switch`, `RadioButton` or `Slider` added to a disabled `Ui` (`ui.add_enabled(false, ..)`) whose `disabled_alpha` is below 1 is faded as one widget over its backdrop by that `Ui`'s `disabled_alpha`, as the `SegmentedControl` is, the one level of fade egui gave the painter taken back (a `Ui` disabled twice, or restyled after it was disabled, keeps the rest); egui faded each shape on its own there, so a thumb, dot or knob showed what lay under it.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `SegmentedControl` built with `.wrap()` rounds the corners its segments share with the outline in the rows as placed: the first row's last segment its top-right corner, the last row's first its bottom-left, and the first segment no longer its bottom-left mid-edge. It rounded by index in the whole list, so the segments at the outline's other two corners kept square corners that poked through the outline's curve. The rows are taken from the last pass, and where they come out otherwise (the first pass, a changed list, a resized `Ui`) the pass is discarded and run again with them known (`Context::request_discard`, egui's mechanism for a layout guess), with a repaint where egui declines to run it again, and neither in a pass that is not shown, as egui's `Grid` does. Its `Parts` record a divider only between two segments of one row (side by side and overlapping in height, so segments of two heights keep theirs), where one at a wrap was an inverted rectangle.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: the `Expander` the crate lays out itself (a theme stating `expander.arrow_side`, `arrow_gap`, `content_indent` or `frame_enabled`: adwaita, kde-breeze, windows-11, macOS) wraps a title too long for its column at the width the padding and the arrow leave it, and its header is at least as wide as a title that cannot wrap that narrow, as egui's own `CollapsingHeader` is at least its text. The title was laid out on one line whatever its width, past the header's right edge and, with a trailing arrow, under the arrow.
- **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a `ProgressBar` with no `desired_width` is the `Ui`'s available width, at least `progress_bar.min_width` (macos-monterey and windows-11 100, kde-breeze 6), egui's own rule with the theme's floor in place of egui's 96: in a 50-point column it was 96 wide everywhere, and is now 100 on macOS and Windows and 50 on KDE.
- **native-theme**, **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-iced**: the READMEs' overlay recipes (the connectors' *Apply user overrides*, the core README's two) state the overlay in `[light.defaults]` and `[dark.defaults]`, as the core crate docs and `SystemTheme::with_overlay`'s example do, and the core README says that an overlay changes only the variants it states; with `[light.defaults]` alone they changed nothing on screen under a dark OS mode.
- **native-theme**: the README's dark-mode recipe is a check, not a poll: `detect::system_is_dark()` keeps its answer until `detect::invalidate_caches()`, so a poll of it never saw the user switch modes; the recipe says so and names `detect::detect_is_dark()`, which reads the OS on every call, for polling.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the README's *Animated spinners* recipe converts the indicator with `icons::animated_frames_to_image_sources_for` in the colour `widgets::Spinner` draws it in (`spinner.fill_color` for Material and Lucide, `defaults.text_color` for the others), so a frame is coloured as its set colours it, a spinning icon is drawn in that one colour at `spinner.diameter`, and it points at `widgets::Spinner`, which does it all; it converted every frame uncoloured, so a symbolic or monochrome indicator drew black on a dark theme.
- **native-theme-iced**: the README's hand-played spinner recipe loads the theme's own indicator (`load_icon_indicator(loaded.icon_set, loaded.icon_theme.as_deref())`) and converts it with `icons::animated_frames_to_svg_handles_for` in the theme's text colour, and colours a turning icon as its set is coloured (a freedesktop icon's `currentColor` filled only where it sets no colour of its own, a Material or Lucide icon tinted); it converted Material's indicator with no colour, so it drew black on a dark theme, as gpui's did.
- **native-theme-iced**: the README's *Text scaling* says the cached accessibility block outlasts only the change the theme watcher does not report, Windows' high contrast; it listed GNOME's text scaling and high contrast, the macOS accessibility flags and Windows' text scale, which the watcher reports.
- **native-theme-gpui**: the README's builder table lists `combo_box.border.line_width` among the fields `geometry::select` and `geometry::combobox` read, the trigger's edge.
- **Showcases**: egui's Basic page draws its disabled checked box in the connector's `RoleVariant::SelectedDisabled` cell, faded as one, with its label in `checkbox.disabled_text_color` as `docs/showcase-elements.toml` states; it chose `Selected` or `Disabled` from the theme itself, patched the border, and under adwaita drew the label in `checkbox.font.color`. Its Selection page's disabled box is unchecked, the `Disabled` cell, and the showcase test requires every `RoleVariant` that `mapping.toml` declares sinks in to be demonstrated, where it named `selected` and `disabled` alone.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_release.sh` (both modes) tests and lints native-theme with its Linux features (`kde`, `portal`, `system-icons`, the two bundled icon sets, `svg-rasterize`, `watch`, `system-fonts`), so the tests of the KDE and GNOME readers, the freedesktop icon loader, the rasteriser, the spinners and the KDE watcher run before every commit, and on a host with Breeze and Adwaita the tests that need them; it ran native-theme's tests with no features and with features only for the `watch::` and `fonts::` modules. A test leg that passes no test fails (a filter or a cfg that matches nothing exits 0), and the full run adds native-theme's ignored stem-index test. CI tests native-theme with `linux,watch` (no gate ran the KDE watcher's tests) and runs native-theme-derive's tests.
- **native-theme-egui**: the install plugin lets a closed viewport's focus-ring entry go: each viewport keeps one entry in the `Context`'s data with that pass's role scopes and `register_focus_shape` outlines, which egui never clears of temporary values, so every immediate or deferred viewport an application opened and closed left its entry behind. At the start of each pass the plugin drops the entry of every viewport whose input it saw and that egui no longer has, as egui drops its own state of it, and at its first root pass it adds the viewports of the entries written before the install (`register_focus_shape` needs no atlas) to those it saw.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_widget_coverage.py` fails (exit 2) on a toolkit in which its discovery rules find no widget, as it already did for `iced_aw` and egui's `Ui` methods: an egui or egui_extras release whose layout the rules no longer match passed as `discovered 0 … missing 0`, since their exception sections are empty and have no stale entry to catch it. A moved `src/widget.rs` (iced_aw) or `src/ui.rs` (egui) is reported the same way instead of ending in a traceback, and `--self-test` runs these guards on synthetic crate roots in `scripts/check_release.sh` and CI.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_release.sh` is hard on every check but the semver checks, in both modes: the gpui connector's checks and the connectors' packaging warned instead of failing, so a full run whose connector did not package still ended "ready for release". `cargo outdated`'s rows count where Latest differs from Project, each dependency once however many members list it (a dependency whose only newer release is semver-incompatible, as every 0.x minor bump is, read as current) and its failure is reported; it said "All dependencies current" for a run that had failed. A missing `cargo-audit` or `cargo-outdated` fails the run with its install command instead of prompting to install it, which ended a run without a terminal with no failure line and no summary; a skipped cross-target check is a warning the summary repeats; the clean-tree and release-tag checks run in a git worktree and count staged and untracked files; jq is required up front and a failing `cargo metadata` or an empty crate list fails; the panic-pattern scan (now `scripts/check_panic_patterns.py`, with a `--self-test`) covers every workspace member's `src/`, `native-theme-derive` and `native-theme-build` among them, counts braces on the code with comments and string, raw-string and char literals blanked (a `{` in a test's TOML fixture left four files' counts inside their test modules, so an item added after those modules would never have been scanned), takes `#[cfg(test)]` to cover the next item only (on a field, a variant, a match arm or a `mod name;` it skipped the whole braced item after it), scans a line that also holds other code or an item it cannot bound, and fails on a file it cannot read or a member without `src/`; and the summary repeats every failure that did not end the run.
- **Development**: `scripts/check_release.sh` (both modes) documents every crate with its private items under `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings"` (`native-theme` with its Linux features, beside its public no-feature run): no gate built `--document-private-items`, so a broken link or a rustdoc warning in a private item's docs passed every check.
- **Development**: the security audit is hard: `scripts/check_release.sh` reported an advisory, or an audit that could not run, as a warning and still ended "ready for release". `.cargo/audit.toml`, which CI's `cargo audit` reads too, denies the informational advisories as well (unmaintained, unsound, yanked) and lists the six the lockfile holds, each with the dependency that holds it and why (instant, paste, rustls-pemfile, rustybuzz and ttf-parser unmaintained, with no patched release: rustls-pemfile only in wasm builds of the GPUI Kit, ttf-parser through gpui-pre, rustybuzz and fontdb 0.23, which iced's and GPUI's cosmic-text require and native-theme keeps to share their copy, though fontdb 0.24 drops it; lru 0.16.4 unsound, fixed only in 0.18.2, which cryoglyph 0.1.0 does not accept); its RUSTSEC-2026-0049 entry, which no longer matched the lock, is gone.
- **Development**: the crates.io workflow uploads only after the whole of CI has passed on the tag's commit: it calls `ci.yml` (`workflow_call`), so MSRV, the macOS and Windows test legs, the strict-panic lints, the showcase-parity checks and the GPUI Fast builds gate the upload, where its own gate job, a subset of CI, did not wait for CI's run on the same tag; `ci.yml` has no tag trigger of its own. A `package` job packages all seven crates in one `cargo package`, from clean (no cache, whose stale build of the same version cargo would reuse), before any upload, so a crate whose tarball does not build stops the release before derive, native-theme and build are on crates.io, not after (v0.5.8's partial upload). A `workflow_dispatch` must name a tag: the release gate refused nothing on a branch ref and compared no version there, so a dispatch from a branch uploaded that branch's crates. The gate runs `scripts/update_compatibility.sh check` beside the provenance check. The workflow's GitHub token, and so CI's inside it, only reads the repository (`permissions: contents: read`). The workflow's shell is `bash -eo pipefail`, and the docs.rs emulation collects its crate list first and fails on an empty one; a failed `cargo metadata` documented no crate and passed. What the removed gate job ran and CI did not is in CI now: clippy on `native-theme-derive`, native-theme's tests with the icon features and `system-fonts` together, and each published crate documented alone beside the workspace run, whose feature unification documents native-theme with the features the connectors turn on.
- **Development**: CI's clippy job runs clippy on native-theme for the macOS and Windows targets with all and with no features, and the egui connector's macOS check, as `scripts/check_release.sh` does: no workflow linted that platform code with its tests and examples, though `check_release.sh` said CI ran the same checks.
- **Development**: the nightly dependency canary's shell is `bash -eo pipefail`, so a failing `cargo update` fails the run instead of passing on `tee`'s status with a summary saying Cargo.lock is already at the newest versions; its Test step runs after a Clippy failure too, and it documents `native-theme-egui-widgets` with its default and with all features.
- **Development**: the GitHub Pages API documentation is built as docs.rs builds it, through `scripts/check_docsrs.py`: each crate with its `[package.metadata.docs.rs]` features and targets on nightly, the macOS and Windows documentation under their target triples; the run fails when a crate's front page is missing for a target it lists. It documented the workspace with default features, so native-theme's readers, watchers, icon and font APIs were missing from it.
- **Showcases**: `--dump-layout` records the chrome at rest wherever the desktop's pointer is: all three keep the window's own pointer off their widgets under it, as under `--capture`, so Widget Info holds its hint, the status bar names no hovered widget and two dumps of one theme are byte for byte the same; they recorded the readout of the element under the pointer, so a comparison of two dumps differed with where the pointer sat. `--pointer X,Y` still holds a pointer of its own. All three follow the OS theme only in an interactive run (`CliArgs::follows_os`): the egui and gpui showcases started their theme watchers under `--capture` and `--dump-layout`, which show the theme the run started with, and the iced showcase followed the OS theme there; none starts its watcher under `--screenshot`, as before.
- **Development**: `scripts/generate_gifs_theme_switching.sh` removes its three frame directories when it fails or is interrupted; it removed them after the last GIF only, so a failed capture left them in the temporary directory.
- **Development**: `scripts/generate_assets_release.sh` checks for ImageMagick 7's `magick` before it starts: without it, the spinner GIFs ended the run in a Python traceback after the CI screenshots run was dispatched.
- **Development**: `scripts/update_compatibility.sh` hashes the egui connector's `tests` directory with its other sources, so a change to `tests/timings.rs` makes egui's compatibility claim stale; the script, the `docs/COMPATIBILITY.toml` header and `scripts/README.md` said egui had no `tests` directory, and the hash left it out. `scripts/README.md` states the gates and families the script runs: iced's `iced_futures` and its `--features iced_aw,watch,native` tests, and the egui widgets crate's four gates and its `Cargo.toml` and `src` in egui's hash.
- **Showcases (iced)**: the Basic and Display pages pad a card inside its border, as gpui and egui do: a side `card.border.padding` states reaches iced plus `card.border.line_width` (`padding_inside_border`), since iced paints a container's border over its padded box; the padding is measured from the border's inner edge (`docs/platform-facts.md` Chapter 2, the border struct). It showed under kde-breeze once its card states a 1px edge and its padding: iced put the content a pixel nearer the edge than gpui and egui.
- **native-theme-iced**: `styles::text_input` and `styles::text_editor` draw a focused field's border as the theme's focus ring where that ring is inset at the field's edge (`defaults.focus_ring_offset` is minus `defaults.focus_ring_width`, libadwaita's `outline-offset: -$width`): `defaults.focus_ring_width` wide in `input.focus_border_color`, or the ring's colour, laid over the field's fill as the platform lays it (iced's quad puts a border in place of the fill rather than over it). iced paints a border inside the widget's bounds, where an inset ring lies; a ring outside the edge or across it (KDE, Windows, macOS), or inset with a gap, is not one iced can draw, and the contract's `UNREACHABLE` list records it. Under adwaita a focused field draws libadwaita's 2px inset ring over its fill, where it recoloured its 1px line.
- **native-theme-gpui**: `geometry::select` and `geometry::combobox` give the trigger's edge `combo_box.border.line_width`, over upstream's `border_1`, as `geometry::input` gives the text field's. Under adwaita, whose drop-down has no line at rest now (see *Changed*), the trigger has no edge, as iced and egui draw none; under macos-monterey, macos-tahoe and ios-13 the trigger's edge goes from upstream's 1px to their stated 0.5px, as their text field's already is.
- **native-theme**: on GNOME the reader gives the focus ring and a focused text input's edge libadwaita's colour for the portal accent, `$focus_border_color`: the accent's standalone colour at 50 %, its Oklab lightness capped at 0.5 in light and raised to 0.85 in dark (`defaults.focus_ring_color`, `input.focus_border_color`; libadwaita 1.10.0 `doc/css-variables.md:137-140`, `adw_rgba_to_standalone`, ported and checked against libadwaita's documented accent table). It wrote the opaque accent into the ring, over adwaita's corrected `#0461be80` / `#81d0ff80`, and left the text input's focus edge blue under any other accent.
- **native-theme**: under high contrast the GNOME reader gives the focus ring and a focused text input's edge libadwaita's `$focus_border_color_hc`, the accent's standalone colour at 80 % (`_drawing.scss:24-26`; `_colors.scss:261`, `:308`), where the portal's `contrast` or gsettings' `high-contrast` asks for it; it wrote the 50 % ring in either case.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's switch track is 12 tall with radius 6 under the 18 handle, as qqc2-desktop-style draws it (`SwitchIndicator.qml:41-48`, `:65-76`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.21); it stated 18 and 9, the indicator's height, so the track was as tall as the handle.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's hovered switch tracks are its rest tracks, #92cae8 / #e6e7e7 (dark #3e7797 / #3e4144): in qqc2-desktop-style hover turns only the outlines `hoverColor` and no fill reads `hovered` (`SwitchIndicator.qml:27-30`, `:49`, `:62`, `:77`; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.21). It stated #2e9ad6 and #a8acab (dark #596068), with no source.
- **native-theme**: kde-breeze's disabled switch tracks are qqc2-desktop-style's, its tracks computed from the Disabled colour group: on #e6e7e8, off #e9e9e9 (dark #26292c, #2e3135; `docs/platform-facts.md` §2.21). It stated the accent and the border colour at half alpha, #3daee980 and #bcc0bf80 (dark #4d545b80), with no source.
- **native-theme**: the KDE reader derives the switch's outlines from the colour scheme as qqc2-desktop-style draws them: the off track's and the thumb's, the Button set's background moved towards its text by `[KDE] frameContrast` (Kirigami's `linearInterpolation`, in Qt's arithmetic), and the on track's, the selection colour (`SwitchIndicator.qml:27-30`, `:50`, `:78`); and the thumb's fill, the Button set's background (`:29`), the hovered tracks as the rest tracks, and `button.hover_text_color`, the Button set's text (Breeze letters a non-flat button in `ButtonText` in every state, `breezestyle.cpp:5350-5362`). They kept kde-breeze's Breeze values under another colour scheme or accent. The disabled outlines come from the Disabled colour group, which the reader does not compute; they stay kde-breeze's.
- **native-theme**: windows-11 states no `menu.row_height`: its 23 px took the 14 px font size for the text's line box. WinUI 3's `MenuFlyoutItem` has no minimum height; it is its padding, 4 + 5 when opened by mouse, pen or keyboard and 8 + 9 otherwise, round the line spacing of Segoe UI Variable at 14 px, which no source states (`docs/platform-facts.md` §2.6). A connector now sizes a windows-11 menu row to its font and stated padding, as it does wherever a theme states no row height, where it took the stated 23 px, which the line box and the padding do not fit in.
- **native-theme-gpui**, **native-theme-iced**, **native-theme-egui-widgets**: a switch thumb taller than its track overhangs it above and below and sits flush with the track's ends, as qqc2-desktop-style's handle does (`SwitchIndicator.qml:68`). gpui's and egui's thumb overhung the ends too, and iced's `switch`, a button holding the thumb, squeezed it into the track; it is a widget of its own now, as tall as the thumb, all of which, the overhang included, is hovered and pressed, and its thumb is round, as gpui's and egui's are, where it took the track's radius.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the number field's Widget Info describes the field the showcase draws round gpui-base's spin button: its fill `input.background_color`, its edge `input.border.color`, `input.hover_border_color` under the pointer and `input.focus_border_color` while focused, its value padded by `geometry::input`, and its ChevronUp and ChevronDown step buttons. It described gpui-component's `NumberInput`, which is not drawn there: upstream's `input_background` as the fill (on kde-breeze light the window's `#eff0f1`, where the field is the view's `#ffffff`), no padding from `geometry::input`, and Minus and Plus buttons.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the Basic page's table rows, header and frame each have a Widget Info of what the showcase draws: the selected row in `list.selection_background` and `list.selection_text_color`, the others in `list.item_font`'s colour and, under the pointer, `list.hover_background` and `list.hover_text_color`, the header in `list.header_background` and `list.header_font`'s colour, the grid lines in `list.grid_color`, the frame `list.border` round `list.background_color`. They described the declarative `Table` the page shows without a native theme: the `table` fill and `foreground` text on every row, the selected one included, and on every row after the first a `table_row_border` top edge, which is not drawn.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: `--screenshot` on Linux exits with 1 before a window opens, saying that it captures on macOS and Windows only and which script captures the window on Linux; it printed a note and kept the window open until it was closed, then exited 0, with no file written. A main window that fails to open exits with 1, where it exited 0. On macOS and Windows `--dump-layout F --screenshot P` writes both files and quits once the later of the two is written: the showcase quit once F was written, about a second in, before P's capture. A file that fails to be written exits with 1, naming its flag and path, and so does a layout dump whose layout has not settled after 20 s, the time the capture scripts wait for it, where the run would otherwise stay open. Under `--capture` the window stays open after either file, for the tool that captures it, as it did after the dump alone.
- **Showcases (iced)**: `--screenshot` on Linux exits with 1 before the window opens, saying that it captures on macOS and Windows only and that `scripts/generate_screenshots_iced.sh` captures the window on Linux, as the gpui and egui showcases do: it wrote iced's own capture of the window's content, without the title bar and frame the other platforms' captures and the capture scripts' show.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the Widget Info of a toolbar button, the page tabs' menu button and the Basic page's spinner names the installed theme's icon theme under `defaults.icon_theme` (kde-breeze's `breeze`, or `breeze-dark` in dark mode), as the iced and egui showcases name it; it said `not stated` under every theme. Its rows, their values and its Copy text are those of the crate the three showcases share (`tools/showcase-support`).
- **Showcases (gpui)**: a colour the showcase prints, in Widget Info, the inspector and the Theme Map, prints through native-theme's `Rgba`, as a theme colour does, so one whose alpha rounds to 255 (from about 0.998 up, which only a computed colour reaches) prints as `#rrggbb`, and the Theme Map notes it translucent no more; it printed `#rrggbbff`, with the note.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the status bar's font item and the inspector's Theme tab name the family gpui draws, the connector's `font_family` of the stated one: a family the system lacks reads, for windows-11 on one Linux system, "Segoe UI (not on this system; drawn in the platform's substitute, Adwaita Sans)", and the Theme tab's `mono_font_family` row is named the same way. Both named the stated family alone, the Theme tab noting only macOS's `.SystemUIFont`.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the Inputs page's Textarea is drawn over the input surface the Basic page's text area takes, framed by `text_area.border.color` and under the pointer by `input.hover_border_color` (kde-breeze: `#3daee9`), where its edge stayed upstream's `input` token in both states. The Layout page's Form fields are the Inputs page's refined fields: filled with `input.background_color`, framed by `input.border.color` and under the pointer by `input.hover_border_color`, where they kept upstream's fill (on kde-breeze light the window's `#eff0f1`, where a field is the view's `#ffffff`) and had no hover edge. Widget Info follows: a refined field's and a text area's edge and hovered edge are the surface's, with its line, and none is claimed where the theme strokes no line (adwaita), where the info claimed upstream's `input` token; the Basic page's text area routes its edge, hovered edge and fill through the surface, where it said its edge was the `input` token and its hover edge not applied; and a Select under a native theme claims no upstream `foreground` trigger text, which `geometry::select`'s `combo_box.font` colour replaces.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the chrome menus' items and titles take `menu.font` as `geometry::menu_item` sets it, its style included, and an item is as tall as `geometry::menu_item` makes a menu item, `menu.row_height` at a text scale of 1 and at least that above it, as the Overlays page's menu rows already were: an item grew past a stated `menu.row_height` to its font's line and padding, and an italic menu font was drawn upright. Where the theme states neither a row height nor a vertical padding, an item is upstream's 26 px at a text scale of 1 and at least that above it, so a scaled font grows it, where it stayed 26 px.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the Overlays page's menu rows, which the application draws itself, are painted as the showcase's own menus are: the frame in `menu.background_color`, edged by `popover.border`, the rows in `menu.font.color`, rounded by `menu.border.corner_radius` and hovered in `menu.hover_background` and `menu.hover_text_color`, where the frame took the popover's fill and text inside the showcase's demonstration frame (adwaita light: `#fafafb`, where it was the popover's `#ffffff`). Their Widget Info claims those leaves, and none of the frame's edge where `popover.border.line_width` is 0, where it claimed the popover and accent tokens.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: Widget Info claims the colour of an edge, a frame or an outline only where the widget draws that line, at the width the theme states for it, and otherwise says `none: <widget>.border.line_width is 0`: the refined Buttons claim no edge at rest, hovered or pressed under adwaita and material-3, whose `button.border.line_width` is 0, and the menus no `menu edge` under material-3, whose `popover.border.line_width` is 0; the theme-drawn ProgressBar claims no frame where `progress_bar.border.line_width` is 0 (adwaita, windows-11, material-3); a GroupBox and a card claim `card.border.color` where `card.border.line_width` is above 0 (windows-11, kde-breeze) and elsewhere say that no edge is drawn, an Outline box's own border giving way to the 0 width. They claimed those edges in every case, and a GroupBox's and a card's in none while saying it was drawn on every variant. A card's Widget Info claims no content text, its text being a Label in `foreground` (it claimed the group box's colour), a refined Button's names `button.border.color` at rest, the Basic page's icon buttons, which no `geometry::button` refines, no longer say it edges them, a disabled refined field claims the edge it is drawn with, faded by `input.disabled_opacity`, and the ProgressBar's frame line no longer points at a question only an archived spec holds. A source gate, `every_edge_colour_is_claimed_through_edge_claim`, holds every claim of a theme's line colour to the width the line is drawn at.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the Basic page's expander arrows and the number fields' step buttons (Basic and Inputs pages), which the showcase draws itself, show the chosen icon theme's icons for ChevronRight before a title or ChevronDown after it and for ChevronUp and ChevronDown on the steps, and none where that icon theme has none, where they were gpui-component's own Lucide chevrons under every icon theme, two icon sets on one page. The steps' icons are `defaults.icon_sizes.small` across, where they were gpui-component's Small icon size. Their Widget Info names the chosen icon theme's icon or says none is drawn, where it called them gpui-component's own.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the theme-drawn expander's Widget Info (Basic page) says what frames it and how it is padded: no frame colour where `expander.frame_enabled` is false (kde-breeze), where it claimed `expander.border.color` as drawn; each item framed on its own by `expander.border.color` at `expander.border.line_width`, `layout.widget_gap` apart, where `frame_enabled` is true (adwaita, windows-11), where it described one frame round the whole and between the items; and each title row's padding side by side from `expander.border.padding` (adwaita 14 across and 8 above and below, windows-11 16 on the left and 0 elsewhere), upstream's `px_3` on an unstated left or right and none on an unstated top or bottom, with the body's own padding only where no `layout.widget_gap` parts it from its title (`frame_enabled` unstated, or no `widget_gap` stated), where it called the padding unstated on every preset.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: without a native theme, the Basic page's expanders' Widget Info is the Layout page's Accordion's, with its radius, padding, chevron and animation lines, and no longer says the connector gives its fill the window's background; the Accordion's says its items get no hover style, as the expanders' did.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: a checkbox, radio, switch or toggle activated from the keyboard (Tab to it, then Space or Enter) is drawn in its new state at once: the Inputs page's two checkboxes, its radio group and its switch, the Buttons page's Bold and Italic toggles and the Basic page's radio group changed their state and asked for no new frame, so they stayed drawn as before until something else redrew the window. A mouse click was not affected, since the click itself redraws.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under `default` the icons are the shown mode's own: the colour-mode switch to the mode the OS is not in loads that variant's icon theme (on KDE with the OS dark and the showcase light, `breeze`), where it loaded the OS mode's (`breeze-dark`). Where the OS theme cannot be read, the system icon set's icons (freedesktop, SF Symbols, Segoe Fluent) keep their own colours, where every recoloured one drew black, on gpui-component's dark theme too, as the loaders took the placeholder font's transparent black as their colour.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the labels the showcase draws in a widget's font, the side panel's labels and the Basic page's segments, expander titles and table cells, take that font's family and style as the connector's builders do (`geometry`'s `with_text`), where they were drawn in the window's family and upright whatever the theme stated; no bundled preset states another family or a slanted style for these fonts, so a theme file shows it. Their Widget Info, and the font lines of the chrome menus and the theme-drawn Checkbox and TabBar, name the family drawn (a family the system lacks with the substitute drawn in its place), the size the text-scaling factor scales, the weight and the style, where they printed the stated size as the size drawn, which it is not at a text scale other than 1, the Checkbox and TabBar lines a substituted family by its stated name alone and the menus' none, and the routes called the family the window's.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: under a native theme the Typography page's monospace sample is drawn in `defaults.mono_font`: its size (`Theme::mono_font_size`), weight and line box, one `defaults.line_height` line, as the Basic page's Monospace line, and its style, where it took the mono family alone at the window's size and gpui's own line height (the golden ratio, so the sample is shorter now on every bundled preset); its Widget Info's size line names `mono_font_size`, where it said that size did not reach the sample.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the page tab row's Widget Info names `geometry::icon_size_small` while its menu Button shows the chosen icon set's ChevronDown, which that builder sizes, as every other small icon of the showcase is sized and named; the icon was sized from `defaults.icon_sizes.small` read directly, at the same size, and the info named no builder for it.
- **Showcases (gpui)**: the inspector's Copy button is lettered in `button.hover_text_color` under the pointer and in `button.active_text_color` while pressed, as its Widget Info says; it kept `sidebar.font.color` in both.

- **Showcases (gpui)**: a status bar too narrow for its environment text, in a narrow window or with a font family the system lacks named with its substitute, cuts the text short with an ellipsis in the room the side-panel toggle and the shown title leave it, and its Widget Info gives the whole text; the text ran on under the middle region and the shown title, which upstream's right region cut short as it shrank with the left one.

The flat icon buttons (the toolbar's, the Basic page's Copy, Paste and Delete, the page tabs' menu and the side-panel toggle) say their hovered and pressed text colours are not drawn where the set's icon stands in for the label, its colour fixed as it loads, and an OS icon theme's icon keeps its own colours; the side-panel toggle, selected while the panel shows, names the selected fill and colour it draws in every status and says its rest and hover colours are not drawn. - **Showcases (iced)**: under the OS theme (`default`) each colour mode draws its icons from its own variant's icon theme, `breeze-dark` with Mode Dark on a light KDE Plasma desktop (or `--variant dark`), as the `kde-breeze` preset does, where it took the OS mode's (`breeze`). A colour-mode switch there reads nothing from the OS, picking the other variant of the read the showcase keeps from startup, Reload System Theme and the theme watcher, where every switch read the whole OS theme again; under `--features watch` the watcher follows the OS's changes in every colour mode, as the gpui showcase's does, so a return to System shows the OS theme as it is then, in its mode, where it followed them only while the colour mode was System. A preference set in the Preferences dialog (Text scale, Reduce motion, High contrast, Reduce transparency) stays what the user set across colour-mode and theme switches and Reload System Theme, and one not set follows the OS, where under `default` every such install put the OS's values back and the dialog jumped back to them. The OS's request for reduced motion keeps the animations stopped whatever the Reduce motion switch shows, as before, and its row now says so; the OS's setting is read with the theme, where it was read again at each icon reload. The iced theme's light/dark flag (`Extended::is_dark`, which the window's title bar follows) is the mode installed on every path, where only the startup fallback set it and every other install left it to iced's lightness test of the background. Where the OS theme fails to read at startup the showcase starts in the platform's own preset, the one the OS theme builds on (`native_theme::pipeline::platform_preset_name`, as the egui showcase does: `kde-breeze` on KDE, `adwaita` on another Linux desktop, the running version's generation on macOS, `windows-11` on Windows), with the OS's accessibility preferences, where it started in adwaita on every platform, which the theme picker does not offer off Linux, with the default preferences. A Reload System Theme whose read fails installs a preset again, in the OS's mode as now detected, where it left it in the mode before.

Widget Info is built once per hover and rebuilt after each change the showcase handles (a theme, a preference, a tab or a drag among them), never on a timer's tick or a redraw, and the resolved theme it reads is serialised once per install, where each time the showcase rebuilt its view, twenty times a second on the Basic page while its spinner animates, every hoverable widget's text was built for its hover message and a listed element's info serialised the whole theme and built its rows and Copy text again.
